# DUELL: specification (draft 1, 2026-10-10)

Bitimbral polyphonic synth firmware for the M-VAVE FM-1. Fork of Felucca (GPL-3.0, so DUELL is GPL-3.0). Developed and auditioned in the browser emulator first; flashed to the device only when every feature is done.

Items marked **[verify]** are assumptions about Felucca's code that must be checked before building on them. Items marked **[decide]** need an answer from the owner.

---

## 1. Design rules (these override everything below)

### 1.1 The label law
Every control does what its printed label says, on every page, in every mode.

- A label is never reused for something else. A control that has no honest job in DUELL does nothing and the screen says so.
- Felucca breaks this in one place that DUELL fixes: SELECT sets the tempo. DUELL keeps two Felucca habits by the owner's choice: ALGORITHM selects the track (here: the part), and OCT-/OCT+ mean "back / do it" in dialogs and menus.
- Holding a button may show a temporary version of the same function (for example hold SAVE = SAVE OVER). A hold never changes the meaning of the label. (The FX button's hold layer of performance effects was removed in 0.2.2, section 5c.6; since 0.2.5 holding FX turns on PERFORM mode, section 5e.)
- No hidden state changes what a control does. The meaning of KNOB 1-4 is always on screen, in the part's colour.

### 1.1a Navigation principle (owner, 2026-10-10)
**SELECT selects, the knobs adjust.** One method of navigation, everywhere:
- SELECT turns through whatever there is to select: the sub-pages of a screen, the rows of a list, the rows of the menu **continuously across its tabs** (after the last row of a tab it goes on to the first row of the next tab; it stops at the very ends of the menu and never jumps back to the top).
- KNOB 1-4 adjust the values on the screen shown. In the menu, any of the four knobs adjusts the selected row (a knob with no dedicated job does the same as the others). **Where the screen marks a knob with a job, it keeps that job**: on the SAVE lists KNOB 1 is marked as the row picker and stays so, and SELECT turns the SAVE sub-pages there.
- ALGORITHM and PRESETS never navigate pages, tabs or rows. They always do their own job (ALGORITHM: the part; PRESETS: the sound). The one exception the owner accepted: in PERFORM mode (and on the MORPH page) ALGORITHM is PART 1 / BOTH / PART 2, and in PERFORM SELECT (which has no page to turn to there) is BALANCE, and the screen says so (section 5e).
- OCT-/OCT+ keep their octave job, and may step a value or answer a dialog as in Felucca, but no screen advertises them as the way to navigate or adjust. On-screen hints name SELECT and the knobs.

### 1.2 Fixed panel map (hardware, from Felucca's control chart)

| Control | DUELL function (label = job) |
| --- | --- |
| MASTER | Output volume. Always. |
| SELECT (knob) | Turn left / right: previous / next sub-page of the screen shown, on every screen, always. In lists and menus it moves the row. Never tempo. The one exception: in PERFORM mode (one page) it is BALANCE (section 5e). |
| PRESETS | The selected part's patch (a sound). On HOME and on SAVE > PATCHES. In PERFORM mode too: the sound of the part(s) ALGORITHM chose (section 5e). |
| ALGORITHM | Selects the part, A or B (Felucca's track select; kept by the owner's choice). Works on every page. **On the MORPH page (HOME 1/4; 1/5 up to 0.2.13) and in PERFORM mode: PART 1 / BOTH / PART 2** (0.2.9, owner 2026-10-14; left to right, a detent a step, clamped, no wrap): the morph's target (section 3.4a, 5e). Every other page: PART 1 / PART 2 only, as before. |
| KNOB 1-4 | The four columns of the page shown. |
| FX | Effects page (per-part insert + global sends). Again = next sub-page (as Felucca, for now). **Held 0.7 s: PERFORM mode on** (and off again); a white key pressed with FX held turns it on at once and is PERFORM's key, so keys pressed with FX held are no longer notes; in PERFORM a tap of FX goes back (section 5e, since 0.2.5). **Pages (0.2.21, owner): only the part's own, in the order the sound flows: FX (SAT and the part's CHO / DLY / REV sends), INSERT 1/2, INSERT 2/2, EQ 1/2, EQ 2/2, DUCK 1/2, DUCK 2/2, and as the LAST page **SEND FX** (0.2.22, owner; it was GLOBAL FX): the SELECTED part's view of the global effects, with only that part's badge lit, showing each effect's main settings and NO send levels (the sends are on the FX page, one slide away): K1 **CHORUS** (the label only: no rate or send is listed), K2 **DELAY** (the mode, e.g. RAND, and under it the time, 1/8), K3 **REVERB** (the type, e.g. PLATE, and under it the size, 71 %), no K4 card. The second line is the card's one small line (50 px). **The cards are always drawn** (also at a send of 0 or with MODULES SENDS OFF): then the value is dim and the small line says **INACTIVE**. The delay shows its mode in DUEL too since 0.2.22 (`params.c dly_modes_here`, the one predicate). A twist of K1, K2 or K3 (either way) jumps to GLO's CHORUS, DELAY 1/2 or REVERB page, also when the card is inactive: an explicit page change only, no value changes, no sticky or snapping knob (the rest of that twist is swallowed for 0.3 s, as after a reset, so it does not move the new page's first card). The global effects' own pages (CHORUS, DELAY, REVERB, SHIMMER, BASS MONO) are on GLO.** |
| SCL | Two pages. **SCALE** (page 1, GLOBAL: the whole instrument's, not a part's): K1 SCALE, K2 ROOT, K3 OFF-SCALE (SNAP / PLAY); the header lights both part badges and the page is titled GLOBAL SCALE. **CHORD** (page 2, the selected part's): K1 CHRD, K2 VOIC, K3 TRN; its badge alone is lit. No page mixes global and per-part controls. Held, SCL is the quick layer of the global three (a key sets the ROOT, K1 SCALE, K3 OFF-SCALE; OCT- puts them back). The button is printed SEL on the hardware; the spec calls it SCL as Felucca does. |
| ENV | Envelope pages. **Held: QUICK ENV** (0.2.9; section "Quick edit layers" below): K1 AMP ATK, K2 AMP REL, K3 FILTER ENV's FLT depth, K4 FILTER ENV's DEC. |
| LFO | LFO pages and modulation matrix. **Held: QUICK LFO** (0.2.9): K1 LFO 1 RATE (its SYNC while LFO 1 is synced), K2 LFO 1's FLT depth, K3 LFO 2 RATE (SYNC2 while synced), K4 LFO 2's FLT depth. (Felucca's MOMENTARY, LFO held + a knob = a temporary change, is gone with it.) |
| EDIT | **First sub-page: ENGINE** (KNOB 1 ENGINE, KNOBS 2..4 the engine's first three controls; T0227, owner: no sound list, no FAV, no INIT here: patches are PATCHES' and SAVE's, INIT SOUND is SAVE > TOOLS K2), then EDIT 1 (the engine's next four) and EDIT 2 (its eighth, only on an engine that has one). An engine change changes only the engine and its own eight controls; the patch name and slot shown stay. Engine selection is never hidden behind a hold: Felucca's hold-EDIT engine keys are gone (decided 2026-10-10). **Held: QUICK MULTI** (0.2.9): K1 CUT, K2 RES, K3 MODE of the multimode filter (on every engine since 0.2.14), K4 AMP REL; **the keys play notes** (0.2.11, owner: an F played in the layer used to INIT the sound; INIT is SAVE > TOOLS, K2 (T0227; it was EDIT > ENGINE, K4); the DRUM lane mutes that sat on the black keys are gone from here) (up to 0.2.8 its knobs were ENGINE, SOUND, FAV: they are on the ENGINE page). |
| GLO | Global (the instrument's, not a part's: both badges lit). A tap opens **MIXER** (0.2.25 layout round, owner: GLO's first page again: K1 LEVEL 1, K2 PAN 1, K3 LEVEL 2, K4 PAN 2, PAN not drawn in DUEL; both parts' cells read MUTE, lit in the part's colour while muted; the level meters; drawn as the hold layer below, the same layout; graph `GR_GLO`, scope `SC_GMIX`; HOME's MIXER page is gone), tapped again / SELECT, in this order: **TEMPO** (K1 CLK = clock source AUTO / INT / USB / TRS, default AUTO since 0.2.14, K2 TEMPO, K3 TAP, K4 SWG), then the global effects (0.2.21, owner: they were FX's pages): **CHORUS** (CRT, CDP), **DELAY 1/2** (MODE, TIME, FDBK, COLR), **DELAY 2/2** (SPREAD, MRATE, MOD (CHANCE in RANDOM / OCT UP), MIX), **REVERB** (TYPE, SIZE, DAMP, LOCUT), **SHIMMER** (only while the reverb TYPE is SHIM; 0.2.19; K1 PITCH, K2 DRIFT), then the master stages in the order of the chain: **STEREO MASTER** (0.2.25 layout round: K1 BASS MONO, K2 WIDTH; it replaced the pages WIDTH and MASTER; sections 5h, 5l), **COMPRESSOR** (0.2.21 as COMP; 0.2.25 layout round: K1 RATIO, OFF its lowest value, K2 THRESH, K3 ATTACK, K4 RELEASE; section 5j), then **MODULES** (0.2.14, owner: K1 ARP, K2 SEQ, K3 SENDS, K4 PART FX, each ON or OFF, the device's switches; section 5f), then the MIDI pages together: **SIDECHAIN** (0.2.22: the ducker's MIDI setup, a device setting: K1 PART 1 CH, K2 PART 1 NOTE, K3 PART 2 CH, K4 PART 2 NOTE (two-line labels); section 4.x), **MIDI** (PART 1 CH, PART 2 CH, MIDI IN), **MIDI LEARN** (LEARN, CLEAR) (**all the MIDI settings, and only here; MENU has none**), and round to MIXER: twelve pages, thirteen while the reverb TYPE is SHIM. **Held: QUICK GLO** (0.2.11, owner): the MIXER's cards and cells and the level meters as a quick layer over the page you are on (PERFORM too), the keys keep playing, let go and the page is back; no double-tap lock. The CPU test is no GLO page (0.2.2): it was **MENU > SYSTEM > TEST** (section 6.1), which is now in developer builds only (`DUELL_TEST_MODE`). (Up to 0.2.20 GLO held + PLAY restarted from the top on the main GLO page; the MIXER page of 0.2.25 does not bring that back: PLAY is PLAY.) |
| HOME | Home screen. Hold = menu. |
| SAVE | Everything that is saved: patches, patterns, multis. Tap opens the page that fits (below); hold = SAVE OVER the patch (PATTERNS / MULTIS: the slot picked). |
| ARP | Arpeggiator of the selected part (as Felucca's), plus ARP TARGET (section 2.3). **Held: QUICK ARP** (0.2.9): K1 MODE, K2 RATE, K3 GATE, K4 OCT. |
| SEQ | Opens the step pattern of the arp's SEQUENCER mode: 16 steps, each with gate, accent and a note offset (scale degrees of the global scale, or semitones when it is CHROMATIC) (section 2.3). |
| PLAY/STOP | Start/stop the arp and tempo-synced modulation; follows MIDI start/stop. **Pressed twice (second press within 300 ms) or held 0.6 s: STOP ALL** (owner, 2026-10-12): the transport stopped and reset, both parts' arps and patterns off with their latches / holds cleared, every held and releasing voice faded out within about 3 ms, **STOP ALL silences everything: the effect tails are flushed** (0.2.14, owner: the delay, chorus and reverb lines fade out in about 25 ms, are cleared, and the sends fade back in over the empty lines in about 22 ms, so the next notes have the normal effects; the machinery is the MODULES SENDS OFF fade, `fx.c snd_panic`; a double press on an already stopped unit flushes too, a user's SENDS OFF stays OFF; a single STOP is unchanged, the tails run on), the keys' and MIDI's held-note state cleared, All Sound Off (CC120) and All Notes Off (CC123) sent on both parts' channels (DUELL sends no MIDI clock, so there is no transport stop to send), and "STOP ALL" shown for about 0.8 s. Running, a press stops at once. Stopped, a press starts nothing: a short press (under 0.6 s) starts on its release, and a hold of 0.6 s is STOP ALL at once with nothing started (the release does nothing more). In a double press the first tap starts on its release, so a short blip of start can come before the second press's STOP ALL. Code: `ui_input.c play_stop_all / play_hold`, `seq.c stop_all` (transport_req 4); help: HOME's hint `[PLAY]x2: STOP ALL`; test: midi_control_test `stop_all_test`, `stoptail_test` (STOP ALL flush). **The LED (DUELL 0.2.10, owner 2026-10-14):** the button has two LEDs, each only on or off (no dimming): **green while the transport is stopped** (a press plays) and **red while it plays, and on each beat of the count-in** (a press stops); in every LEDS mode, MAX too. The red one is the button's own key-matrix LED, the green one is the extra LED found at column 8 (`ui_input.c LED_PLAY_GREEN`). |
| REC | Arms recording (notes and knob motion) on the selected part. **On every page except the sequencer's it is the HOLD switch instead (0.2.9, owner):** a tap toggles the HOLD of every part the keys play (0.2.11: both parts in LAYER, the selected part in DUEL and STEREO), each by its own arp state (arp on, MODE not OFF: OFF and ARP, from ALL to OFF; arp off: OFF and ALL), with one direction for all: if any of those parts has HOLD on, all go OFF, else all go on. The header says HOLD ON or HOLD OFF, and the REC light shows the HOLD state there (lit fully when any of those parts has HOLD on, the idle level of the LEDS mode when off). "Sequencer" means the sequencer's own pages and layer: SEQ's STEP and AUTOMATION pages and the SEQ layer; there REC arms / disarms recording as always. A recording already armed stays armed on the other pages and is only armed or disarmed on the sequencer pages (`ui_input.c rec_is_hold`); on the sequencer pages the REC light shows the armed state. **The armed mark (0.2.10, owner 2026-10-14):** there is no REC mark in the header. While any part is armed, every page shows a red dot and "REC" in the footer's second row, just left of the page's number (the patch name gives way to it): a filled dot when the selected part is armed, an outlined dot when only the other part is (`ui_draw.c foot_rec_mark`). |
| OCT-/OCT+ | Octave down / up of the keys, **moved when the button is let go** (0.2.9; a tap still moves it), both together = octave reset to 0; in action pages, dialogs and menus: back / do it (unchanged). **OCT- held + a knob turned = that knob's card goes to its default** (see "OCT- + knob: RESET" below). |
| Keys | Play the selected part (both parts in LAYER). |

**OCT- + knob: RESET (0.2.9, owner-approved).** Holding OCT- and turning a knob sets that knob's card to its default, on normal pages and in the QUICK EDIT layers (0.2.10, below). Normal turning (OCT- not held) is unchanged; nothing snaps or sticks. One reset per gesture: the header says `RESET <CARD>` once, the turn itself is not applied, and the knob's further detents are ignored until it has been still for about 300 ms (also after OCT- is let go), so nothing creeps afterwards. Motion capture and MIDI LEARN see an ordinary move to the default. Ids and formats are unchanged (`ui_input.c knob_reset`, `oct_rel`).
- **Resets:** every plain parameter card (the parameter's default in the table, HOME's four cards and MIXER-style LEVELS included, TEMPO's TEMPO / SWG / CLK, DUCK, the FX and EDIT pages); GLO > MODULES' four switches to ON (0.2.14, `RESET ARP` .. `RESET PART FX`); **0.2.24 (owner: on AMP ENV the reset "did not work"): under a LIVE MORPH (End B rolled, the fader off A) the card shows live + (blend - Original), so the reset lands the SOUNDING value on the default by moving the stored one (`reset_desc`; `tests/reset_walk_test.c` walks every page and knob); PERFORM K1 MORPH to A (0), K2 SPACE 0, K3 KILL 0, K4 DEPTH 100; MORPH page K1 (fader to A) and K4 AMOUNT (its default); MIXER LEVEL (default), PAN (centre), K3 SPACE (0, the header says `RESET SPACE`; 0.2.10: the MIXER's third card is SPACE); GLO K3 / K4 LEVEL 1 / 2 (default); MODE K2 BAL (centre); SCALE K1 SCALE, K2 ROOT (their defaults).
- **Does not reset (the turn goes on as ever):** switches and choosers with no default value (MUTE, ENGINE / SOUND / FAV / INIT, PATCHES, TAP, MODE K1, XFADE, SPLIT, OFF-SCALE, MORPH SET, ROLL / UNDO, MIDI channels and LEARN, the MOD slot picker, FM6 operator pages, STEP / AUTOMATION). A card greyed by a selection does nothing (PAN and SPREAD in DUEL mode, TEMPO with an external clock, a DIA chord with no scale).
- **No reset where OCT- has another job:** dialogs, NAME, the menu, action pages (PATTERNS, MULTIS, TOOLS, SAVE / LOAD pages, AUTOMATION), the SET layers other than QUICK MULTI (GLO, QUICK SCALE, QUICK SEQ, QUICK REC: OCT- puts back), MIDI LEARN (OCT- clears). OCT-/OCT+ keep their back / do it meanings there.
- **In the QUICK EDIT layers (0.2.10, owner):** with EDIT, LFO, ENV or ARP held (QUICK MULTI, QUICK LFO, QUICK ENV, QUICK ARP), OCT- held + a knob resets that layer card the same way (`ui_layer.c layer_quick_reset`). OCT- used so is a modifier: its release neither puts back (QUICK MULTI) nor moves the octave (QUICK LFO, ENV, ARP). A plain OCT- (pressed and let go with no knob turned) keeps its meaning: QUICK MULTI puts its four back, the others move the octave. Test: `reset_test.c`.
- **Octave on release:** OCT- / OCT+ move the octave when let go; both together still reset it to 0 (at the second press); a hold used as a modifier (a knob was turned) moves no octave on release.

**Quick edit layers (0.2.9, owner-approved).** Holding EDIT, LFO, ENV, ARP or GLO past the HOLD time (MENU > CONTROL > BUTTON HOLD, 0.3 .. 0.6 s; named HOLD up to 0.2.13, the same threshold as every layer) shows a layer over the page: four performance knobs for the **selected part** (QUICK GLO: one card per part, K1 and K3 part 1, K2 and K4 part 2). A tap still opens the page when let go, exactly as before (two quick taps: the next sub-page; no lock on LFO, ENV, ARP). A knob turned with the button held opens the layer at once and edits; letting go then opens no page.

| Hold | Header | K1 | K2 | K3 | K4 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EDIT | QUICK MULTI | CUT | RES | MODE | AMP REL |
| LFO | QUICK LFO | LFO 1 RATE (SYNC when synced) | LFO 1 FLT depth | LFO 2 RATE (SYNC2 when synced) | LFO 2 FLT depth |
| ENV | QUICK ENV | AMP ATK | AMP REL | FILTER ENV FLT depth | FILTER ENV DEC |
| ARP | QUICK ARP | MODE | RATE | GATE | OCT |
| GLO | QUICK GLO (0.2.11; 0.2.24 layout) | LEVEL 1 | PAN 1 | LEVEL 2 | PAN 2 |

- **The cards are the parameters' own:** the same ids, names and values as on their pages, and the same hide rule (a card is not shown only where a selection makes it inapplicable, never for a zero), recorded as motion and picked by MIDI LEARN like any knob move. No new ids: the web editor, the protocol, morph and saved data are unchanged.
- **QUICK MULTI is the multimode filter on every engine (0.2.14, owner):** K1 CUT, K2 RES, K3 MODE (ids 45, 46, 47), K4 AMP REL (`P_REL`), one mapping for all ten engines (ANALOG, TRIO and NOISE too: the multimode filter runs in series after their own, section 2.5). SLOPE stays on the MULTI FILTER page. The three engines' own filters stay on their own EDIT pages (VCF, TRIO FILTER, NOISE FILT). Up to 0.2.13 QUICK MULTI showed the engine's own filter there (ANALOG CUT RES TYPE, TRIO CUT RES MODE, NOISE FREQ RES): turning TYPE on ANALOG switched the VCF between LP and BP, not the multimode filter the owner expected. Note: the routes, CC74 and CC71 still drive those three engines' own filters (section 2.5), so on them QUICK MULTI's CUT and CC74 are two different cutoffs. The panel names the groups (FILTER / AMP, LFO 1 / LFO 2, AMP ENV / FILTER ENV, ARP), so the two FLT cards are told apart.
- **The keys:** in every quick layer (MULTI FILTER, LFO, ENV, ARP, GLO) they play notes as on any page (a key pressed with the button is no layer key and no combo: a quick tap stays a tap). 0.2.11 (owner): QUICK MULTI had EDIT's keys (INIT on F3, the DRUM lane mutes on the black keys): playing an F erased the sound; both are gone, nothing in a quick layer acts on a key. (In PERFORM the white keys are PERFORM's, as without a layer.)
- **The header in a quick layer (0.2.11, owner):** QUICK MULTI, LFO, ENV, ARP (and QUICK SEQ, REC) light the badge of the selected part alone (`song.sel`), never both, even with ALGORITHM at BOTH on PERFORM or MORPH: these layers edit one part, and a header that shows both must edit both. QUICK SCALE (global) and QUICK GLO (per-part cards) light both. `ui_draw.c badge_lit`.
- **QUICK GLO (0.2.11; 0.2.24, owner: PAN could not be set from the panel):** the GLO main page as a layer: **K1 LEVEL 1, K2 PAN 1, K3 LEVEL 2, K4 PAN 2** (PAN not drawn in DUEL), the level meters; **the mutes are on the keys: with GLO held OCT- mutes / unmutes part 1 and OCT+ part 2** (on the press, `layer_glo_oct`; the key's release moves no octave, the hold becomes a combo so GLO's release is no tap; the part cells read MUTE, lit in the part's colour while muted (0.2.25 layout round; they said LIVE / MUTED), with the key under them (on the MIXER page: GLO + the key); up to 0.2.23 K1 / K2 were MUTE 1 / 2 and K3 / K4 the levels). Same HOLD threshold; no lock, no OCT- reset (it never had one: the quick EDIT layers only); the page under it (PERFORM too) comes back when GLO is let go. A tap of GLO opens its MIXER page (0.2.25 layout round: the same cards, cells and meters as this layer; 0.2.21 .. 0.2.25 a tap opened TEMPO); GLO > TEMPO and the other pages are not in the layer.
- **OCT-:** QUICK MULTI is a SET layer as before: OCT- puts its four back as they were when it opened (no octave). In QUICK LFO, ENV and ARP OCT- / OCT+ keep their octave job (nothing is put back). In all four, OCT- held + a knob resets that card (0.2.10; see OCT- + knob: RESET), and then the release of OCT- does neither.
- **The header of every quick layer** (these four, SCL's QUICK SCALE, SEQ's QUICK SEQ, REC's QUICK REC) is "QUICK <PAGE>" in M after a small lightning bolt (ICON_X_QUICK: the ACCENT icon's glyph at 12 px, no new icon cell), the bolt in the accent and the words in the accent where it reads (3:1 on the background, else the text colour), so it is never read as a page's title. The tap's "HOLD [..] QUICK" hint goes as the layer opens; LFO, ENV, ARP and GLO share one "seen" bit.
- Code: `ui_layer.c` (`LAYERS`, `LY_Q`, `ly_page`, `layer_quick`), `ui_draw.c head_msg`; test: `select_test.c sweep_quick`.

**Leaving the SAVE family (owner bug, 2026-10-12).** On the SAVE pages (PATCHES, PATTERNS, MULTIS, TOOLS), in a SAVE dialog and on the NAME screen, EDIT, FX, ENV, LFO, ARP, SEQ, SCL, GLO and HOME always leave for their page and save nothing; a dialog or NAME is cancelled like OCT- ("NOT SAVED" once). Nothing on the SAVE pages may keep the unit there. Dialogs keep OCT+ = YES, OCT- = NO.

### 1.3 Consistency rules
1. Same page = same four columns, always. A page with more than four parameters has sub-pages, reached with the SELECT knob or by pressing the page button again (as Felucca, kept for now; to be reviewed after trying it in the emulator), with a dot indicator.
2. Dialogs and menus follow Felucca: OCT- = back, OCT+ = do it, with an on-screen hint.
3. The tempo is one parameter, on GLO's TEMPO page, KNOB 1, labelled TEMPO. The only other way to set it is KNOB 2 on the same page, labelled TAP (tap tempo, internal clock only: each detent of the knob, in either direction, is one tap; the BPM is the mean interval of the last four taps, set from the second tap on; 2 s without a tap starts a new run; the card shows the BPM while tapping). GLO's pages in order: GLO, TEMPO, SIDECHAIN, MIDI, MODULES, MIDI LEARN. With external MIDI clock it shows EXT and the knob is inert. No other control changes the tempo.
4. Part colours: see section 5a (each part has its own colour in every palette; the whole screen takes the colour of the selected part).
5. Every page in the emulator is screenshot-tested against a layout lint (Felucca already has this; DUELL's pages join it).
6. **Header and footer (0.2.1, owner 2026-10-12).** *Header:* the part badges at the left (0.2.10, owner 2026-10-14: no multi number, no asterisk and no REC mark after them, on any page; the room is the page name's), the **page name in the top middle** (centred on the screen's middle within the room between the badges and the battery; M, or S where M does not fit; then the octave readout gives way for a long name), the battery at the right with the **CPU bar** under it (DUELL 0.2.9, below). The transport cluster (metronome, play/stop mark, tempo number) is gone: the tempo is on the TEMPO page and PLAY's LED shows the running state (0.2.10: green while stopped, red while it plays or counts in). A message or a layer's label replaces the page name for its moment (a layer's label is "QUICK <PAGE>" after a small lightning bolt, in the accent: section "Quick edit layers"); a dialog, the count-in, UPDATE MODE, NAME and the GUIDE title themselves there (CONFIRM, COUNT-IN, UPDATE MODE, "SAVE U07", "GUIDE 2/6"); the MENU's title is centred there too. The splash and the ABOUT animation have no header. *Footer:* the engine's icon and name, then the **patch name in full** (up to 12 characters), and the page's number ("3/9") at the right; the page name is not repeated there. Its first row: the sequence's **16 step bars** (the playhead, the step cursor) **only on the sequencer's pages** (0.2.9, the owner: "hide the step strip except on sequencer pages": SEQ's STEP (and its GRID's PAGE / ACCENT line on a drum part) and AUTOMATION pages and the SEQ TOOLS layer, and the three ARP pages while the part's arp MODE is SEQUENCER, where the marked step is the arp's own, step arp_idx modulo LEN, while a key is held or latched; `ui_draw.c foot_steps`, `foot_playhead`); on every other page it is empty unless it has its own line (MIDI LEARN's, PERFORM's PRESETS / ALGO, PATTERNS / MULTIS' HOLD SAVE hint). Elsewhere the footer is not redrawn for a step or the playhead. *Naming:* a page is named for what it is; pages of ONE unit carry their place in it ("DELAY 1/2", "EQ 2/2", "VOICE 2/2"), a number stands alone only where there really are several units (LFO 1 / LFO 2, AMP ENV / FILTER ENV, MATRIX 1-4 / MATRIX 5-8 for the slots).

---

## 2. Architecture

### 2.1 Two parts
- Two parts, A and B. Each part has its own engine, preset, level, pan, 2 LFOs, 2 envelopes, matrix, insert effect, arpeggiator and MIDI channel.
- Felucca has 4 tracks and 8 voices shared. DUELL keeps the 8-voice pool in the first build and splits it per part after the CPU measurement (below). **[verify]** how Felucca allocates voices and how many engines can run at once.
- **Ten engines** are available on both parts: ANALOG, FM6, PHASE, LOFI, VOICE, TRIO, WHEEL, PHYS, NOISE and DUELL's own **WAVE** (a wavetable engine, section 2.6). **DRUM is removed** (DUELL is a synthesizer, not a drum machine): hidden the way Felucca hides its retired DIGITAL engine, so stored data layouts stay valid. NOISE stays (owner: "very cool"). **[decide later]** after measuring, heavy engines (FM6, PHYS) may get a per-part voice limit. This is a measurement result, not a promise.
- **The sample engines SAMPLE, GRAIN and SLICE are removed (DUELL 0.2.9, owner: "REMOVE the sample engines"; the built-in samples were too small to be flexible, and WAVE replaces them).** Their code, the built-in sample data (PIANO, FLUTE, SAX and SLICE's break: about 124 KB of ADPCM) and the user sample slots are gone from the firmware. As with DIGITAL, their **engine numbers stay reserved** (4, 8, 13; `core.h ENGI_RETIRED`, `engines.c ENG_RETIRED`): they are in no list (the ENGINE knob, PATCHES > TYPE, the editor's list; `NENG_SHOWN` = 10), never offered and never picked (a pick of one, INIT on such a part or the editor's ENG, gives ANALOG's first sound). **Stored data that names one loads safely**: a saved patch, a multi part, a project or autosave part keeps its engine number and every value as stored (a save writes them back unchanged), **plays silent**, shows its engine as "-" with blank EDIT cards that no knob changes (its matrix engine slots read UNUSED, MORPH leaves them alone), and the header says **NO ENGINE** once (at the load, not on its notes); the ENGINE knob takes the part to a real engine. A stored patch on a removed engine stays in the PATCH store, is listed in PATCHES ALL (and its category), under no engine filter, and RESTORE (its slot's factory sound comes back) removes it. Their 21 factory sounds (SAMPLE 8, GRAIN 7, SLICE 6) were replaced by 21 sounds of the same categories on the other engines (SOUND_RECIPES.md, batch R): the factory bank holds **114 sounds** as before. The flash of the user sample slots USR1 / USR2 (0xA0000..0xC7FFF, 160 KiB) was left unused; DUELL 0.2.14 uses its first 24 KiB (0xA0000..0xA5FFF) for the WAVE user wavetables (section 2.6, "User tables"), 0xA6000..0xC7FFF stays free. The editor's sample commands stay reserved and answer "unsupported" (`web/EDITOR_PROTOCOL.md`).
- No drum/sequencer tracks in the first build: the product is two synth parts played from the keys or from any MIDI source (sequencer, DAW, keyboard, controller).

### 2.2 Stereo / DUEL mode (superseded by section 5b.1: three modes DUEL, STEREO, LAYER, on HOME sub-page 2)
- **DUEL** (default): part A is hard left, part B is hard right. Each part's own pan and stereo spread are forced to the centre of its side (stereo effects fold to the part's side). This is the dawless mode.
- **STEREO**: both parts are full stereo; each has PAN and WIDTH. For headphones and for playing a single sound.
- Switching is click-free (50 ms crossfade of the pan law).

### 2.3 What SEQ, PLAY/STOP and REC do (decided: option A)
Felucca's sequencer is not part of the product, but the three buttons exist and may not lie. Options considered:

| Option | SEQ | PLAY/STOP | REC |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **A (chosen)** | Per-part 16-step pattern of the arpeggiator (a rhythm/gate pattern for the ARP). | Start/stop the arp and tempo-synced modulation (restart from the top). Follows external MIDI start/stop. | Record the played notes into the arp's held notes (latch). |
| B | Full Felucca sequencer, trimmed to 2 parts. | Transport. | Record. |
| C | Unused, screen says "NOT IN DUELL". | Start/stop arp only. | Unused. |

#### Arp, chord keys and scale (SCL, ARP, SEQ)
- **The arp is Felucca's arp**, per part (the part picked with ALGORITHM): modes, rate, gate, octaves, swing, latch. **[verify]** against `seq.c`.
- **ONE GLOBAL SCALE (owner, 2026-10-12; replaces SCALE LINK).** SCALE, ROOT and OFF-SCALE KEYS are settings of the **instrument**: kept with the device settings (two free settings bytes, `favorites.factory[15][3]` bit 7 the format flag, bit 6 PLAY, bits 0-3 the scale, and `[15][4]` the root; `ui.c` `scale_sync`), shared by both parts everywhere, in every output mode (DUEL, STEREO, LAYER). **The default is CHROMATIC (root C, OFF-SCALE SNAP):** a new unit and any older settings blob read it (a blob without the flag bit is the defaults, the former SCALE LINK bits are not read). **No patch, part, MULTI or factory sound stores or applies a scale any more:** the patch writer never writes one (the AK block's SCALE slot, ordinal 8, is retired: an old record that holds one is read and the value discarded), the factory sounds set no SCALE (CHRD and VOIC only), a MULTI load ignores the per-part SCALE / QNT it may hold (the ids stay reserved, no format change) and never touches the global scale, and the lend rule (`ui.c` `ds_keys`) lends CHRD and VOIC only. The per-part SCALE / ROOT / QNT appear in no UI; every consumer reads `core.h` `scale_of` / `root_of` (and `scale_mask`, `note_snap` in `seq.c`). MENU > CONTROL > **SCALE LINK is removed** (it was never in the editor protocol's MENU list, so no id is lost). The SCL pages and the quick layer are described in the SCL row of section 1.
  - **OFF-SCALE KEYS (replaces QNT; QNT's OFF / SNAP / WHITE / SEQ are gone; the parameter id 27 holds the multimode filter's KEY TRK since 0.2.21, section 2.5).** Two values, global: **SNAP** (the default): a key or MIDI note outside the scale moves to the **nearest** scale note before it reaches the parts, a tie goes **down** (C major: C# gives C, F# gives F, G# gives G; pentatonic C D E G A: F gives E, B gives C); **PLAY**: out-of-scale notes pass unchanged. With the scale CHROMATIC there is nothing to snap. Keys and MIDI input take the same path (`kb_pitch` for the keys, `chord_make` for any note that starts a chord or a single note), so **both parts always sound the same note for the same key**, MIDI OUT sends the note after snapping (what sounds), the key LEDs and the arp's latched keys follow it, and a note already sounding keeps its pitch when the scale changes (every source remembers the notes it started). Kits (DRUM, SLICE: an engine that maps the keys itself) are never snapped.
  - **Chord keys (SCL > CHORD, VOICING)** (`chord.c`): CHRD and VOIC stay per-part patch settings. The played note is **snapped first** (if SNAP), then the chord is built on it. **DIATONIC types (DIA3, DIA7)** stack scale degrees of the global scale (every tone is in the scale); with PLAY and a root outside the scale the root stays as played and the shape is that of the nearest scale degree below it (C# in C major is C#-F-G#). **FIXED shapes (MAJ MIN DOM7 MAJ7 MIN7 SUS4 POW)** keep their intervals from the (snapped) root; their tones are the user's explicit choice and are **not** snapped again (MIN on C# with SNAP: C Eb G). **DIA3 / DIA7 with the scale CHROMATIC** have no scale to stack: they play the plain MAJ triad / MAJ7. The value stays as chosen; the CHORD page shows the card **faded with "= MAJ" / "= MAJ7"** and the graph panel says **"NEEDS SCALE: PLAYS MAJ"** (MAJ7) in full. MONO / LEGATO / UNISON parts play the chord's root. With chord keys on, one held key gives a scale-aware chord and **the arp plays those chord notes**; with chord keys off, the arp plays the keys you hold (the snapped notes).
  - **Scale lights are automatic (0.2.5).** While the global scale is not CHROMATIC the playable keys show it, with no menu step: the scale's keys are lit at one level (`fm1_led_scl`), the root's keys at the mode's top level, **full and STEADY, never pulsing** (`fm1_led_root`; 0.2.11, owner), the keys outside the scale depend on the global OFF-SCALE KEYS setting: with **SNAP** (they play a scale note) they are completely dark, with **PLAY** (they play as they are) they are faintly lit so they can be seen and the two settings look different, and a key played, held or sounding (keys, arp, sequencer, chord; with a scale and without) **pulses** between the mode's top level and a still-bright trough (0.2.11, owner; a root key too), by the rule under the table. The levels follow MENU > DISPLAY > LEDS (one brightness for everything, the keys too; see LEDS modes below for the table), each state at least about 1.4 times the one below it (out of the scale < in the scale < played trough < root = played peak), and the played keys' trough never goes down to the scale keys' level (MAX: see its row). With CHROMATIC every key is in the scale, so nothing is shown and the keys look as without it. The lit keys are exactly the keys whose note, as the keys play now (TRN, OFF-SCALE SNAP / PLAY; the octave buttons change only the octave), is in the scale. Not shown where the keys have a map of their own (NAME, the layers' maps, the DRUM grid) or the engine maps the keys itself (DRUM, SLICE). MENU > CONTROL > SCALE LEDS: **AUTO** (default) or **OFF** (stored in bit 7 of the settings byte `favorites.factory[15][28]`; clear in every older setting and on a new unit = AUTO; the old bit 6 "SCALE LEDS ON" is retired and ignored, so old ON and OFF settings alike load as AUTO). With CHROMATIC the row has nothing to show; the menu cannot fade a row, so it stays as it is.
  - **HOLD has three values (0.2.9, owner): OFF, ARP, ALL** (per part, ARP 1/3, right after MODE; stored 0, 1, 2, so older patches, multis and editor data keep their meaning). ARP (1) is the old ON: with the arp on, the chord stays latched after the keys are up. **ALL** latches the keys even with the arp off, for pads and drones: a played chord (keys or MIDI IN) keeps sounding after the keys are released, its voices held without retriggering; the next chord (the first key down when no key is held) replaces it; with the arp on, ALL holds the chord as ARP does. The latch ends, with the normal release, when HOLD is set to OFF or ARP (arp off), and STOP ALL clears it; turning the arp on or off keeps the chord (the arp plays it, or the voices hold it). Each part latches on its own, so in DUEL one part can drone while the other is played. SMART and the morph never touch it. Code: `seq.c hold_all_keys`, `arp_add`, `input_off`, `events_block`; test: `tests/hold_test.c`.
  - **LEDS modes (0.2.5 names).** MENU > DISPLAY > LEDS is OFF, LOW, MEDIUM (default), MAX (before: OFF, DIM LO, DIM HI, INV). Only the names changed: the stored values are the same (MEDIUM 0, MAX 1, OFF 2, LOW 3), so every saved setting loads into the same mode; MAX is still the stock look (idle LEDs lit, active ones dark). Levels (0.2.5, share of a fully lit LED; all in one table at the top of the LED code in `hal/fm1_input.h`, "THE LED LEVELS", to tune by editing numbers): OFF idle 0 %; LOW idle a pulse of about 1/60 of a frame, about 1.7 %; MEDIUM idle lit on 1 scan frame in 4, 25 %; MAX idle 100 %; **the keyboard (0.2.9 rev. 2)**, one table per mode (`KEY_LV` in `ui_input.c`, in 240ths of full; the scan lights each set on whole frames by its own first order sigma-delta, `fm1_led_key_levels`, so 1/2, 1/3, 1/4, 1/5, 1/6, 1/8, 1/10, 1/12 are exact; the lit frames evenly spread, a dark run at most 9 frames, ~91 Hz, at 1/10; no glow under them; the played keys' pulse (0.2.11): `FM1_LED_PULSE_FRAMES` 1024 frames, ~1.13 s by default, the breath's smooth shape from the peak down to the trough and back, the trough the 5th column of `KEY_LV`):

  | LEDS | out of the scale, PLAY (SNAP: dark) | in the scale | root (steady) | played / sounding: peak .. trough |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | OFF | the glow, 1/60, ~1.7 % | 1/4 (60/240), 25 % | lit, 100 % | 100 % .. 7/10 (168/240), 70 % |
  | LOW | the glow, 1/60, ~1.7 % | 1/6 (40/240), 16.7 % | 1/2 (120/240), 50 % | 50 % .. 7/20 (84/240), 35 % |
  | MEDIUM (default) | the glow, 1/60, ~1.7 % | 1/2 (120/240), 50 % | lit, 100 % | 100 % .. 7/10 (168/240), 70 % |
  | MAX | 1/8 (30/240), 12.5 % | 7/10 (168/240), 70 % | lit, 100 % | 1/4 (60/240), 25 % .. dark, 0 % |

  **The played-key pulse (0.2.11, owner 2026-10-14).** The root is steady and never pulses. Keys that are played or sounding pulse slowly from the peak (the root's level) down to the trough and back; a root key played is full at the peak too, and differs from the steady root by the pulse. MAX keeps its inverted look: there a played key is darker than the lit keys around it, so it pulses between a dim level (25 %) and dark. **The speed follows the notes:** with the transport running (song or sequencer, a count-in too) or an arp sounding (a key held with the arp on) the pulse has the period of the tempo's beat, 60 000 / BPM ms, clamped to 150..2000 ms, and is phase-locked to the beat clock (the peak as the beat starts, the trough by the next one; the hardware re-locks it when it is more than 1/16 of a period off); a plain held key with nothing running pulses free at the default 1024 frames, about 0.9 Hz. Not done: a per-note fade for a lone sounding note (the pulse stays on the beat or free). Code: `ui_input.c ui_leds`, `fm1_led_pulse`, `fm1_led_key_levels` (5 levels); tests: ui_test (levels, ratios, rule), input_test (steady root, pulse shape, period).

  The played pulse holds without a scale too: a key held or sounding pulses with OFF, LOW and MEDIUM, and with MAX it pulses dim..dark among the lit keys. With MAX, SNAP's keys out of the scale and a held key are both dark (pressing such a key shows nothing on it). The buttons are not in this table: their lit LEDs stay fully lit in every mode but MAX. The DRUM grid's beats (above the grid's other glowing keys): about 10 % with LOW (1 frame in 12 over the glow), 1 frame in 2 (50 %) with MEDIUM (the high step `FM1_LED_HI_ON` / `_OF`, now theirs alone), the glow alone with OFF and MAX. The breath keeps its peaks (about 60 %, LOW about 30 %). The glow is now one level for every mode (about 1.7 %; before MEDIUM's was about 3.3 %).
  - **Stored steps follow the scale (0.2.9).** The step sequencer snaps a step's notes when it plays them (same rule as the keys: SNAP moves a note to the nearest scale note, PLAY passes it, CHROMATIC does nothing), so a stored pattern follows the global SCALE, ROOT and OFF-SCALE when they change. The stored notes stay as entered or recorded, so CHROMATIC gives the original pattern back. Drum lanes and the DRUM kit are not snapped. MIDI IN and MIDI OUT are unchanged.
  - **Chord keys under the arp follow the scale (0.2.9).** When the SCALE, ROOT or OFF-SCALE changes while keys are held under the arp, the arp builds each held chord again on its key (C major to C minor, DIA3 on D4: D F A becomes D F Ab). The arp keeps its place in the phrase; held notes are not retriggered.
  - **Arps and the SEQUENCER:** the arp uses the held (snapped) notes and its octave steps; SEQUENCER step offsets move onto the global scale when one is set (a step's note offset is moved onto the scale, down) and in semitones when CHROMATIC (as before).
  - **HIDE WHAT DOES NOT APPLY (owner rule, 2026-10-15; replaces the fade-with-reason rule of 2026-10-12 / 13, "NO SILENT IGNORING").** A control that a **selection** makes inapplicable (the engine, the reverb or insert type, the delay mode, the output mode DUEL, the scale, the clock source, a MODULES switch) is **not shown**: its card is not drawn and **its column stays empty** (the panel's background; the four cards sit above the four knobs, so the other cards keep their knobs K1..K4). The knob of a hidden card **does nothing and says nothing** (no header message, no footer line, no value moved; the same as a blank 0xFF slot). **ONE mechanism:** `ui_draw.c` `card_hidden_vp(vp)` is the predicate for a parameter's card (drawing and `ui_input.c` `edit_param` / `reset_desc` both ask it), `draw_column_hide(column)` draws the empty column (the cache mark `\x7f`); a page with its own hand-drawn cards calls `draw_column_hide` under the same condition and ignores the knob (SCALE's OFF-SCL, MODE's XFADE and SPLIT, MIDI's P1 / P2 CH, GLO > SIDECHAIN's four cards (PART FX OFF), AUTOMATION's PLAY, PATCHES' FAV and RESTORE, SAVE > TOOLS' RESTORE, the MIXER's PAN and SPACE, PERFORM's SPACE, the engine page's FAV). **A control that can function is never hidden because its value is zero now** (RES at CUT OFF, the FLT depths, the delay modulation's MRATE at MDEPTH 0: a zero can be changed). **No empty page:** a page whose cards are all hidden (INSERT 2/2 = MIX with the INSERT OFF; AMP ENV on FM6; the FX / DELAY pages with SENDS OFF) is still shown, with its title and four empty columns, and stays in the page cycle, so the page numbers (2/2) never move under the player's hand. **The selectors stay:** a card that is the choice itself is shown even when its choice has no effect now: the matrix's DEST (its panel line says in words that the route does nothing; hiding it would lock the slot), CHRD on DIA3 / DIA7 with no scale (it plays the plain MAJ / MAJ7, the panel says it), and every TYPE / MODE card. Hidden today: DELAY MODE and SPREAD in DUEL, SPREAD in DIGITAL, MRATE in RANDOM / OCT UP (PING-PONG hides nothing); INSERT A B C MIX while the INSERT is OFF; the ADSR of FM6 (its operators' envelopes); a synced LFO's RATE (SYNC replaces it); the PAN of a part in DUEL; the TEMPO and TAP cards with an external MIDI clock (CLK stays and says INT USB or TRS); DUCK 1/2's second card for SRC MIDI (it has none); XFADE and SPLIT outside LAYER; P1 / P2 CH while MIDI IN is SELECTED (P2 CH in LAYER too); OFF-SCL while the scale is CHROMATIC; FAV on a sound in no slot and on a place in no slot; RESTORE on a slot without a saved record and RESTORE ALL with none saved; and the cards of a module that is OFF (below). Keys that cannot run (PERFORM's) and buttons (PLAY with SEQ OFF) still say why in the header: they are keys, not cards. The web editor follows the same rule (`layout.js` `inertReason`, `ginert`: a hidden control keeps its place, invisible).
  - **Global vs part pages.** `params.c` `page_global()` is the one flag the header uses: on a global page both part badges are lit equally. Today: the SCALE page and GLO's pages (0.2.21: they include the global effects CHORUS, DELAY, REVERB, SHIMMER and BASS MONO, which lit the selected part's badge only while they were FX pages; 0.2.22: SIDECHAIN too, its setup is for both parts). FX's last page, SEND FX, is the selected part's: one badge. (TEST, the CPU test screen of MENU > SYSTEM, is not a page and lights the selected part's badge, as its morph is that part's). Other global pages (MODE, MULTIS) still light the selected part's badge; extend the function to change that.
- **Page order, top-down (0.2.18, owner rule: a mode or type first, an amount next, a level or mix last; `docs/PAGE_ORDER_AUDIT.md`; page tables and hand-drawn pages only, no saved id moves).** MIXER **K1 MUTE, K2 PAN, K3 SPACE, K4 LEVEL**; MULTI FILTER stays **CUT, RES, MODE, SLOPE** (owner, 0.2.20: not reordered; QUICK MULTI CUT, RES, MODE, REL; 0.2.21: it is page 1/2, 2/2 is KTRK, FILTER ENV, LFO 1, and the engines' own filter pages come before it); ARP 1/3 **MODE, HOLD, ORD, OCT**, ARP 2/3 **RATE, GATE, SWG, PROB**, ARP 3/3 TRGT (QUICK ARP stays MODE, RATE, GATE, OCT); TEMPO **CLK, TEMPO, TAP, SWG**; DUCK 2/2 **CURVE, ATK, RLS**; VOICE 1/2 **VCE, PRIO, ALLOC, DTUNE**, VOICE 2/2 **GLMOD, GLD, SPRD, PAN**; FM6 EDIT 1 **SLOT, ALG, FB, MLVL**, EDIT 2 **MRAT, MEG, VMOD, DTUN** (the cards only: `engine_t.uiord`, `params.c pg_id`; the stored E1..E8, patches, multis, matrix and MIDI LEARN do not move; the pages are titled PATCH and MACRO). **Left as they are:** TRIO's PW (a stored E slot: a format change), AMP DEST's ESYNC (a unit switch, rarely touched), INSERT 2/2 and ARP 3/3 (one card each, forced by the card count), the MIXER's SPACE (a global macro on a part page, a deliberate exception: it is PERFORM's K2 too). **Earlier orders (0.2.9):** ARP 1/3 MODE, HOLD, RATE, GATE; ARP 2/3 SWG, PROB, OCT, ORD; ARP 3/3 TRGT. EQ 1/2 LOW Hz, LOW dB, HIGH Hz, HIGH dB; EQ 2/2 MID Hz, MID Q, MID dB (first where to cut, then how much). DUCK 1/2 SRC, RATE (THR, NOTE by SRC), DEPTH; DUCK 2/2 ATK, RLS, CURVE. DELAY 2/2 PING, SPREAD, RATE, DEPTH. The FX round keeps the signal-flow order. AMP DEST PIT, FLT, SHP, ESYNC. LFO 1 and LFO 2: WAVE, RATE, SYNC, FADE, then the pages LFO 1 TRIG and LFO 2 TRIG: TRIG, PHS, POL; LFO 2's RATE is faded while its SYNC is on (as LFO 1's). EDIT's VOICE pages (the voice's own PAN; MUTE is on GLO and the MIXER only, and PAN stays step-lockable).
- **The layout round (0.2.25, owner; `release_drafts/LAYOUT_PROPOSAL.md` with the owner's decisions).** GLO's first page is **MIXER** (K1 LEVEL 1, K2 PAN 1, K3 LEVEL 2, K4 PAN 2, the MUTE cells, the meters; HOME's MIXER page is gone, HOME's round is MORPH, MORPH SET, MODE); **STEREO MASTER** (K1 BASS MONO, K2 WIDTH) replaces WIDTH and MASTER; **COMPRESSOR** K1 RATIO (OFF lowest), K2 THRESH, K3 ATTACK, K4 RELEASE; **EQ 1/2** LOW FREQ, LOW GAIN, MID FREQ, MID GAIN, **EQ 2/2** MID Q, HIGH FREQ, HIGH GAIN (no switch). **Two-line labels** (`ui_draw.c label_split`): a label too wide for the card's 50 px breaks at a space into two lines, or carries its break (`desc_shown`: PATCH / LEVEL, BASS / MONO, the EQ's bands, PART 1 / CH, PHASE's WAVE / SHAPING and SHAPING / ENV); the value then takes the third line in S, without a gauge (the TEMPO page's clock card's method). Display names (the stored labels stay the keys): PATCH LEVEL (TRIM), CHANCE (the arp's PROB), DRIFT (SHIMMER's MOD), MOD (the delay's MDEPTH; CHANCE in RANDOM / OCT UP). Hidden when they do nothing (the hide rule): UNISON DETUNE outside UNISON, SPREAD in MONO and LEGATO. **Pictures in the panel** (`ui_graph.c`, drawn in the UI loop when a value changes): the EQ curve (eq_design's coefficients), the COMPRESSOR's transfer curve and a live gain-reduction bar, the STEREO MASTER picture, the filter response on both MULTI FILTER pages and the VCF, the arp's pattern on ARP 1/3 and 2/3 (OFF and SEQ keep the scope), the envelope / LFO curve on AMP DEST, FILTER ENV DEST, LFO 1 DEST and LFO 2 DEST. Not built (they wait for the owner's review): the rename tables F1 / F2 of the proposal.
- **SEQUENCER is an arp mode** (decided): the arp's MODE list is SEQUENCER, UP, DOWN, UP-DOWN, ... with **SEQUENCER first**, so it is not missed. Choosing it makes the arp play the 16-step pattern: each step has gate (on/off), accent and a note offset (scale degrees when a scale is set, semitones when CHROMATIC), applied to the held or chord notes; it runs from the key-down on the internal clock, PLAY not needed, and PLAY does not also play a SEQUENCER part's steps from C4 (0.2.12, section 3.7). The SEQ button opens the pattern editor of the selected part (16 steps, KNOB 1-4 = the selected step's gate, accent, offset, length). The pattern is saved with the preset.

#### ARP TARGET: which parts of the sound the arp moves (new)
A voice has one root pitch (the held key) and an arp pitch (the note the arp plays now). ARP TARGET chooses, per part, which of the engine's **pitch units** follow the arp pitch; the rest keep following the root pitch.

- **Pitch units per engine** (each engine declares its own; an engine with no separable units has ALL only). Checked against the code (appendix A): FM6 = OP1..OP6; TRIO = OSC1..OSC3; ANALOG = OSC A and OSC B only (the SUB is OSC B an octave down); WHEEL = 9 partials; PHASE = main and SUB. **ALL only**: LOFI, VOICE (one pitch unit; SAMPLE and GRAIN were too until 0.2.9). **No arp target at all**: DRUM (the note picks a lane, not a pitch; SLICE too until 0.2.9). Two reads of the root pitch stay on the root by design: ANALOG's filter key tracking and VOICE's formant tracking. ANALOG's OSC B ignores unison detune, TUNE and bend today (it drops `m->fine`); fixing that is part of the arp-target work.
- **ARP TARGET** is a bit mask on the ARP page: ALL (classic arp), or any combination of units. Set on the ARP page, KNOB 4 steps through the combinations; the screen shows the units as a row of boxes, lit = follows the arp.
- **Behaviour**:
  - Mask = ALL: the normal arp (a new note-on per step).
  - Mask = some units: **one voice per held key** keeps sounding while the key is held; each arp step changes the pitch of the masked units only (with the part's glide setting, so a step can jump or slide), and the unmasked units stay on the root. In FM6 this means a modulator can step through the arp while the carrier holds the root: the pitch stays, the timbre moves. The arp step is also a matrix source (STEP) so the user can shape the movement.
  - Gate and accent from the arp still apply as a pulse on the masked units' level, so rhythm remains audible.
- **Engineering [verify]**: voices get a second pitch (`arp_pitch16`) next to `pitch16` (core.h); each engine's note computation takes the pitch per unit. FM6 currently computes all operator frequencies from one value (`fm6_note_logfreq`, called once per block in `eng_fm6.c`); it needs one value per operator. TRIO already derives osc 2/3 from the base pitch (`trio_inc`). Cost is a few extra frequency lookups per voice per block. No extra voices.
- Tests: with mask = ALL the render is bit-identical to the plain arp (golden hashes); with a partial mask the unmasked units' frequencies are measured to stay on the root over 100 arp steps.

### 2.4 Modulation (more than Felucca)
Felucca has per track (checked in `core.h` / `params.c`): **1 ADSR envelope** (with FLT / PIT / SHP depth amounts), **1 LFO** (SIN TRI SAW SQR S&H, 4 destination amounts, rate or tempo sync, NOTE/FREE trigger, BI/UNI, fade), and a **4-slot matrix** (sources LFO ENV VEL KEY RAND MODW AT EXPR S&H SLEW; destinations PITCH CUT SHP AMP PAN DIST CHO DLY REV RATE VIB E1-E8 DEPTH). DUELL per part, as the owner asked for "another envelope and LFO":
- Envelopes: ENV 1 (as Felucca; shown as **AMP ENV**, see below) + **FILTER ENV** (new, same stages and ENV SYNC, also a matrix source).
- LFOs: LFO 1 (as Felucca) + **LFO 2** (new, same shapes, S&H, SLEW, trigger, polarity, tempo sync, also a matrix source).
- **LFO TRIG defaults to FREE (owner decision, DUELL 0.2.2; Felucca's default was NOTE):** both LFOs run free by default (the phase is never put back), a new or INIT sound and every factory sound (all 108, both LFOs, set in `tools/duell_sounds.py`) and factory multi (the four emulator-made ones, M01..M04 then, M03..M06 since 0.2.13, and M13..M16 rewritten by `tools/factory_multis_lfo_free.py`) are FREE. NOTE is the option: it restarts the LFO at PHS on a new phrase, and an arp is one phrase (`track_t.arp_ph`). FADE starts over on each new phrase with either setting. Saved data keeps its own TRIG: multi, project and editor records store every value; the sparse patch store (`upreset.c`) keeps NOTE as its own default for the two ids (`up_def`), so a patch stored before 0.2.2, which left a NOTE out, still loads NOTE.
- **Classic arrangement (owner, 2026-10-11):** ENV 1 is the **AMP envelope** (ATK DEC SUS REL shape every note's volume; ENV 1 DEST adds its FLT / PIT / SHP depths). FILTER ENV is the **FILTER envelope**: page FILTER ENV DEST with FLT (cutoff depth) and SHP (shape depth). LFO 2 gets page LFO 2 DEST with PIT (vibrato), FLT and SHP. Same units, ranges (-64..+63) and behaviour as ENV 1 DEST / LFO 1 DEST, added in the same place (voice.c track_render: FILTER ENV's level x FLT into the cutoff input, LFO 2's value (FADE, DEPTH2 applied) x PIT / FLT / SHP into pitch, cutoff, shape). Defaults 0 (every stored sound unchanged); new factory sounds set FILTER ENV FLT (+40) themselves. FILTER ENV PITCH and LFO 2 AMP (tremolo) stay matrix routes. Page order: ENV: AMP ENV, AMP DEST, FILTER ENV, FILTER ENV DEST, MOD 1, MOD 2; LFO: LFO 1, LFO 1 SYNC, LFO 1 DEST, LFO 2, LFO 2 SYNC, LFO 2 DEST, MOD 1, MOD 2. Ids 114..118 (P_E2FLT P_E2SHP P_L2PIT P_L2FLT P_L2SHP; P_COUNT 127, the 7-bit maximum; stores load older ones by count). MORPH: the FLT / SHP depths roll as continuous values (like their ENV 1 / LFO 1 siblings, in the filter "no dead path" rule too), LFO 2 PIT is a pitch item (zero stays zero, PITCH ON lifts it); they draw from a stream of their own so the other values of a seed roll as before.
- **Naming (2026-10-11):** ENV 1's pages are titled **AMP ENV** and **AMP DEST** ("AMP ENV DEST" is 12 characters, one more than the 11 the old footer held; the title room is now the header's, ui_graph.c page_parts), the matrix source ENV1 is **AENV** (stored value 2 unchanged, its place in the list too: OFF AENV ENV2 LFO1 LFO2 ...). The printed button stays ENV (hardware); FILTER ENV / FILTER ENV DEST keep their names (series: AMP ENV, FILTER ENV). The help hints say AMP ENVELOPE / FILTER ENVELOPE. The automation list keeps "ENV ATK" etc. (a 5-character prefix does not fit its column).
- **SHP (the shape input) on every engine (2026-10-11):** the SHP depths (AMP DEST, FILTER ENV DEST, LFO 1 / 2 DEST) and the matrix's SHP move each engine's timbre control, as an offset (centre = the knob as set, bit for bit: the goldens unchanged). ANALOG pulse width / sync detune, PHASE distortion depth, FM6 feedback, TRIO duty, NOISE colour, GRAIN position, FORMANT buzz (as before), and new: LOFI the pulse width around DUTY (smooth, +-12.5 % of the period as ANALOG's PW, 6.25..93.75 %; PLS and STEP's pulse; not the WRAM table, a stepped switch the morph would make a cliff of), PHYS STRC (SYMP: BUZZ, its structure input), SAMPLE DRV (its drive; CUT is FLT's), SLICE TONE (its only tone control, the same one FLT moves; DCAY acts before the render, where the shape input is not known), WHEEL brightness (the upper bars toward full and the sub bars toward silence, or the reverse, smooth, renormalised; PERC's silent 1' stays silent). The morph's new routes still add SHP only to the engines that had it (adding the five would change what a saved End B seed rolls into).
- **LFO polarity in the matrix (owner bug report, 2026-10-12: a sine LFO on PAN seemed to only go to one side):** per-voice destinations (PITCH +-11.8 st, CUT / SHP +-63 steps at AMT 63) were always centred on the base value, as the LFO 1 / 2 DEST pages (PIT FLT SHP, also centred; those pages have no PAN). AMP (tremolo, matrix and LFO DEST AMP) is deliberately one-sided: a bipolar LFO is read as 0..1 and AMT 64 swings the level from 0 up to the base, never above it; a negative AMT inverts the phase. The per-block destinations (PAN, sends, RATE, VIB, E1..E8) were the inconsistency: a bipolar source (LFO 1/2, S&H, SLEW, KEY, RAND) at AMT 63 moved them by +-the whole range, so on PAN (-64..63, base 0) the sine was clipped flat at both ends for most of the cycle and sounded like a pan that jumps right (or left at a negative AMT) and stays. Now a bipolar source swings +-half the range (its full swing = the whole range, so PAN at AMT 63 sweeps full left to full right around its own value, AMT -64 the same inverted); a unipolar source (ENV, VEL, MODW, AT, EXPR, MORPH, FILTER ENV, a UNI LFO) still moves it by the whole range one way. Remember TRIG NOTE restarts the LFO at PHS on each fresh phrase: a slow LFO on short notes only covers the start of its cycle (set TRIG FREE for a continuous sweep). **An arp is one phrase (owner bug report, 2026-10-12: LFO 1 -> PAN and -> DIST did nothing on a layered ARP sound, LFO 1 -> CUT did):** with the arp running, the LFO (TRIG NOTE) and its FADE restart when the keys are pressed, no longer at every arp step (before, each 1/16 step with a gap restarted the sine at phase 0, so a per-block destination only ever saw the first quarter-wave of the swing, a faint one-sided shift; CUT, whose range is tens of semitones, still showed it). Letting go of all keys and pressing again is a new phrase (`track_t.arp_ph`, set by `seq.c` arp_step after the first sounding step, cleared when nothing is held; voice.c trk_note_on). PAN and every matrix destination work the same in STEREO and LAYER (same mix code; BAL, XFADE and the split only scale levels); in DUEL part 1 is hard left and part 2 hard right by design, so EDIT > VOICE 2 PAN shows the faded card with the reason DUEL (since 0.2.7 the matrix's PAN destination is faded DUEL too, 2.4a). No factory sound routes an LFO to a per-block destination, so none changed.
- **SAT blend (owner, 2026-10-15, DUELL 0.2.14):** SAT 0 is the bypass (the block untouched, bit for bit). SAT 1 .. 11 outputs `dry + (wet - dry) * SAT / 12` (the wet: low cut, drive, clip, tone, make-up, as before); from SAT 12 up the wet alone, sample for sample as before. Switching on from 0 restarts the filter states from the signal (the high-pass at the first sample, the two low-passes at rest), not from what the last use left. Cost: nothing at 12 up, one multiply and divide per sample below. The wet alone at SAT 1 cut the bass and ~1.3 dB of level (a step from OFF); now 1 differs from the dry by -33 dB (under it, RMS, 220 Hz sine), 4 by -21 dB, 8 by -15 dB, 12 by -12 dB. `tests/insert_test.c` test_dist.
- **CRUSH BITS curve (owner, 2026-10-15, DUELL 0.2.14):** INSERT A of TYPE CRUSH, 128 detents, every detent a different depth (the quantiser step is fractional, Q8, `ins_bits.h` from `tools/gen_ins_bits.py`). Whole bit counts sit on knots: 1 bit at A 0, 2 at 16, 3 at 30, 4 at 44, 5 at 57, 6 at 69, 7 at 79, 8 at 90, 9 at 99, 10 at 108, 11 at 111, then every 3rd detent to 16 bits at 126 (127 too: clean, step 1). Between knots the bit count is linear (the step 2^(16 - bits)). The card shows the nearest whole count, 16 only where clean. 10 bits and more is under -60 dB of a loud voice: no map makes those clicks loud, so 11 .. 16 share the last 18 detents. The default A of the type CRUSH (reset) is 99 (9 bits); P_IA's own default stays 64 (the DRIVE's). Stored data from before (A >> 3 + 1 bits) is moved onto the knot of its bit count: project byte 67 (0 -> 1, PROJ_REV), multi version 2 -> 3, patch flag 128 (PTF_CB); the automation and locks on INS A of a CRUSH part too (core.h `ins_bits_migrate`). `tests/insert_test.c` test_bits_knob, test_bits_migrate.
- **INSERT TAPE and VINYL (owner, 2026-10-15, DUELL 0.2.14; `fx.c` `ins_lofi`, `params.c` `INS_DESC`):** two types appended to the INSERT list (IT_TAPE 9, IT_VINYL 10; `N_ITYPE` "TAPE", "VINYL"; no migration: before alpha). Written by us from the Warble idea of CHOMPI (a random delay wander; `docs/CHOMPI_RESEARCH.md`, MIT): no CHOMPI code or structure is used (it is float code for an M7), so no THIRD_PARTY / NOTICE entry. **Labels by the UI label law, each knob does only what it says; nothing is faded (every value is always possible).** TAPE: A WOW (PCT 0..127, default 47) the depth of the slow random wander of the delay (two one-poles of ~1.7 Hz on random targets every 0.25 .. 1 s: a wander of ~0.5 .. 2 Hz, 0 still, 127 = 65 samples peak to peak, about 30 cents peak to peak measured), B SAT (PCT, default 71) a tanh saturation 1x .. 5x (g = 1 + (B/64)^2) with the level kept by gain^-0.75 (a 12000 sine within 2 dB at every B), C TONE (CUTOFF, default 110 = 7 kHz) one one-pole high-cut. **Fixed in TAPE (not a knob):** the flutter, a sine of 6 .. 9 Hz (its rate follows the wander) at +-1.5 samples (~3 cents), and the hiss, uniform +-48 (rms ~ -66 dBFS after the default TONE), added before the high-cut. VINYL: A DUST (PCT, default 47) the density of the crackle, 0 none, else (16 + A mod 16) << (A div 16) / 16 clicks a second (1 at A 1, ~16 at 64, 248 at 127), a Poisson process, each click a random sign, size 100 .. 3500 (mostly small) and decay of 4 .. 32 samples, high-passed so it has no area; B NOISE (PCT, default 47) a white noise through a ~880 Hz one-pole high-pass, 0 none, 127 ~ -42 dBFS rms; C WARP (PCT, default 17) a sine at 33 1/3 rpm (0.556 Hz) moving the delay, 127 = +-35 samples (~5 cents, measured 10 cents peak to peak). **Fixed in VINYL:** the band narrowed on the signal only (one-pole high-pass ~110 Hz, one-pole low-pass 6 kHz; the clicks and the noise are added after it). **State:** the 256-sample line of FLANG / CHOR and the PHASR words (`ins_t.u`, a union: one type runs at a time), `ins_t` stays 580 bytes per part, the pool is unchanged. **Noise:** a xorshift32 in the state (`noise32`), seeded at the start of every run from the part's index (a part and its settings always give the same noise; it does not touch the shared `rng`). Like every type: MIX 0 or TYPE OFF runs nothing (bit for bit); a start clears the state; a TYPE change fades out and in. The INSERT does not run on a silent part (`mix_part`, after the 64-block tail): the hiss, dust and crackle sound while the part sounds and for ~46 ms after, not as a continuous bed (not changed: it would cost the INSERT's CPU on idle parts). Graph: TAPE draws SAT's curve, VINYL a trace (warp wave, noise ripple, clicks). Host cost (`cpu_scenarios_test` letter I, ANALOG 4 + 4 notes): TAPE +2.76 %, VINYL +2.88 % of the device's half per part (PHASR 2.83, SOFT 2.37, CRUSH 1.48; the device reads 10 .. 25 % lower than this scale). `tests/insert_test.c` test_lofi.
- Matrix: 8 slots per part (from 4). Sources: LFO 1-2, ENV 1-2, velocity, aftertouch, mod wheel, pitch bend, key, random-per-note, **MORPH**. Destinations: every knob parameter, plus LFO depth and rate (LFO to LFO, fixed evaluation order, one block of delay, no loops). As built, see the destination table below (2.4a).

#### 2.4a Matrix destinations (owner, after trying 0.2.6: "not enough destinations ... you do not know what E7 or SYNC is ... the column header says DST: it should say DEST"; built for the next build, 0.2.7)
Every destination is stored as its number in the slot's DST value (`mod.c` MD_*, `params.c` N_MDST); numbers are **append-only**, so a stored matrix (patch, multi, project, autosave, editor) means what it meant: 0..22 are 0.2.6's, 23..45 are new. Per-block destinations add an offset in the parameter's own units, **AMT 64 at a full source = the whole range** (a bipolar source swings +- half of it around the stored value, a unipolar one moves it one way), clamped to the range; the stored value is never changed (the offset lives only inside the audio block: `mod_begin` / `mod_end`, the bus values `mod_bus` / `mod_bus_end`). Several slots on one destination add.

| DST | card | DEST line | kind | notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | OFF | NONE | | |
| 1 2 3 4 | PITCH CUT SHP AMP | PITCH, MULTI FILTER CUTOFF, SHAPE (TIMBRE), VOICE AMP (TREMOLO) | per voice | as before (PITCH +-11.8 st at 63; CUT / SHP +-63 steps; AMP a gain <= 1) |
| 5 | PAN | PAN | per block | the PAN card is not shown in DUEL (each part on its own side) |
| 6 7 8 9 | SAT CHO DLY REV | SATURATION, CHORUS / DELAY / REVERB SEND | per block | the part's |
| 10 11 20 | RAT1 VIB DEP1 | LFO 1 RATE, LFO 1 PITCH DEPTH, LFO 1 DEPTH | per block | |
| 21 22 | RAT2 DEP2 | LFO 2 RATE, LFO 2 DEPTH | per block | |
| 12..19 | the engine's own label (POS, SYNC, ...) | "DEST: WAVE POS": the engine's name and label | per block | E1..E8 = the engine's eight stored slots in the order of its table (EDIT 1 KNOB 1..4, EDIT 2 KNOB 1..4 of the part's CURRENT engine, except FM6, whose cards show SLOT first since 0.2.18: its E1 is ALG as ever), named at draw time (the engine's descriptor, its mode-dependent one where it has one). Faded with a reason and doing nothing: **UNUSED** (a "-" slot; the line says where: "DEST: WAVE EDIT 2 KNOB 3", NOT USED BY THIS ENGINE), **REBUILD** (WAVE's TABLE: a table change builds a new table, the notes silent meanwhile, not a modulation; `mod_begin` skips it), **LOADS** (FM6's SLOT: loading a patch is the main loop's), **AT NOTE** (values an engine reads only as a note starts: SAMPLE SET; SLICE SRC, DIV, START, REV; none since 0.2.9 retired both engines: a retired engine's slots read UNUSED) |
| 23 | TRIM | PATCH TRIM | per block | the sound's TRIM (0.2.19; the destination was LEVEL, the part's one level; before BAL), so a route moves the patch's own loudness and not the player's mixer level; for smooth tremolo use AMP |
| 24 25 26 | the INSERT TYPE's labels (DRIVE TONE LEVEL / BITS RATE LPF / RATE DEPTH FDBK) | "DEST: INSERT DRIVE" | per block | its cards are not shown while TYPE is OFF; the DEST card shows it dim and the panel line says INS OFF ("INS A" .. then) |
| 27 | IMIX | INSERT MIX | per block | the MIX card is not shown while TYPE is OFF; the DEST card shows it dim, the panel line says INS OFF |
| 28 29 30 | EQLOW EQMID EQHI | EQ LOW / MID / HIGH GAIN | per block | 1 dB steps; the EQ runs while the moved gain is not 0. **Not offered since 0.2.25** (EQ HIGH clicks badly; a stored route plays as before) |
| 31 | DUCK | DUCK DEPTH | per block | also with DEPTH stored at 0: the pump / a trigger starts while a slot moves DEPTH (`fx.c` duck_pump, duck_note) |
| 32..35 | ATK2 DEC2 SUS2 REL2 | FILTER ENV ATTACK / DECAY / SUSTAIN / RELEASE | per block | FILTER ENV's steps are made each block (ENV SYNC: note values) |
| 36 37 | E2FLT E2SHP | FILTER ENV FILTER / SHAPE DEPTH | per block | the FILTER ENV DEST depths |
| 38 | FRES | MULTI FILTER RESONANCE | per block | the multimode filter's RES (2.5); on ANALOG, TRIO, NOISE it is the multimode filter's RES (their own RES is an engine knob, E slot, reached by its label); faded **ENGINE** only on an engine with no multimode filter (DRUM) |
| 39 40 | CRT CDP | CHORUS RATE, CHORUS DEPTH | bus value | see below |
| 41 | FDBK | DELAY FEEDBACK | bus value | |
| 42 43 | DMDEP DMRAT | DELAY MOD DEPTH, DELAY MOD RATE (the cards on DELAY 2/2 are MDEPTH and MRATE since 0.2.18; the short names stay: "MRAT" is FM6's own label) | bus value | the depth keeps its own glide (no click) |
| 44 45 | RSIZE RDAMP | REVERB SIZE, REVERB DAMP (REVERB LOW CUT, G_RLOCUT, is not a matrix destination, see 5g) | bus value | within the knobs' own range (every model is stable over it) |
| 46 | FMODE | MULTI FILTER MODE (the editor: FILTER MODE) | per block | 0.2.25: the multimode filter's MODE (P_FREEC: LP 0, BP 64, HP 127 and the blends); clean as it is (uf_run ramps its output weights over each block). Not a bus value, though appended after them. Works at the open CUT too (a MODE above LP runs the filter). FRES (38) is shown dim with **CUT OPEN** on ANALOG, TRIO, NOISE while their multimode filter is bypassed (CUT open, LP, no KEY TRK): a value, so still offered |

**0.2.25: matrix moves without a step (owner, the matrix round; `release_drafts/MATRIX_DESTINATIONS.md`, `tests/mx_step_test.c`).**
- **Source slew** (`mod.c` mx_slew): each slot's source value, as mod_begin reads it, moves at most 32767 / 5 a block: a jump (S&H, a square or saw LFO's edge, an LFO restarted by a note, a MIDI CC step, a new note's VEL / KEY / RAND on a per-block destination) becomes a glide of 3.6 ms (unipolar full swing) to 7.3 ms (bipolar), on every destination. An LFO or envelope that moves less a block passes unchanged: a full sine up to RATE's top (40 Hz), every synced rate but 32T above 200 BPM, an attack of 4 ms or more. A slot that was off, or whose SRC changed, starts at its value. Per-voice sources on per-voice destinations (ENV, VEL, KEY, RAND to PITCH CUT SHP AMP) are per voice and not slewed. 80 bytes of RAM, flash code.
- **In-block ramps** (`fx.c` mix_ramp): while a matrix route moves PAN, PATCH LEVEL (TRIM) or a send (CHORUS, DELAY, REVERB), and for one block after it stops, the plain mix takes the left / right gains, the level and the three sends in a straight line from the block before's values. A part without such a route mixes bit for bit as before. Not in the SPREAD mix (hot code: there they still step a block).
- **SAT's blend zone** (`fx.c` track_dist_ramp): while the followed SAT moves and either end of the block is below 12, the dry / wet blend ramps inside the block.
- Measured on a held sine, 2nd difference in 1/1000 of the peak (before -> now): CUT jump at RES 40 377 -> 26; PAN 613 -> 3; PATCH LEVEL 575 -> 17; DELAY send 385 -> 2; CHORUS send 221 -> 2; SAT into the blend zone 103 -> 23.

**0.2.25: what the DEST knob offers (owner: the hide rule applies to modulation; `docs/MOD_INERT_TABLE.md`).** The DEST knob (and the editor's list) passes:
- **removed** destinations: EQLOW EQMID EQHI, and the engine slots that are a list of choices, each change a hard switch: ANALOG WAVE, PHASE WAVE 1 / WAVE 2 / LINE, LOFI BITS / WAVE / ARP, TRIO WAVE / MODE, PHYS MODEL, NOISE MODE, FM6 ALG (WHEEL's REG and ROTR stay: the engine ramps them);
- what the current selection leaves without effect (`mod.c` mod_dst_why): the slots and the SHAPE input the current wave does not use (ANALOG SHAPE outside PWM / SYNC; LOFI SHAPE outside PLS, DUTY on TRI SAW NOIS; TRIO PW and SHAPE with no pulse, INT2 with no oscillator 2, INT3 with no oscillator 3 that sounds or rings, DTN with neither: reason **WAVE**), unused slots, WAVE's TABLE, FM6's SLOT, PAN in DUEL, the INSERT's values with TYPE OFF, the delay modulation's RATE in RANDOM / OCT UP.
- The ids stay (append-only). A stored route to any of them stays and plays (removed ones as before); the knob only does not stop on it, and turning away leaves it. The SHAPE depth cards (AMP DEST, FILTER ENV DEST, LFO 1 DEST, LFO 2 DEST) are hidden on a wave without shape input, and the cards move left.

**Bus values (the global effects).** CHORUS, DELAY and REVERB settings are global (one per instrument) while a matrix is a part's. A part's routes to them make an **offset per part**; just before the effect buses run each block, `mod_bus` writes stored value + offsets into the effect's value and `mod_bus_end` puts the stored value back after them (nothing outside the audio interrupt ever sees it). **DUEL**: each part has its own buses (A: part 1, B: part 2): part 1's offsets move bus A's value only, part 2's bus B's only; the other part is untouched, bit for bit (`mod_test`). **STEREO and LAYER**: one set of shared buses: the offsets of **both parts add** (then clamped). PERFORM's SPACE works on top of the moved values (in DUEL recomputed per bus). The DEST line says which: MOVES BUS A ONLY (PART 1) / MOVES BUS B ONLY (PART 2) / SHARED BUS: BOTH PARTS ADD. **Not a destination: the delay's TIME** (a note value: a step jumps the read point, a click every block; the delay modulation's DEPTH is the smooth wobble), COLOR and MIX (not asked for).

**Feedback.** No loop is possible: every source a per-block destination reads is the value the block before left (LFO 1 / 2, ENV, FILTER ENV, DUCK, S&H, SLEW). An LFO on its own RATE / DEPTH, FILTER ENV on its own times or DUCK on DUCK DEPTH is heard a block (0.7 ms) later, never inside the block that computes it; the morph never adds such a route (`morph_loop`).

**The MATRIX page.** The third card is labelled **DEST** (was DST). Its value is the short name (above); a destination a selection makes useless stays on the card (the card is the selector) in the dim colour, and the panel says why (UNUSED, REBUILD, LOADS, DUEL, INS OFF, ENGINE; AT NOTE until 0.2.9, for the retired SAMPLE and SLICE). Under the four slot rows the panel names the selected slot's destination in full, "DEST: WAVE SYNC", "DEST: INSERT DRIVE", "DEST: REVERB SIZE" (M), and on a second line (S) why it does nothing, or which bus a bus value moves. A row whose destination is faded is dim. The knob goes through the list grouped: OFF, PITCH CUT FRES SHP AMP, the engine's eight, LEVEL PAN, VIB RAT1 DEP1 RAT2 DEP2, FILTER ENV's six, DIST, INSERT A B C MIX, EQ LOW MID HIGH, DUCK, the sends CHO DLY REV, then the bus values. The FILTER ENV DEST, AMP DEST and LFO DEST pages have fixed depth knobs (FLT, PIT, SHP, AMP), no destination list, so nothing changed there.

**Engine change.** A slot keeps its number; it then means the new engine's slot of that index, named by the new engine (UNUSED in the panel line if that engine has none). Nothing is left modulated across the switch (every block puts the stored values back).

**MORPH.** SMART rolls the AMT of every assigned route, new destinations included (rule 3f). WILD re-rolls a route's source and destination only among 0.2.6's destinations (0..22), so a saved seed still rolls the same End B, and the morph never adds a new route to a new destination.

**Cost.** Per block only: a route to a part destination costs what a PAN route costs; the bus values a few compares per block and part when unused, a 7-value write and restore around the buses when used. Measured (`mod_test`, host instruction counts): 8 slots of every kind (SHP AMP VIB E3 LEVEL EQ MID REVERB SIZE DELAY FDBK) on a PHYS part with 4 voices: +51 instructions / sample (+3.8 %, about 1650 per 32-sample block), the effects the routes switch on not counted (EQ, insert and reverb run in both). The 4-part mix with every slot active: 817 -> 881 (+7.8 %, 0.2.6: +7.5 %), under the test's limit of 8 %.
- CPU: all new modulation is computed per block, with per-sample smoothing only where needed. Cost is measured in the emulator's instruction counts (Felucca's `tests/cpu_baseline.txt` method) and re-measured on the device.

### 2.5 Multimode filter (owner's request; the universal filter built 2026-10-12, DUELL 0.2.2; multimode since 0.2.9; on every engine since 0.2.9 "FILTER")
Some engines have a real filter with a cutoff, some do not. The ones that do not get **one per-voice multimode filter** (0.2.2 .. 0.2.8: the "universal filter", one bipolar CUT) in place of one; the ones that do (ANALOG, TRIO, NOISE) keep their own, unchanged, **and since 0.2.9 get the same multimode filter in series after it** (owner, the cheap option: "FILTER", bypassed by default, so every sound of before is bit for bit the same). So now every engine that makes sound can take the multimode filter.

**Step 1: which engine has a filter of its own (read from the engine code).** A filter of its own is a section in the voice with a cutoff control that the generic cutoff routes (ENV 1 / FILTER ENV / LFO 1 / LFO 2 FLT, the matrix's CUT, CC74) already drive.

| Engine | Filter of its own? | Reason (eng_*.c) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ANALOG | **yes** (+ the multimode filter in series, `ufilt` 2) | trapezoidal state-variable filter, TYPE LP / BP / HP, CUT, RES, KTR (the VCF page) |
| TRIO | **yes** (+ the multimode filter in series, `ufilt` 2) | state-variable filter, MODE LP / BP / HP / notch, CUT, RES |
| NOISE | **yes** (+ the multimode filter in series, `ufilt` 2) | resonant state-variable filter FREQ / RES (low-pass to band-pass) |
| PHASE | no | DCW is the phase-distortion depth (a waveshaper), no filter section |
| LOFI | no | TONE is a one-pole tone control after the crusher |
| VOICE | no | the formant resonators (F1..F4) are the vowel, not a cutoff; the FLT routes used to move the vowel |
| WHEEL | no | tonewheel organ, nothing that reads a cutoff at all |
| PHYS | no | BRIT is the model's exciter tone and loop damping, a physical parameter, not a cutoff |
| FM6 | no | MLVL is a modulator level (brightness by FM), no filter |
| WAVE (0.2.5) | no | a wavetable read; POS is a place in the table, not a cutoff (section 2.6) |
| (DIGITAL, DRUM) | retired (code removed in 0.2.14) | unchanged |
| (SAMPLE, GRAIN, SLICE) | retired (0.2.9) | gone with their engines (SAMPLE had its own one-pole CUT; GRAIN and SLICE had the universal filter) |

**Step 2: the multimode filter** on PHASE, LOFI, VOICE, WHEEL, PHYS, FM6 and WAVE in place of a filter (`engine_t.ufilt` 1; voice.c `uf_run`, `uf_voice`; GRAIN and SLICE had it until 0.2.9 retired them), and on ANALOG, TRIO and NOISE **in series after their own filter** (`ufilt` 2, below): all ten engines. Elektron-style (owner, 0.2.9): the DJ sweep (one knob, low-pass left, high-pass right, OFF in the middle) is PERFORM's K3 FILTER (section 5e); EDIT > FILTER is a multimode filter with four knobs.
- **Parameters** (ids 45..48, the old free / SLICER ids; still 127 parameters): **CUT** (`P_FREEA`, F_FILT, 0..82, default 82) the cutoff, shown in Hz / kHz: dial step d is table index (40 + 396 d) / 256 of the engines' cutoff table (`SVF_G`, 30 Hz .. 16 kHz over 127), so 0 is 30 Hz and 82 16 kHz, **the open end**; every step is 0.2.2's step (1.55 table steps, 0.11 octave), which keeps every low-pass of before exact (below). **RES** (`P_FREEB`, 0..127, default 0): the damping from 1.41 (Q 0.71) to 0.145 (Q 6.9) at 12 dB, as before. **MODE** (`P_FREEC`, F_FMODE, 0..127, default 0): 0 LP, 64 BP, 127 HP and every blend between, shown LP, LP>BP, BP, BP>HP, HP. **SLOPE** (`P_FREED`, enum 1..2, default 1): 12dB / 24dB per octave; **never 0** (0 marks a store of before, see Old stores). All four are the sound's (a sound load sets them).
- **The filter:** the trapezoidal state-variable filter of dsp.c (ANALOG's and TRIO's: unconditionally stable), whose low-pass, band-pass (normalised: `k v1`, unity at the cutoff) and high-pass outputs are summed with MODE's weights: LP (1 - m/64) + BP m/64 below the middle, BP (127 - m)/63 + HP (m - 64)/63 above it (Q10; written as `cx x + cb bp + cl lp`). The weights are worked out per voice and block and **ramp over the block** from the last block's (exactly onto the new ones at its end), so turning MODE (even a jump LP <-> HP between two blocks) and a route through a kill zone never click. **24 dB:** a second stage of the same MODE on the first's output (its input clamped to +-65536), the two dampings a 4th-order Butterworth's (1.848 fixed, 0.765 narrowed by RES to 0.104: `UF_K24A`, `UF_K24B`), so its peak at RES 127 matches the 12 dB one (+13 dB at the cutoff). The second stage starts charged with the first's low-pass state; turning SLOPE while a note sounds fades the second stage in (out) over one block. The integrators clamp at +-150000, the output goes through a soft knee at the voice's full level (48000), on the voice after its amplitude, in a scratch block added to the part's (as before). A steady low-pass (MODE 0, no ramp) runs 0.2.2's loop (its output is the low-pass itself: the same cost as before).
- **Kill zones:** over the last 4 dial steps at an end (the effective cut, routes included) the shares that end closes fade to 0: low-pass and band-pass towards 30 Hz, high-pass and band-pass towards 16 kHz. **A fully closed filter is silent** (LP / BP at CUT 0, HP / BP at CUT 82: the output exactly 0); a high-pass at CUT 0 is open (30 Hz).
