- **The effective cut** of a voice, per block, is CUT plus every modulation: ENV 1 FLT (AMP DEST), FILTER ENV FLT, LFO 1 FLT, LFO 2 FLT and the matrix destination CUT (any source incl. aftertouch), in the units they had (1/256 dial steps; 63 steps = 0.2.2's whole dial), clamped to 0..82. The accent's built-in lift stays the engine's; PHASE DCW, LOFI TONE, FM6 MLVL, PHYS BRIT and VOICE's vowel are the engines' own controls, not cutoff routes. The matrix's FRES moves RES (per block).
- **BYPASS (exact rule): MODE LP (0) and the effective cut at the open end (82).** RES and SLOPE do not matter there. Then the voice renders straight into the part's buffer as always: no work, bit for bit. These are the defaults, so every sound of before plays bit for bit (the goldens are unchanged). A route that only opens (a positive depth at CUT 82) keeps it bypassed; one that closes it runs it from rest (the low-pass charged with the block's first sample: no step), next to the open end it is transparent (CUT 81, 14.8 kHz). Any MODE other than LP runs the filter at every CUT (MODE HP at CUT 82 is the closed high-pass: silent).
- **Measured** (`uf_test.c`, a sine through `uf_run`, RES 0; UF_TABLE=1 prints RES 80 % too): 12 dB LP corners 35 Hz (CUT 4) .. 3.4 kHz (CUT 62) at -12 dB an octave (-18 near 22 kHz, the bilinear warp); 24 dB -24 / octave, 1 kHz -91 dB at CUT 4 (41 Hz), the stop band under -90 dB (the fixed point's floor); HP at CUT 78 (11.8 kHz): 2 kHz -36 dB (12 dB), -71 dB (24 dB); BP at CUT 41 (690 Hz): 0 dB at the cutoff, 50 Hz -20 dB, 10 kHz -22 dB (24 dB: -43 / -47); the blends LP>BP / BP>HP between the two (the halfway blend is 6 dB down in the band the other end removes: a plain crossfade).
- **UI:** EDIT > MULTI FILTER 1/2 (0.2.21: after EDIT 2 and after the engine's own filter page; 2/2 is KEY TRK and two cutoff amounts, below): K1 CUT, K2 RES, K3 MODE, K4 SLOPE, only on the `ufilt` 1 engines; ANALOG keeps its own VCF page. A part page.
- **In series on ANALOG, TRIO, NOISE (`ufilt` 2, 0.2.9; owner: the cheap option).** The engine renders as ever (its own filter, its KTR / TYPE, the accent's lift, every route) into a scratch block, and the multimode filter above runs on that, per voice, after the amplitude, exactly as on the other engines (`uf_voice`). The same ids 45..48, the same four knobs and displays, on **the page FILTER, the same name on every engine** (owner, 2026-10-14: never "FILTER"). *Names:* the three engines' own filters got pages of their own with distinct names (titles are 11 characters at most, ui_graph.c `page_parts`): **VCF** (ANALOG: TYPE CUT RES KTR, the former "FILTER" page), **TRIO FILTER** (MODE CUT RES), **NOISE FILT** (FREQ RES); the filters themselves are not changed. EDIT's round (0.2.21, signal order): ENGINE, EDIT 1, EDIT 2, the engine's own filter page, then MULTI FILTER 1/2 and 2/2 (the multimode one, which runs after it), then VOICE 1/2, VOICE 2/2. *Bypass:* the same rule (MODE LP and CUT at the open end 82, the defaults), so a sound of before renders bit for bit (the goldens and the 114-sound audit are unchanged), and costs nothing while bypassed. *Routes (unchanged):* ENV 1 / FILTER ENV / LFO 1 / LFO 2 FLT, the matrix's CUT, the accent's lift and CC74 / CC71 keep driving **the engine's own filter** (`MIDI_CC_ENG` untouched); they never move or switch on the multimode filter, whose effective cut is its CUT knob alone (so no route can close it from the open end). *Reached by the matrix:* **FRES** (destination 38, the multimode filter's RES) is live there and moves the multimode filter's RES (it was shown faded ENGINE on these three because RES was an engine knob; the DEST line reads "MULTI FILTER RESONANCE", FILTER being the multimode page; it stays faded on an engine with no multimode filter, DRUM). There is **no matrix destination for the multimode CUT** (the destination CUT is the engine's own cutoff, and adding one would grow the stored destination list); it is reached by the knob, MIDI LEARN (any id), and automation of the knob move. *No name collides:* no destination name was added (N_MDST is unchanged; "CUT" and "FRES" were there, and the engine's own RES / CUT are E slots named by their labels as before); the ids 45..48 are no engine's (ANALOG's TYPE is `P_FTYPE`, the rest E slots P_E0..); mod_test's rule that no destination name equals an engine knob label holds. *Morph:* as for every engine (below). The cost while it runs is the same per voice (below); at 8 voices ANALOG's 12 dB low-pass adds +46.8 per voice and sample, 24 dB +77.4, as on PHASE. **Nothing is faded:** no value of these four is impossible by a selection, and a zero is not a reason (the FLT depth cards and RES are live too).
- **MIDI:** CC74 is CUT, 0..127 over the whole dial (0 30 Hz, 127 the open end; 0.2.2's 64 = OFF is gone), CC71 RES; a MIDI LEARN map on CUT spans the new dial the same way. Recorded as knob moves (automation takes CUT and RES, not MODE or SLOPE).
- **Morph (owner, 2026-10-14: the filter is fully rolled):** on **every engine** with the multimode filter, in SMART **and** WILD, **CUT, RES and MODE roll** like continuous values (AMOUNT % of their range) and **SLOPE** like a choice (12 / 24 dB flips with AMOUNT % chance); all four from their own stream rs3 (CUT and RES draw as before, then MODE, then SLOPE; the old draws on the main stream stay, so no other value of a saved End B seed moves). A filter that is **bypassed may be switched on** by SMART (explicit override of rule 3g for this filter; since 0.2.11 with **half the chance**, rule 3g). The fader **cross-fades SLOPE** (it is a choice with the other end's value in `mxf` slot `MX_SLOPE`; `uf_run` blends the two slopes in the first stage's damping and the second stage's share, 0 .. 32, so no cliff at the middle). A FLT depth at 0 still stays 0 on a `ufilt` 1 engine while the original's filter is bypassed (SMART; a route is not a way to switch it on). **Every result is kept audible** (`morph_uf_safe`, SMART and WILD alike) by the shares of the rolled MODE (low-pass, band-pass, high-pass, in 1/64; a share of a quarter or more counts): a band-pass share puts CUT in 36 .. 50 (~0.7 .. 2 kHz) with SLOPE 12 dB and RES at most 40 (or the original's); a low-pass share needs CUT 45 or above (~1.3 kHz); a high-pass share alone CUT 25 or below (~210 Hz), with a band-pass share too CUT 24 .. 30; RES never above max(original, 60). So a roll never lands in a kill zone, at a closed end or in a thin resonant slice, and the level stays within ~26 dB of the original's on the six test engines (`morph_test`: worst 500-roll minimum x0.05). The original's own CUT is kept where it is already beyond and its MODE is within 8. A CUT forced beyond AMOUNT % of its range pulls MODE back toward the original's until it fits. On ANALOG, TRIO and NOISE the same, for the multimode filter; their own filter's rules are unchanged.
- **Old stores (0.2.2 .. 0.2.8):** 45 was a bipolar CUT (0 OFF, -64..-1 a low-pass, 1..63 a high-pass), 47 and 48 free (0). Since SLOPE is never 0 now, **SLOPE 0 (or absent) marks a store of before**, and `uf_legacy` (core.h) converts it on load, before any value is fitted: OFF -> CUT 82, MODE LP (the bypass); a low-pass c -> CUT 82 + c, MODE LP: **the same cutoff exactly** (index 40 + 396 (82 + c) = 32512 + 396 c, 0.2.2's formula; `uf_test` checks every effective value and runs 0.2.8's loop beside today's bit for bit); a high-pass c -> CUT c, MODE HP, 0.16 of a table step higher (0.01 octave: 1 % of the signal at RES 0, 7 % at RES 127 where the peak moves with it); SLOPE 12 dB. Where: patch records (`up_values`; the patch store's default for CUT and SLOPE is 0, `up_def`, so a record of before reads them as 0 and every record of today stores SLOPE: 4 bytes more), projects, multis (the factory multis M01..M04 are such records), the autosave and a backup's project (`proj_uf` in `proj_bound` and `project_restore_runtime`), the editor's `UP_PUT`. A track's CUT automation converts with it: on a low-pass track a low-pass value at the same cutoff, OFF and a high-pass value the open end; on a high-pass track a high-pass value the same, OFF and a low-pass value 0 (MODE is not automated). A store of Felucca's layout (the SLICER's values on 45..48) reads the defaults (`param_retired`): the bypass. **On ANALOG, TRIO and NOISE (`ufilt` 2) nothing was meant by 45..48 before 0.2.9, but a store may still hold a leftover there (an engine switch keeps the values): `uf_legacy(p, serial)` therefore gives such a store of before the bypass whatever 45..48 hold (CUT 82, RES 0, MODE LP, SLOPE 12 dB) instead of converting them, so no old sound is filtered by a leftover; its CUT automation goes to the open end (`proj_uf`; the editor's `ufLegacy(p, serial)`). A record of today (SLOPE stored) is kept as it is on every engine.** Factory sounds are written in today's form (tools/duell_sounds.py: EVOLVING PAD 62, REESE BASS 63, HOOVER 74, DIGITAL PAD 70, BREATH PAD 54: their 0.2.2 values -20 -19 -8 -12 -28, the same cutoffs; PURPLE FLUTE 54 and LICORICE 65, two of 0.2.9's replacement sounds, made on the old dial as -28 and -17, the same cutoffs).
- **Bass is kept at high RES: measured** (`uf_test.c` t_bass; no change to the filter). Unlike the Moog ladder, this state-variable filter has no feedback of the low-pass output into the input, so RES raises only the peak at the cutoff, never lowers the passband. Sines at 40 / 60 / 100 / 200 Hz, MODE LP, RES 0 against 64 / 100 / 127, CUT 100 / 200 / 500 / 1000 / 4000 Hz, 12 and 24 dB: no tone ever loses level; a tone 2.8 times or more under the cutoff changes by at most 0.88 dB (a small lift, never a loss), and with the cutoff at 1 kHz or more the 40..200 Hz tones are within 0.4 dB. The resonant peak is +2.9 dB (RES 64), +7.8 (100), +13.5 dB (127) over the passband, 12 and 24 dB alike. The soft knee (48000) bounds the output: a full-level saw (+-24000) at a 100 Hz cutoff, RES 127, peaks at 59144 at worst (+7.8 dB; 82 Hz, 12 dB) and a +-48000 saw at 63794 at most, never over 65536.
- **Cost** (the repo's model: host instructions per sample, 33 = 1 % of the device's half; `uf_test.c`, 8 voices): PHASE bypass 866; 12 dB low-pass +46.6 per voice and sample (0.2.8: +44), a blend +58.6, 24 dB low-pass +77.3, 24 dB high-pass +104 (the uf_voice scratch clear and add included). So at 8 voices: 12 dB LP ~11 % of the device as before, a blend ~14 %, 24 dB LP ~19 %, 24 dB HP ~25 %; only while the filter runs, the bypass costs nothing. The second stage alone is +31 per voice and sample at LP (+7.4 % at 8 voices), more than the prototype's 12 (it also mixes MODE, ramps and fades). Flash (no device build here; a Thumb-2 -Os build of the functions as a proxy): `uf_run` + `uf_coef` about 1.06 KB against 0.2.8's 0.41 KB, about +0.65 KB, and about +0.35 KB elsewhere (the conversion, the formats, morph, PERFORM's kill zone). **The multimode filter on ANALOG, TRIO, NOISE, flash: about +0.2 KB** (estimate, no device build here: the series branch in `track_render`, the extra `serial` argument of `uf_legacy` and its three callers, the open-end rule for a leftover CUT lock in `proj_uf`, the page entry and its visibility test, three page entries; `uf_run` and `uf_voice` are shared, not copied). RAM: nothing new (`voice_t.uf` etc. exist for every voice). Stable at RES 127 across the dial, every MODE and SLOPE (loud chords: the part's peak at most 2.4x the unfiltered one, no growth).
- **KEY TRK, the multimode filter follows the note (owner, 2026-10-16; DUELL 0.2.21).** The effective cut of each voice also moves with the played note: **KTRK -100 .. +100 %**, default **32 (+51 %) for a new or INIT sound since 0.2.22** (owner: 50 %; 50 is between the steps 31 = 49 % and 32 = 51 %); the factory sounds too, except the BASS, PERC and kick ones, which are set to **0** (`tools/gen_duell_sounds.py`; the stored default of a patch record stays 0, `upreset.c up_def`), per part, part of the sound (patch records, multis, projects, morph). At +100 % the cutoff moves **one octave per octave** of the note's pitch, around **MIDI note 60** (pitch16 960: the centre of ANALOG's own KTR, `eng_analog.c`, whose curve it mirrors: linear in pitch); at -100 % the other way; the units: 63 stored steps = 100 % (-63 .. +63, shown with `F_KTRK`: +100 %, +51 % at 32).
  - *Where:* `voice.c track_render`, per voice and block, before the clamp that gives the filter its cut: `kt = UF_KT(KTRK, pitch_cur)` (core.h: `KTRK x (pitch16 - 960) x 785 >> 12` in Q8 dial steps; one octave is 2320 of them; 785/4096 is 0.08 % short of exact) is added to the dial and the routes (ufilt 1) or to the dial alone (ANALOG, TRIO, NOISE, where the multimode filter is in series after the engine's own: KTR there stays ANALOG's). `pitch_cur` is the note with glide, so a legato or glided note takes the cutoff along with the pitch; bend, vibrato and the matrix's PITCH do not move it. Nothing in `uf_run` changed (the coefficient is worked out every block anyway; no click on a turn, measured).
  - *Exact at 0:* the multiply is not done at 0 and nothing else changes, so every sound of today renders bit for bit (the goldens, `mrender` and the 114-sound audit are unchanged). With **CUT at the open end and KTRK above 0 the notes below C3 close** (the effective cut falls under 82, the filter runs for them; notes above stay in the bypass): that is key tracking, and it is the only way the new value can wake a filter (a sound load sets KTRK 0).
  - *Cost* (`uf_test`, PHASE, 8 voices, the model: 33 host instructions per sample = 1 % of the device's half): per voice and block one load, a branch, two multiplies, a shift and an add while KTRK is not 0 (about 8 instructions: 64 per block of 32 samples at 8 voices, 2 per sample, 0.06 % of the device); nothing at 0 (the multiply is skipped). The run-to-run difference of two measured runs (CUT 52, KTRK 0 against +63) is -0.08 instructions per sample, inside the counter's noise. `uf_run` and `uf_voice` (hot, in RAM) are untouched: the fast RAM block is the same 12,348 B, 164 B free, `elfcheck` ok; the package is 610,086 B as before.
  - *Id:* **27, the retired `P_QUANT`'s** (QNT; the owner's design note; the earlier build had already dropped it: nothing read it, every store wrote its default 0, `project.c` reset it on load). Now `P_KTRK`; `P_COUNT` stays 127, the formats and the protocol do not change size. `upreset.c` and `project.c` no longer write / reset it; `ui.c param_kept` no longer counts it among the player's settings (ids 17..32 are the arp, keys and pattern), so a sound load sets it (to 0 on a factory sound) and a patch keeps it. Only a stored sound or multi saved while QNT still existed (saved before 2026-10-12; the OFF / SNAP / WHITE / SEQ values 1..3) can hold a non-zero value there: it now reads as +2 .. +5 % key tracking, 0.03 octave per octave at most (nothing was done about it: no migration, per the no-backcompat-before-alpha rule); a record of 0.2.14 .. 0.2.20 holds 0. Automatable and learnable like CUT and RES (`motion_param`; MIDI LEARN takes any id below `P_E0`; not CC74 / CC71, which stay CUT / RES).
  - *Morph:* KTRK rolls with the filter family (`morph.c`): only on the `ufilt` engines, SMART leaves a 0 alone (it never switches key tracking on), one that is set rolls by the one % at most half the range from the original's, WILD also rolls it from 0; its draws are a stream of their own (`rs5`), so no other value of a saved End B moves; an End B whose filter is bypassed (MODE LP, CUT open) keeps the original's KTRK ("MORPH never switches the filter on by itself"). The fader cross-fades it like CUT.
  - *Pages (signal order):* the engine's **own** filter page (VCF on ANALOG, TRIO FILTER, NOISE FILT) comes **before** the multimode one, which runs after it: EDIT's round is ENGINE, EDIT 1, EDIT 2, [VCF | TRIO FILTER | NOISE FILT], **MULTI FILTER 1/2** (K1 CUT, K2 RES, K3 MODE, K4 SLOPE, unchanged), **MULTI FILTER 2/2** (K1 **KTRK**, K2 **FILTER ENV**, K3 **LFO 1**, K4 **LFO 2**; 0.2.22), [OP TUNE, OP ENV, OP MODE on FM6], VOICE 1/2, VOICE 2/2. QUICK MULTI stays CUT, RES, MODE, REL. K4 (0.2.22, owner) is LFO 2's cutoff depth `P_L2FLT` (there is no AMP ENV card: envelope 1 is the amp envelope and the filter takes no default depth from it; AMP DEST keeps its own FLT card). The stored cutoff depths with an id of their own are four: ENV 1 (AMP DEST's FLT, `P_ED_FLT`), FILTER ENV (`P_E2FLT`), LFO 1 (`P_LD_FLT`) and LFO 2 (`P_L2FLT`, LFO 2 DEST); the others (velocity, aftertouch, mod wheel) are matrix routes (SRC, DST CUT) with no id of their own. LFO 2 is on this page (K4) and on LFO 2 DEST.
  - *FILTER ENV / LFO 1 / LFO 2 -> cutoff: one stored value, at both places (0.2.22, owner: a depth connects two modules, so it is reachable at both; this replaces 0.2.21's "one card per value").* K2, K3 and K4 of 2/2 are the same ids as the DEST pages' FLT cards (`P_E2FLT`, `P_LD_FLT`, `P_L2FLT`; only the card names differ here: FILTER ENV, LFO 1, LFO 2; the editor says FILTER ENV > CUT, LFO 1 > CUT, LFO 2 > CUT). The DEST pages (FILTER ENV DEST, LFO 1 DEST, LFO 2 DEST, and AMP DEST for its own) draw their FLT cards on every engine. Where those routes drive the multimode filter (the `ufilt` 1 engines) the three cards (FILTER ENV, LFO 1, LFO 2) are on **MULTI FILTER 2/2** too (`ui_draw.c card_hidden_vp`); on ANALOG, TRIO, NOISE (`ufilt` 2) the routes drive the engine's own filter, so there they are on the DEST pages only and 2/2 shows KTRK alone. QUICK LFO's K2 is still LFO 1's FLT (a hold layer is a shortcut, not a page). The editor: the ENGINE tab's MULTI FILTER card (after the engine's own filter) holds KTRK and the three amounts (FILTER ENV > CUT, LFO 1 > CUT, LFO 2 > CUT) on the `ufilt` 1 engines, and the MOD tab's cards keep them too.
  - *Tests:* `uf_test` (a sine at the notes 36 .. 84 through the 12 dB low-pass: the cutoff read from the level follows the note within 0.1 octave at +100 / +50 / -100 %, 0 not at all; the same level at 48, 60, 72 at +100 %; the bypass kept at 0 and at the open end above C3; no click while it is turned; the card, hide and page tests; morph), `select_test`, `ui_test`, `engine_page_test`, `test_editor`, `test_layout`, the browser test of the ENGINE and MOD tabs.
- **History:** 0.2.2 built it as one bipolar CUT (centre OFF, a low-pass from 16 kHz to 120 Hz to the left, a high-pass from 30 Hz to 3.8 kHz to the right, 12 dB) and RES; 0.2.5 let the routes work with CUT at OFF (a negative sum a low-pass, a positive one a high-pass). 0.2.9 measured that it did not close (full left a 120 Hz 12 dB low-pass, A2 only -3 dB; full right a 3.7 kHz high-pass, the highs untouched) and made it this multimode filter; the DJ sweep moved to PERFORM's FILTER, whose ends now close too.
- **Sounds:** the 97 factory sounds of 0.2.2 were not retuned then (the 46 on these engines whose renders changed are listed in CHANGELOG 0.2.2). 0.2.9 changes no factory render: the goldens and every End A level of the sound audit are as before (the End B of 40 sounds, which now carry a rolled filter, had a seed re-picked: tools/duell_sounds.py `bseed`; CROONER and SMOKE RING also a smaller AMOUNT, 11, since no seed of 600 kept their lead level at 17); the End B of the five sounds with a CUT set rolls CUT within the new range (82 steps), and where a random End B used to cross OFF into a high-pass (HOOVER, BREATH PAD, EVOLVING PAD) it now stays a low-pass.

### 2.6 WAVE: the wavetable engine (DUELL 0.2.5, built 2026-10-13)
A new engine, appended: **engine 14** (NENGINES 15; 13 is SLICE's reserved place since 0.2.9, so WAVE is 14 in every store), shown **last** in the engine order (PATCHES > TYPE keeps an engine filter as its place in that order; 0.2.9 took SAMPLE, GRAIN and SLICE out of it, so a TYPE filter saved on an engine after LOFI may show another engine, or ALL, once after the update). Our own code (`eng_wave.c`; nothing from Melodee or Plaits).

**Controls** (EDIT 1 "TABLE": TABLE POS BEND SYNC; EDIT 2 "OSC": SUB DTN and two unused; HOME: POS BEND SYNC REL)
| E | Label | Range | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | TABLE | 29 names (26 factory, 3 user: USR1..USR3) | which table (below); a change builds the new table (see "the build") |
| 1 | POS | 0..127 (%) | the place in the table's scan, first frame to last; two neighbouring frames are mixed |
| 2 | BEND | 0..127 (%) | a warp of the read: the cycle's first half squeezed, read up to 10 x as fast (even steps of pitch), the second half stretched: a pulse width for any wave. 0 = none |
| 3 | SYNC | 0..127 (%) | hard sync: the read restarts at every cycle of the note and runs 1 .. 8 x as fast (three octaves, even steps of pitch). 0 = none |
| 4 | SUB | 0..127 (%) | a sine an octave below |
| 5 | DTN | 0..127 ct | a second read DTN cents up, mixed half and half (with its own SYNC). 0 = one read |
| 6, 7 | - | | unused |

**What moves POS (the decision).** WAVE has no filter of its own, so, as on the other universal-filter engines (2.5), the cutoff routes (ENV 1 / FILTER ENV / LFO 1 / LFO 2 FLT, the matrix's CUT, CC74) drive the multimode filter, and CUT RES MODE SLOPE are on EDIT > FILTER. POS is moved by the **SHP routes**: AMP DEST SHP, FILTER ENV DEST SHP, LFO 1 / LFO 2 DEST SHP and the matrix's SHP, as an offset on POS (63 steps = the whole scan, the centre = the knob as set), per voice, ramped over each block (no steps). This is the convention the engines already follow (SHP is each engine's timbre control: PHASE's DCW, LOFI's pulse width, 2.4). Per-block matrix routes to E2..E6 (POS, BEND, SYNC, SUB, DTN) work as on any engine (SYNC LEAD uses FILTER ENV -> SYNC; the MATRIX page names them "DEST: WAVE SYNC"). A route to E1 (TABLE) does nothing since 0.2.7 and the page shows it faded REBUILD (before, each change of the value meant a new build, a moment of silence: not a modulation); E7 is STEPS since cloud job F (OFF, 2 to 19: the scan's position rounded to that many places, eng_wave.c wave_steps; stored 0 = OFF, n = n + 1 places; table 24 is named STAIR now) and works as a matrix destination; E8 stays UNUSED (2.4a). The accent's built-in lift (velocity above 110) has no tone control to open on WAVE.

**The tables** (19 frames each; names of at most 5 letters, what a card shows; POS 0 -> 127)
| # | Name | Recipe |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | SAWSQ | saw -> square: the even harmonics fade out |
| 1 | PWM | pulse width 50 % -> 5 % (the pulse's harmonic levels sin(pi k w) / k) |
| 2 | HARM | sine -> saw: the harmonics come in one by one, their count 1 -> 64 in even steps of the octave (POS works like a filter's cutoff) |
| 3 | ODD | sine -> square: the odd harmonics one by one (1 -> 32 of them): hollow, clarinet-like |
| 4 | ORGAN | drawbar registrations 8' / 8' 4' / 8' 4' 2 2/3' / 8' 4' 2 2/3' 2' / all seven (8' 4' 2 2/3' 2' 1 3/5' 1 1/3' 1'), each drawbar step -3 dB; the 16' is SUB |
| 5 | VOWEL | A -> E -> I -> O -> U: three formants of an adult male voice (Peterson and Barney's averages), gliding between the vowels; placed for C3, so they move with the note as on any wavetable |
| 6 | BRASS | the harmonics grow in as a brass tone's with its loudness (K^2 / (K^2 + k^2), K 1.5 -> 12) |
| 7 | BOWED | a bowed string: the Helmholtz saw with the bow-position comb (sin(pi k b) / k), the bow from 1/4 to 1/16 of the string: from soft to bright and nasal |
| 8 | RESO | a saw through a resonant low-pass (Q 6) swept from harmonic 2 to 64 |
| 9 | COMB | a saw through a comb (1 + 0.9 cos), its notches every 3 -> 24 harmonics: a slow flanging colour |
| 10 | DIGI | FM 1:1, the index 0 -> 7: a sine to a bright DX-like tone |
| 11 | GLASS | FM 1:7, the index 0.2 -> 3.2: sparse high partials |
| 12 | CHIME | the modes of a free bar (a chime tube) 1 : 2.76 : 5.40 : 8.93 .. rounded to harmonics 1 3 5 9 13 19 25 32 40 49 (a single cycle cannot hold inharmonic ratios), from soft to bright |
| 13 | ROUGH | saw -> fixed random harmonic levels and signs (the same draw on every build): rough, raspy |
| 14 | BAND | (0.2.7) a band of a few harmonics (a raised cosine, at least 1.5 harmonics to each side) moving up from harmonic 2 to 16 over a soft fundamental: the thin, singing sweep of the classic digital wavetables (in the spirit of the PPG Wave's resonant tables) |
| 15 | DUAL | (0.2.7) a saw through two resonant peaks, one rising from harmonic 2 to 32 while the other falls from 32 to 2, crossing in the middle (in the spirit of the dual-filter wavetables) |
| 16 | THIN | (0.2.7) a saw through a 24 dB high shelf (the lows down by up to 18 dB) whose corner sweeps from harmonic 1 to 5.7: full to thin and buzzy; a high-pass sweep that keeps a little body, so high notes do not vanish |
| 17 | PIPE | (0.2.7) a pipe organ's ranks drawn one by one: 8' 4' 2 2/3' 2', then the mixture 1 1/3' 1' 2/3' 1/2' 1/3' 1/4' (harmonics 1 2 3 4 6 8 12 16 24 32), each rank a little softer and with its own overtones (1, 1/4, 1/9): a church plenum, brighter than ORGAN |
| 18 | REESE | (0.2.7) three saws, the outer two drifting in phase against the middle one (1 + 2 cos(k d), d 0 -> half a cycle): what detuned saws pass through in half a beat; with DTN and a slow LFO on POS a Reese bass |
| 19 | HOOVR | (0.2.7) three saw-and-pulse waves an octave apart (16' 8' 4' at 1, 0.75, 0.5, locked, no detune), the pulse width 50 % -> 10 %: a fast LFO on POS is the hoover's pulse-width sweep |
| 20 | GROWL | (0.2.7) a saw under a man's vowels U -> short U -> AW -> AH -> AE (Peterson and Barney's averages), strong narrow peaks, placed for C2: the "oo-wah" of a growl bass |
| 21 | WOBBL | (0.2.7) a saw through a 24 dB resonant low-pass (two Q 3 stages), its cutoff from harmonic 1.4 to 11: the wobble bass's filter along the scan (an LFO on POS wobbles it) |
| 22 | YELL | (0.2.7) a saw under AH -> AE -> EH -> short I -> EE with a strong fixed peak at 3 kHz (the singer's formant), placed for C3: a bright, nasal scream for leads |
| 23 | METAL | (0.2.7) the fundamental (the hum) and the first eight modes of a struck circular membrane (1 : 1.59 : 2.14 : 2.30 : 2.65 : 2.92 : 3.16 : 3.50), 2 -> 8 harmonics apart along the scan; each mode is shared between its two nearest harmonics (clusters: the roughness of a metal plate) |
| 24 | STEPS | (0.2.7) a sine held in S steps a cycle, S 64 -> 3 (one value a frame): the harmonics n S +- 1 at (-1)^n / k, the stepped, aliased sound of a low sample rate, made of harmonics (so still band-limited) |
| 25 | AIR | (0.2.7) a soft flute (harmonics 1, 1/5, 1/10) and a breath of random harmonics from 4 up (a fixed draw, (4 / k)^1/2) growing from 0.05 to 0.6: airy and hollow; a pitched hiss, not true noise |

Append-only: TABLE 0..13 are byte for byte the tables of 0.2.5 / 0.2.6 (`tests/wave_test.c` checks their fingerprint), so a stored sound keeps its table; 14..25 came in 0.2.7. Every new table is a recipe like the others (no stored waves, nothing copied from another instrument; the classic ones are only in the spirit of the 1980s digital wavetable synths).

**User tables (DUELL 0.2.14, owner decision 2026-10-15: "first, three user wavetables; then the rest of the free flash for lifting the patch limit").** TABLE 26..28 are three user slots (`WV_NTF` 26 factory, `WV_NU` 3). A slot is the recipe's own source data: for each of the 19 frames, the levels of the harmonics 1..64, **one byte each** (`wt_obj_t` in `eng_wave.c`: 1228 bytes = a 12 byte header (version 1, 19, 64, flags 0, an 8 character name) + 19 x 64 bytes). The byte is a log amplitude relative to the frame's strongest harmonic: 0 off, `b` >= 1 gives 2^(-(255 - b) / 16) (`wt_q16`: a 16 entry Q16 table, `WV_AMP`, and a shift), 255 = 1.0, 16 steps an octave (0.376 dB, at most 0.19 dB error), -95 dB at the bottom (the engine's own levels are 10 bit, so nothing under -66 dB is heard). Why log: the factory recipes are linear levels like 1 / k; 8 bits of linear level would give a saw's 64th harmonic 4 steps. **The build is the factory one:** `wv_frame` takes the Q16 levels from `wv_user` instead of `wv_recipe` and goes on unchanged (the largest level to 1023, the sums, the gain to `WV_RMS`, the peak at most 127), so everything after it, and the factory tables' bytes, are as they were (the 14 older tables' fingerprint and the 174 golden renders are unchanged). Every frame is built to the same loudness whatever the file's levels were. **An EMPTY slot** (no table stored, or one that fails its checks) builds a sine in every frame and is named EMPTY. **Names:** `N_WV_TABLE[26..28]` point at `wv_uname[3][9]`, set by `wt_names` (the user's name, up to 8 characters; `USRn` if it has none; `EMPTY`); a card shows five. **A slot written again:** `wt_changed` counts the slot's version (`wv_uver`) and drops a build in progress at once (main loop); a part that has the table built compares its build's version (`wv_bver`) in `wave_block` and fades out (`WV_FADE` blocks), drops and builds anew. **Storage** (`wtab.c`, `storage.c` OBJ_WTAB0..2): an A/B pair of sectors per slot at 0xA0000..0xA5FFF, the commit record's CRC-32 over the payload, `wt_obj_ok` (size, version 1, 19, 64, flags 0, printable name, some byte nonzero) at load and before a write; the slots are read into the RAM mirror `wt_ram` (3 x 1228 bytes, `.pool`) at boot; an object that fails is EMPTY. The region is outside the app image and inside the update loader's keep-out store [0x97000, 0xE0000), so an update keeps the tables; ERASE DATA wipes them (after the patch store, before the settings). **Written only by the editor** (`editor_wtab.c`: WT_PUT, and the backup PUT of ids 24..26), through `ed_flash_stop` (the sequencer is stopped; rc 3 if it does not stop in 100 ms: nothing is written while it plays) and `st_save` (the flash duck over the erase, the payload and the commit record, the commit record last). Nothing on the unit writes them. **Phases are not kept** (every harmonic of a user table is a sine) and **a user table has 19 frames and 64 harmonics**: the converter (`web/editor/wavetable.js`) reduces a file to that and says what it lost. A factory recipe whose harmonics have negative signs (PWM, BAND ...) converted back loses those signs (same spectrum, another shape). The converter: WAV (PCM 8/16/24/32, float, extensible, mono mix) -> frames (256..4096 or any size >= 130, the Serum `clm ` chunk or a whole number of 2048 frames) -> 19 frames picked at f (N - 1) / 18, magnitudes interpolated between neighbours -> the 64 harmonics' peak amplitudes (radix-2 FFT, a direct DFT for other sizes) -> each frame scaled to its strongest -> bytes (under -66 dB, or the chosen floor: 0; a frame 80 dB under the loudest stays silent). Ideas credited, no code of others: Cooley and Tukey 1965 (the FFT), Xfer Serum's file convention (NOTICE.md, LICENSING.md, ABOUT > CREDITS). Tests: `tests/wave_test.c` `t_user` (the byte map, a build against a reference computed in the test, an empty slot, names, a slot stored under a playing part, a slot stored in the middle of a build, factory recipes encoded as user bytes play within 2 steps), `tests/wtab_test.c` (flash places, CRC, power-on, torn copies, refusal while playing, stale transfers, erase, ERASE DATA, backup round trip), `web/test_wavetable.mjs` and `tests/wavetable_fixture.c` (the converter: saw / square / sine / sweep, a Serum-like 2048 x 256 file, factory renders converted back).

**How a table is made.** A table is a **recipe**, not stored samples: a few lines that give each of 64 harmonics a level for a place in the scan (integer arithmetic only; the FM tables are made from the FM wave itself, projected onto the harmonics). Every harmonic has the same phase (sine phase) in every frame, so a mix of two frames is the mix of their spectra (no level dips while POS moves). When a table is chosen it is built into the **part's engine memory** (engines.c `eng_mem`, the region the heavy engines share; **12,278 of its 12,888 bytes a part**, nothing new in the pool, both parts can hold a table): 19 frames x 5 band-limited levels of 256 / 128 / 128 / 64 / 64 samples holding the harmonics 1..64 / 32 / 16 / 8 / 4, 8-bit, each frame at one gain (its RMS to half of full scale, its peak at most full scale), plus one byte a frame (its fundamental's amplitude: the sixth level). Only a 4-byte header is cleared when the region goes to another engine; WAVE -> PHASE -> WAVE finds its table still there.

**Band-limiting.** A voice reads the level whose top harmonic stays under ~25 kHz at its effective speed (the note x SYNC's ratio x BEND's steepest part): what passes Nyquist folds back above ~19 kHz. Over the top half octave of a level it cross-fades into the next one (no step in brightness when a note bends or SYNC / BEND sweep); above 6.3 kHz effective it fades to the frame's fundamental alone (a sine). SYNC's restarts are smoothed with a two-sample polyBLEP; a DC tracker takes off the mean BEND and SYNC create. Measured (`tests/wave_test.c`: one held voice, an FFT, the energy away from the note's harmonics below 16 kHz, dB under the note): all 26 tables at POS 0 / 64 / 127, C2 .. C8: **median -48.5 dB, worst -30.7 dB** (GLASS at its brightest end, C5; saw-like tables -42 dB or better); the twelve of 0.2.7 worst -31.9 dB (THIN at its thin end, C5; BAND -32.4, YELL -32.9, AIR -32.1, PIPE -32.9, the rest -34.9 .. -43.3); the same on level 0 alone (no band-limiting): up to +7 dB (aliasing louder than the note). SYNC 20..127 (C3..C6): mean -43 dB, worst -34 dB (without the polyBLEP -38 / -27). BEND 30..127: worst -32 dB. What limits it is the linear read's images (they fall with the table length squared: the reason the upper levels keep 128 and 64 samples), not the 8 bits (within 0.5 dB of exact samples at every level).

**The build.** In the **main loop** (`wave_service`: main.c, every pass and its wait; the browser build's `web_frame`), one frame per call, into 2,236 bytes of main-loop scratch (`.bss`), then copied into the part's memory with the interrupts off (646 bytes, ~0.6 k instructions: the audio side never sees half a frame). Never in the audio interrupt: a table is 0.44 .. 2.0 M host instructions (ORGAN the cheapest, GLASS the dearest; mean 1.2 M; the twelve of 0.2.7 0.58 (BAND) .. 1.56 M (AIR)), ~3 .. 14 ms of the device's CPU (420 k host instructions = one 2.9 ms half buffer), so a few tens of ms of wall time while the audio takes its share. The audio side owns the table asked for and the table in memory: a **TABLE change fades the part's notes out** over 4 blocks (2.9 ms) on the old table, then they are **silent while the new table is built**, then **fade in** (2.9 ms); a change back during the fade-out keeps the old table. A build number stops a build that is no longer wanted (another TABLE, the part's memory going to another engine): changes every block for 300 blocks, and an engine switch in the middle of a build, are tested (no crash, the other engine's memory untouched, the last table built byte for byte). Deterministic: the same bytes on every build. The host renders (`tests/regress.c`, the sound audit) build at once when a sound loads (`tests/hostsim.c` `wave_build_now`): they hear the sound as it plays once built.

**Cost** (host instructions per voice and sample, 8 voices, `tests/wave_test.c`): plain 59, SUB 81, DTN 99, SUB + DTN 122, SYNC 81, BEND 86, all four 178 (PHASE: 57 .. 120). The engine's 9 test presets in `tests/cpu_baseline.txt` (8 voices, the whole mix): 798 .. 1573 (PHASE 741 .. 1263). Memory: pool +0; `.bss` +2,244 bytes (the build's scratch); flash about +7 KB (a host estimate: the device build was not made). The twelve tables of 0.2.7: no new memory (the frames, the scratch and the engine memory are the same size), flash about +2.1 KB (the table code compiled for Thumb-2 at -Os, 2,880 -> 5,010 bytes: a proxy, the device's compiler was not run) and ~0.4 KB of sound data for the six sounds.

**MORPH** (appendix B.2): TABLE never rolls, not in WILD either (a new table must be built and the part is silent meanwhile: no fade can hide that); POS continuous; BEND and SYNC continuous, in SMART a move of at most 32 (both speed the read up, and a sweep of both across the levels took a sparse table's top partials away within a few % of the fader: a cliff, rule 3-bis; `tests/morph_test.c` checks 20 seeds at AMOUNT 100), and SMART keeps a SYNC of 0 at 0 (rule 3g); SUB as PHASE's (rises at most 48); DTN as PHASE's (zero stays zero, 10 ct at most). New routes in WILD may go to SHP (POS).

**ARP TARGET:** ALL only (one pitch unit; SUB follows it). **Favourites:** engine 14's flags are kept in bytes 16..31 of DIGITAL's retired row of the favourites store (row 14 is the MIDI LEARN map, row 15 settings bytes). DIGITAL had 8 presets (byte 0, moved to FM6 and cleared at each settings import, which now clears bytes 0..15 only), so no older setting holds anything in bytes 16..31. **MIDI:** CC74 / CC71 are the multimode filter's CUT / RES.

**Factory sounds (17; 21 since 0.2.9, with FROZEN AIR 5.7, PURPLE FLUTE 2.9, LICORICE 2.10 and BRASS PAD 3.12 of the batch that replaced the sample engines' sounds)**, each from a recipe of SOUND_RECIPES.md (the numbers there; SOUND_RECIPES.md "WAVE" mapping): PWM PAD (3.3), VOWEL PAD (3.5), GLASS PAD (3.6), EVOLVING PAD (3.4 / 5.6: two slow LFOs on POS and one on a gentle filter), DRAWBARS (4.5: 8' 4' with SUB as the 16', a third-harmonic percussion from FILTER ENV), WAVE CHIMES (4.3: bright onset decaying to a purer tone), SYNTH BRASS (2.3 / 4.8), DIGI PLUCK (4.1 / 4.6: the FM index falling with the note), SYNC LEAD (2.2: FILTER ENV and aftertouch sweep SYNC), HARM BASS (1.1: the HARM table's harmonic count as the filter envelope), RESO SWEEP (5.6). They are last within their categories (WAVE is last in the engine order), so the list places after them moved by one or more. **0.2.7 adds six** on the new tables: GROWL BASS (1.7: GROWL, a half-note LFO through the vowels, a sine sub, drive), REESE BASS (1.5: REESE with DTN 25 ct, a sine sub, a ~4 kHz low-pass), HOOVER (2.8: HOOVR, a fast LFO on the width, the pitch diving ~1 semitone into the note, deep chorus), DIGITAL PAD (3.8: BAND, a slow FILTER ENV carrying the band up), BREATH PAD (3.9: AIR, aftertouch to the breath, a 2 kHz low-pass) and PLATE BELL (4.3 / 4.4: METAL, the amp envelope carrying the scan down). Within a category the WAVE sounds are sorted by name, so the new ones sit among the older WAVE sounds at the category's end (114 factory sounds).

---

## 3. MORPH (the signature feature)

One fader, two random end states, one percentage.

### 3.1 Concepts
- **Original**: the patch as saved. The reference for all random changes.
- **End A** (fader at 0) and **End B** (fader at 127): two random variations of the original.
- **MORPH fader**: 0..127, stays where it is left. It is a CC, a matrix source, and a knob on screen. Output sound = the blend between end A and end B at the fader position.
- **AMOUNT**: the one percentage, 0-100%. There is no other randomness control.

### 3.2 How an end is rolled
For every parameter that is allowed to be randomised:
- **Continuous parameter** (level, cutoff, times, depths, ...): the new value is the original value plus a random offset, at most AMOUNT percent of the parameter's range, clipped to the range. At 20%, nothing moves more than 20% of its range from the original. The offset's size is drawn uniformly from 0 to AMOUNT (owner, 2026-10-11: replaces the bell-shaped draw, whose moves were mostly tiny), with a random sign, in the parameter's perceptual domain (log for frequencies and times); a move that would leave the range (or a safety limit) goes the other way instead, so nearly every rolled parameter moves. The EQ rolls only in WILD. The DUCK values (DEPTH at most 50 %, attack, hold, release, threshold) roll in SMART only while the ducker is on (DEPTH above 0) and in WILD always; its SRC, NOTE, RATE and CURVE never roll (rules 3g, 3h).
- **Switch / choice parameter** (waveform inside an engine, filter type, LFO shape; in WILD also matrix source/destination): AMOUNT percent chance that it differs from the original. If it does, a different valid choice is picked at random (never a different engine). At 20%, one in five switches changes.
- A roll never changes the original.

### 3.3 Smart rules (nothing "stupid")
1. **Everything that switches is faded, never flipped.**
   - Continuous parameters blend linearly (log for frequencies and times).
   - LFO shapes: both shapes run, their outputs are cross-faded along the fader.
   - Filter type: the state-variable outputs are mixed (LP/BP/HP), cross-faded.
   - Matrix routes: in SMART an assigned route keeps its source and destination (rule 3f), so only its amount moves. In WILD a route that differs between A and B fades its amount out and the other in.
   - Waveform / oscillator shape inside an engine (for example ANALOG's wave and PHASE's wave; **not** TRIO's wave, which would leave no source and click): both shapes are generated and cross-faded along the fader. Cheap, because it is one engine.
   - The engine itself is **never** changed by the morph.
2. **Parameters that cannot be faded are never randomised**: poly/mono/legato mode, tempo sync mode switches, clock source, MIDI settings, ARP on/off. The list is explicit per parameter (appendix B).
3. **Never randomised at all**: the engine, MASTER, tempo, every octave / semitone / transpose / interval parameter (melodies stay in key; fine detune is allowed within 10 cents), MIDI channel, poly / mono / legato mode, LFO polarity, the arp's on/off, the original patch's name and the morph's own settings.
3-bis. **No cliffs in smart mode** (owner's rule, found on LOFI: near 80% of the travel the sound went crazy): in smart mode a few percent of fader travel never makes a large sonic jump. Any parameter or switch where a small step changes the sound abruptly (mode switches inside an engine, bit-depth / sample-rate steps, noise or wave-RAM modes, sync on/off, anything that restarts or reroutes a generator) is either cross-faded or never rolled in smart mode. Only WILD may contain cliffs. Acceptance test: for every engine, over many rolls at high AMOUNT, sweep the fader in fine steps (at least 256) on a rendered note and measure loudness and brightness (spectral centroid) per step; a step whose change is far larger than its neighbours' (a cliff) fails the test and names the parameter responsible; fix = cross-fade it or tag it never-rolled.
3a. **Pitch stays untouched** (owner's rule, so the pitch stays musical): with the PITCH setting off, the envelope-to-pitch depth, the LFO-to-pitch (vibrato) depth, glide, every octave / semitone / transpose / interval parameter and any matrix route to PITCH or VIB are never changed (WILD: not at all; SMART: source and destination stay, and the route's amount stays whole too, owner 2026-10-12: pitch stays; with PITCH on the amount rolls like every route's, rule 3f). Only the fine DETUNE items may move (within 10 cents in SMART). With PITCH on, those items roll like any other. A zero is not special (rule 3e).
3b. **Pan and stereo spread follow the stereo mode**: in DUEL mode (hard pan) PAN, SPREAD and the per-voice stereo parameters are never changed; in STEREO mode they may change.
3c. **An insert that is off stays off** (owner, 2026-10-12): INSERT TYPE never changes in SMART. While the type is OFF, its values A B C and MIX are not rolled either. With an insert on, A B C and MIX roll within the type. WILD may change the type and switch an insert on.
3d. **Removed engines**: DRUM is not part of DUELL (section 2.1), so the morph never meets them.
3e. **Zero is not a lock** (owner, 2026-10-12): an ordinary parameter at 0 rolls like any other value; for example a SAT of 0 at AMOUNT 11 becomes 0..11 (a subtle change, SAT never rises more than 40 above its original). The only values that stay put at 0 are the mode flips of rule 3g.
3f. **Modulation matrix in SMART** (owner, 2026-10-12): the roll never adds a route, removes one or changes a route's source or destination. Only the amount of an assigned route is rolled, by the one percentage, and **every** assigned route's amount rolls, including a route whose destination parameter the roll itself does not move (e.g. into PHYS BOW while BOW is 0, a non-continuous engine item, PAN in MONO, or from the MORPH fader); the one exception is a route into PITCH / VIB while the PITCH setting is OFF, which keeps its amount whole (rule 3a; with PITCH on it rolls like the others). WILD may add routes and change sources and destinations.
3g. **Distinctive things that are off are not switched on in SMART** (owner, 2026-10-12; **the multimode filter is the exception, owner 2026-10-14: SMART may switch a bypassed filter on and rolls its MODE and SLOPE too, section 2.5**; **0.2.11, owner 2026-10-14 (CPU, section 6.3): SMART switches ON a stage that is off in the Original with HALF the chance it had**: the bypassed multimode filter (MODE LP, CUT open), DIST from 0, the CHO / DLY / REV sends from 0, SPREAD from 0 (STEREO), the FLT depths (ENV, FILTER ENV, LFO, LFO 2 to FLT) and the LFO depths (LFO to AMP, LFO to SHP, LFO 2 to SHP) from 0. Each is a coin (the filter one coin for its CUT, RES, MODE, SLOPE) from a stream of its own derived from the seed (`morph.c morph_wake_half`, rs4: all 13 coins always drawn in a fixed order, so an end is still a pure function of the Original, AMOUNT and seed, and every other value draws as before); tails puts the Original's value back (off), heads keeps the roll as it was, so the chance is exactly half. A stage that is already on rolls as before; WILD is unchanged. A stored End B seed therefore regenerates differently than in 0.2.10 where a coin falls on tails (accepted pre-release; 12 factory End B seeds re-picked so the 114-sound audit passes, section 3.6): insert effects (rule 3c); WAVE's SYNC (0 = no sync stays 0; section 2.6); the ARP (its on/off never; mode, rate, octaves, gate only with the ARP on, and an off arp stays off); the PITCH setting (rule 3a); PHYS BOW (0 = struck; any BOW above 0 replaces the strike by a bowed excitation, so BOW 0 stays 0 and BOW above 0 rolls normally); the ducker (DUCK: if DEPTH is 0 the whole ducker stays off, if it is on, only DEPTH rolls, by the one percentage (owner, 2026-10-13: ATK, RLS and THR stay as tuned, so the pump keeps its feel; their random draws are kept, so a stored End B seed draws the same numbers; HOLD is legacy since 0.2.5, its value never moves). WILD rolls DEPTH, ATK, RLS and THR as before and may switch the ducker on). WILD may switch all of these on. Left out on purpose (no equivalent mode flip): NOISE impulse density, PHYS POS, FORMANT RAND, PHASE WAVE2 "-", sends at 0, LFO and ENV depths at 0, EQ gains (the EQ is rolled in WILD only). The SLICE engine's STUTTER / GATE modes are engine choices that never roll; no insert or other part parameter named SHATTER exists in the firmware.
3h. **The ducker's sidechain input never rolls, in SMART or WILD** (owner, 2026-10-12): SRC (PUMP / PART / CHn), the NOTE / channel selection, the PUMP rate and the release curve are never changed.
4. **Never silent, never painful**: per rule set carried over from the Teensy randomizer (`randomizer.h`): sum of audible source levels capped, filter cutoff floor and no dead filter path, resonance versus drive coupling, no route into an off block, at most a few matrix routes with moderate amounts, no LFO self-loops, output peak checked by rendering a test note in the emulator and in firmware at roll time (quick: 1 block).
5. **Seeded**: each roll uses a seed, so a good roll can be reproduced (saved with the patch; since v3 of the MORPH page, 2026-10-11, the seed number is no longer shown on the panel).

### 3.4 Controls (all four live on one MORPH page, see 3.5)
| Item | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| MORPH | The fader (0..127, A<>B). |
| AMOUNT | The one percentage. |
| ROLL A | Roll a new random end A from the original. |
| ROLL B | Roll a new random end B from the original. |
| UNDO | Step back to the previous roll of End A or End B, chosen like ROLL (knob: twist left = A, twist right = B; CC 23: 0..63 = A, 64..127 = B). History of 2 earlier rolls per end (memory trim 1: the owner, one UNDO is enough; it was 8), so repeated UNDO walks back that far. |
| ~~KEEP~~ | Removed in v2 (3.5): the ends simply stay; saving is SAVE held (3.5). |

**End choice** (owner's revision, supersedes the old far-end rule): UNDO names its end exactly like ROLL (left = A, right = B); it no longer depends on the fader. Rolling or undoing the end you are hearing is smoothed over about 50 ms so it does not click.

### 3.4a MORPH TARGET (owner's addition)
**0.2.9 (owner, 2026-10-14, "option A", a first step): the target is ALGORITHM's position on the MORPH page and in PERFORM.** There ALGORITHM has three positions, PART 1, BOTH, PART 2 (left to right, a detent a step, clamped at the ends, no wrap); PART 1 / PART 2 select that part and the morph works on it (SELECTED), BOTH is the BOTH below. It is one state, shared by the MORPH page, PERFORM, MIDI CC 111 and the TEST screen's MORPH: leaving the page keeps it, coming back finds it as left. On **every other page** ALGORITHM selects PART 1 or PART 2 only (no BOTH there, the header shows that part's badge alone: "if it shows both it should edit both"); turning it there does not clear BOTH. The selected part (`song.sel`) is always the last single part chosen: what the other pages edit and show, the screen's part colour, the keys' part (unchanged: the keys and MIDI IN SELECTED play the selected part, LAYER both, as before); turning ALGORITHM from PART 1 to BOTH keeps part 1 selected, from PART 2 to BOTH part 2. **Not saved:** power-on is a single part (SELECTED; which part is the autosave's selected part, as before); a multi load and a settings import never set BOTH. Formats unchanged: the settings bit (`favorites.factory[15][12]` bit 7) is reserved, kept as stored and neither read nor written (no flash write for ALGORITHM); a multi's TARGET byte (record byte 5) is still written (BOTH when saved in BOTH) and never loaded; the morph block (`morph_pack`) the same. MORPH SET's TARGET knob is gone (section 3.5). Code: `ui_input.c algo_step / algo_input`, `ui_draw.c badge_lit`.
- **Future (owner idea, 2026-10-14; not built):** BOTH also on the edit pages, an edit then moving both parts by the same step (relative). The hook is named `ALGO_BOTH_EDIT_HOOK` in `ui_input.c` (`algo_step`'s comment, `edit_param`'s call).

What the target does:
- **SELECTED**: only the part picked with ALGORITHM. Each part has its own fader, ends, history and AMOUNT; the other part's morph is left where it is.
- **BOTH**: one fader moves both parts. Each part keeps its **own** random ends (rolled independently from its own original, with the same AMOUNT), so the two parts vary differently but move together. ROLL A / ROLL B roll that end on both parts; UNDO A / UNDO B act on that end of both parts.
- Over MIDI the fader is CC 20 on each part's channel: in SELECTED mode it moves only that part; in BOTH mode a CC 20 on either channel moves both.
- ~~The setting is saved with the device settings. Where: the MORPH SET page (section 3.5), KNOB 1 = TARGET (PART / BOTH).~~ 0.2.9: ALGORITHM on the MORPH page / PERFORM, not saved (above). CC 111 sets it (0-42 PART 1, 43-85 PART 2, 86-127 BOTH; BOTH keeps the selected part).

### 3.5 Where it lives, v2 (owner's revision after trying v0.1, 2026-10-10)
**MORPH is the main page of the device**: HOME opens on it. **The HOME round is MORPH, MORPH SET, MODE, MIXER** (0.2.14, owner: OVERVIEW, the round's fifth and last page up to 0.2.13 (the old HOME overview: engine, cutoff, resonance, attack, release of the selected part, the scope or the parts as rows under it), is removed, with its MENU > DISPLAY row and HOME OPENS' OVERVIEW choice: the MIXER now shows both parts at once (below)). After MIXER, HOME and SELECT go to MORPH again; footers read 1/4 .. 4/4.

**The MIXER page** (0.2.9; its panel two sides since 0.2.14; **0.2.25 layout round, owner: GLO's first page, no longer HOME's**): cards **K1 LEVEL 1, K2 PAN 1, K3 LEVEL 2, K4 PAN 2** (both parts at once, the QUICK GLO layer's; up to 0.2.25 HOME's MIXER had K1 MUTE, K2 PAN, K3 SPACE, K4 LEVEL of the selected part); each part's cell reads MUTE, lit in its colour while muted; the mutes are GLO + OCT- / OCT+. **LEVEL here is the MIXER LEVEL** (0.2.19, "The level split" below): the player's volume, not part of a patch. HOME OPENS is MORPH or MODE (a stored MIXER reads MORPH).
- **The panel shows both parts side by side (0.2.14, owner), the GLO main page's layout:** part 1's cell on the left, part 2's on the right (x 6 and 146, 88 x 112 px each), **GLO's level meters between them** (the same bars: the part's peak before MASTER, -48 .. 0 dBFS, the peak hold, the -6 / -1 dBFS zones, the CLIP lamp; one code, `ui_layer.c glo_meter_step` / `glo_meters`). A cell: the part's **badge** (lit in its colour; muted: DIM) and its **sound's name** in its colour (ellipsised when long); **LEVEL** (its dB value in M, as the card; a **MUTE** keycap right of the caption while muted); **PAN** (the card's value; in DUEL **LEFT / RIGHT in DIM**, fixed there, the reason as on the card: turning K2 says `DUEL: PAN IS FIXED`); the part's three sends **REV, DLY, CHO** (the FX page's `P_REV`, `P_DLY`, `P_CHOR`, read only here: they are edited on the FX page). A muted part's values are DIM, a send at 0 DIM. **The selected part's cell has a 2 px outline in its colour**, the other a 1 px RAISE one, so the cell the cards edit is always marked; the value of the knob just turned (K1 LEVEL, K2 PAN, K4's MUTE keycap) is drawn in the accent. SPACE (K3) is global and lives on its card only.
- **Cost:** the panel is drawn again only when its signature changes (the selection, DUEL, the knob just turned, the palette, and per part the name, engine, LEVEL, PAN, the sends, MUTE); the meters are their own 44 px strip, drawn only when a bar, a hold tick or a lamp moved. Idle (nothing changing, silent): no pixel is drawn (`select_test sweep_mixer`). Up to 0.2.13 the panel was two wide strips one above the other (5b.3). No new parameter, no format change.

**The level split (0.2.19, owner decision of 2026-10-15; before, one level per part did both jobs).** A part's loudness is two things, as on a real synth:
- **PATCH TRIM (`P_TRIM`, label TRIM):** the loudness stored WITH a patch. A factory sound's tuned level is its trim (the sounds balance against each other by it: `tools/duell_sounds.py` `"TRIM"`, until 0.2.18 `"LEVEL"`, the same numbers); a patch record holds it (SAVE stores the trim, RESTORE brings the factory one); a multi holds it with the part's sound. Edit it on **EDIT > VOICE 2/2, K4** (GLMOD, GLD, SPRD, TRIM: the sound's output last). Range and scale as the level: 0 off, 0.5 dB a step, 112 = 0 dB, default 104 (-4.0 dB). Not morphed (End B keeps A's trim) and not automatable (it is the sound's, not a move).
- **MIXER LEVEL (`P_LEVEL`, label LVL, id 0 as before):** the player's volume on the mixer (MIXER K4, GLO K3 / K4, the editor's part header LVL, MIDI CC7, MIDI LEARN, automation). It is NOT part of a patch: **loading a sound (the PATCHES list, the PRESETS knob, an engine change, INIT, UNDO, MIDI program change, the editor) never changes it**, so a part 2 you turned down stays down when you load a new sound into it. It is kept with a multi and with the project / autosave (the performance state). Default **unity: 112 = 0 dB.** Range 0 (off) to 127 (+7.5 dB).
- **The audio gain is the product** (`fx.c part_lvl`): `LEVEL_Q12[TRIM] x LEVEL_Q12[LEVEL] >> 12`, the dB sum, one call a block, applied where the one level was (after the part's INSERT, EQ, DIST, PERFORM's mute and the DUCK; before BAL, PAN and the sends, so the sends follow both). At unity the gain is exactly the trim's, so every sound plays as it did (the goldens and the 14 factory multis render bit for bit as before). The product is held to the top of the table, +7.5 dB, the headroom the stages after it were made for; it binds only where trim and mixer together are above +7.5 dB. 0 in either is silent. The matrix destination LEVEL is named **TRIM** now and moves the trim. MODULES (PART FX OFF) and PERFORM's MUTE act on the signal before the level stage, as they did.
- **Formats.** The ids and `P_COUNT` (127) are unchanged: the trim takes the id of the legacy DUCK HOLD (never read since 0.2.5), so no store grew. Multi version **5** (was 4) and project revision **3** (was 2): older multis and projects are not read (the factory multis M01 to M10 show their factory versions; `tools/factory_multis_levelsplit.py` moved their records: trim = the old LEVEL, mixer 112). A patch record carries **`PTF_TR`** (flag 2, the old drum-grid bit, never set): the loudness is the `P_TRIM` pair and the record holds no mixer level (id 0 is never written). **A record written before this change (no `PTF_TR`) is still read: its LEVEL is its trim** (the old default 104 included), so saved patches keep their loudness; the first save over it writes the new form. The backup file format is unchanged (it carries the records).
- **Where it is not shown:** the MIXER page (GLO's) is the mixer page (no TRIM); the sound pages (EDIT) have the TRIM and no mixer setting. PAN, a mixer setting a sound load leaves alone, left VOICE 2/2: it is the MIXER's K2 / K4 and the editor's MIXER block. **0.2.25 layout round (owner):** TRIM is shown **PATCH LEVEL** everywhere (the unit's VOICE 2/2 K4 card PATCH / LEVEL on two lines, the matrix DEST code PATCH and its line PATCH LEVEL, the editor's PATCH block); the stored id and label TRIM stay (keys).
- **K3 SPACE is PERFORM's K2 SPACE, the one global value** (`perf_space`, -100 .. +100, 0 in the middle; the same card, colours and range; `space_turn` / `space_card` in `ui_perform.c`), not a part's setting: turning it on the MIXER moves PERFORM's SPACE and the other way round, and its effect (section 5e) is on whenever it is not 0, on every page (`fx.c` never looked at PERFORM being on). The card is never greyed for 0; in DUEL the width part has no effect (turning it says `DUEL: NO WIDTH` once since PERFORM opened). It replaced the REV card: **the part's reverb send REV is still on the FX page (K4, `P_REV`, CC 91)**. Not stored (SPACE is transient); 0.2.14: PERF LEAVE FADE takes it back to 0 when PERFORM was where it was moved and is left, and on a sound or multi load (section 5e). MIDI LEARN cannot pick it: turning K3 while learning says `SPACE: NO LEARN` (no learn code is free, and the editor protocol is unchanged).

**MORPH page**
| Knob | Job |
| --- | --- |
| KNOB 1 | MORPH, the fader A<>B |
| KNOB 2 | ROLL: twist left = ROLL A, twist right = ROLL B (as built) |
| KNOB 3 | UNDO A<>B: twist left = undo End A's last roll, twist right = undo End B's (as ROLL); the card shows "A<>B" at rest and "<A" / "B>" while a twist builds up. There is **no KEEP**: both ends simply stay as they are until you roll them again or load a sound. |
| KNOB 4 | AMOUNT, the one percentage; **each patch carries its own** (see "AMOUNT follows the patch" below) |

(v3, owner 2026-10-11: ROLL and UNDO moved to KNOB 2 / 3, AMOUNT to KNOB 4; the MIDI CCs are unchanged.) **The panel (v3)** is an animated picture of the morph instead of the old A..B bar, seed numbers and UNDO counts: layered ribbon waveforms that get wilder as the fader moves from A to B (2 ribbons growing to 6, larger, faster, from smooth sines into angular, stepped, glitchy shapes), coloured from the part's A colour to its B colour (High Contrast: each ribbon solid A or B, no blends), breathing with the part's output level (a slow gentle motion in silence); a cursor line at the fader with its value in a thumb on an A<>B track, "A" and "B" at the ends; TARGET BOTH: one ribbon set per part in its colours (part 2 phase-shifted). About 16 frames/s while the page is shown, still with MENU > ANIM OFF. "HOLD SAVE = SAVE PATCH" stays as a small dim line at the bottom.

**A rolled B from the start, and always back at A.** Whenever a patch is loaded (and at power-on) the **fader goes to A (0)**: End A is the patch's sound, and End B is the B saved with the patch, or, for a patch with none saved, a **fresh roll** (default AMOUNT, seeded), so the fader does something at once.

**AMOUNT follows the patch** (owner, 2026-10-12). AMOUNT is held per part (settings bytes: one per part, unchanged format). Loading a patch sets that part's AMOUNT to the patch's own, so the KNOB 4 display changes when presets are switched: a factory sound uses its category amount (the sound's bamt override if it has one); a saved slot and a MULTI part use the AMOUNT saved with them (the patch's End B block carries it in its previously spare byte 5, as AMOUNT + 1; 0 = not stored, as in older patches: the AMOUNT End B was rolled with, itself the knob value when saved); a patch without any End B block keeps the part's current AMOUNT. Loads are: BROWSE / PATCHES, MIDI program change, MULTI recall, power-on. Turning KNOB 4 (or CC 25) changes the AMOUNT of the part(s) of the TARGET until another patch is loaded; saving the patch stores it. The settings keep only the AMOUNT the player last chose with the knob (a patch load does not rewrite the settings, so no flash write per preset change); it is the AMOUNT for a sound that carries none. Format note: patches saved by this version put a non-zero byte 5 in the End B block; older firmware reads such a block as damaged (it then rolls a fresh End B); this firmware reads old blocks fine.

**Saving (2026-10-11, replaces SAVE x2; 2026-10-15: one list of patch slots).** A press of SAVE always opens the SAVE pages, on the MORPH pages too; it saves nothing by itself. **Holding SAVE (about 0.6 s) on any page opens one confirmation, "OVERWRITE #NN <name>?" (OCT+ YES, OCT- NO), and OCT+ writes the selected part's sound into slot #NN.** The slot offered is the one the sound was loaded from (a sound that came from no slot, an engine change for one, is offered the slot its factory sound belongs to); **K1 in the dialog picks another slot** (round the ends, KNOB ACCEL applies), the line under the question says what is written ("WITH <the sound's name>") and the dialog shows the K1 hint. **K2 RENAME (0.2.21, owner, 2026-10-16): turn knob 2 in the dialog and the NAME screen opens for the sound's name** (the field starts with the name it has, or the one chosen before; the title says "NAME #NN" for the slot offered; the same 12 characters and keys as every NAME screen; the footer says OK / CANCEL), OCT+ keeps the name and returns to the dialog, which now reads "WITH <the new name>" (nothing is written yet, the slot K1 picked stays), OCT- returns with the old name; the dialog's OCT+ then writes the record under the new name (the part plays it and the footer shows it), OCT- drops the new name with the dialog. Spaces at the ends are dropped; an empty name keeps the sound's own. Not offered with TARGET BOTH (two names, two slots: the hint line is not shown). Nothing else is asked: no "new sound", no "no place": every slot always holds a sound (a saved one or the factory one), so the only way to save is to overwrite, and a slot can always be written. The sound keeps its name unless renamed there (a copy in another slot has the same name; a sound with no name of its own gets the engine's automatic name, "ANALOG 12"); the web editor renames too. With TARGET = BOTH and the MORPH page shown (0.2.9: the MORPH page only, where the header shows both; not MORPH SET), it asks "OVERWRITE BOTH PATCHES?" (the detail line names the two slots, "#3 + #83") and saves both parts, each over its own slot ("SAVED 1+2"); two parts that came from the same slot are refused ("SAME SLOT") because the second would lose the first. What is saved: the sound (End A, the original), **End B as well** (seed + AMOUNT + roll settings in the record, regenerated exactly on load) and AMOUNT; the fader position is not saved (a slot always loads at A). Hold SAVE no longer undoes the last load (that undo is gone from SAVE; the EDIT layer's OCT- still puts back its loads).
**No split between factory and saved sounds (owner, 2026-10-15: "there is one list of 128 patch slots").** The factory fills the slots by default: **slot #NN is the factory sound at browse position NN** (the sort order category, engine, name that gives the numbers of `docs/PATCH_NUMBERS.md`), nothing is written for that (a virtual fallback: a fresh or erased unit shows the factory sounds in all 128 slots, there is no first-boot write). **Saving writes a record into the slot and the record wins from then on; RESTORE removes the record and the factory sound is back** (section 3.6). The UI never says "user patch", "stored copy" or "factory patch": a slot is a slot. The list (PATCHES page, PRESETS knob, the EDIT layer's sound list, FAV and the type filters) is the 128 slots in slot order, and shows what each holds now. **The factory sound a restored or never-saved slot shows is the one at that position in the installed firmware:** the factory set changes between versions (sounds get added and sorted in), so after an update a slot you never saved can show another factory sound than before, while a saved record stays in its slot number. No compatibility is promised before the alpha (the README and the CHANGELOG say so). Not redirected to a slot: the editor's preset load and an engine change (ENGINE knob: the engine's first factory sound), which load the factory sound itself (the part is then in the slot that sound belongs to if that slot still holds it, else in none). The SAVE pages, in this order and named the same in the footer and the header, are PATCHES (section 3.6), PATTERNS, MULTIS (section 5b.4; was PROJECT), TOOLS. On these pages only, the header's centre shows that title (in the tempo's text style) instead of the transport and tempo; the part badge and REC mark stay, the slot number and octave are dropped when the title leaves no room. A SAVE press opens PATCHES (the first page; again = the next).
Storage requirement: End B must be reproduced exactly. The build chooses how (a compact seed + AMOUNT that regenerates the same end from the saved original, or a stored list of the differences from the original) within the limits of the user-preset record and the format rules (section 6, storage decision), and says which and why; hearing the same sound after a reload is the acceptance test.

**MORPH SET page (every knob has a job)**
| Knob | Job |
| --- | --- |
| ~~KNOB 1~~ | ~~TARGET~~: removed in 0.2.9 (ALGORITHM on the MORPH page is the target, section 3.4a). The knobs below moved one to the left: **K1 SMART, K2 ARP, K3 PITCH, K4 an empty column** (it does nothing; nothing is drawn). The panel still says in plain words what the morph moves: "THE MORPH MOVES PART 1" / "... BOTH". |
| KNOB 2 | SMART: ON (default) or **WILD**. WILD: every parameter and every switch can change, none of the smart rules (exclusions, caps, dead-filter and level safety, fades) apply, switches simply flip at the middle of the fader. The engine still never changes, and the master volume, tempo, system settings and (see PITCH) pitch stay untouched. The master limiter stays on. |
| KNOB 3 | ARP: OFF (default) or ON. ON = a roll also shuffles the arpeggiator settings: its mode (algorithm), rate, octaves, gate and, once it exists, the SEQUENCER step pattern. |
| KNOB 4 | PITCH: OFF (default) or ON, separate from WILD. OFF = the morph never changes anything that moves pitch: octave, semitone and interval settings, transpose, glide, pitch-envelope and vibrato depth (also in WILD). ON = those may change too (the whole-number ones step at the middle of the fader; "zero stays zero" is lifted for pitch modulation). Added at the owner's request in place of a fader-speed setting (the speed of the fader is the player's hand). |
SMART, ARP and PITCH are saved with the device settings (not the patch; TARGET no longer, 0.2.9). CC 111 = TARGET as before; new CCs 113 SMART, 114 ARP, 115 PITCH (**[verify]** no clash).


### 3.6 PATCHES (owner's design, built 2026-10-12; 2026-10-15: one list of 128 slots; replaces SAVE > PRESETS and SAVE > USER PATCHES)
**One list of 128 patch slots.** SAVE's first page, **PATCHES**, is the only list of sounds: **128 slots, #1 .. #128, in slot order**. A slot holds **a saved record if one was written, else the factory sound at that browse position** (category-major: BASS, LEAD, PAD, PLUCK, KEYS, PERC, FX, OTHER, then engine order, then name: the numbers of `docs/PATCH_NUMBERS.md`). There are 128 factory sounds for the 128 slots, so a fresh unit has a factory sound in every slot and **no slot is ever empty, full or "free"**: there is no count anywhere (no "ERASE 0/128", no "FREE nn", no "N of 128 used" on the device or in the editor) and no "NO FREE PLACE". A row is "#12 FOLD BASS" (the slot's number, its name, a favourite starred). The current patch's slot is the cursor; a sound that is in no slot of the list (an engine change loaded it, a multi, or its slot was overwritten since) shows "--".

| Control | Job on PATCHES |
| --- | --- |
| KNOB 1 PATCH | Steps through the list shown (the card: the slot's number, e.g. "#42", and a small gauge). A slot loads at once, as the PRESETS knob loads it (the sound with its own TRIM (0.2.19; the mixer LEVEL stays), plus the arp, keys and SEQ pattern the slot carries, see "Arp, keys and SEQ pattern" below; PAN, MUTE, the mix, ROOT and the other steps stay; MORPH at A). With KNOB ACCEL ON a fast turn moves several slots a click and loads only where it stops. The PRESETS knob steps the same list. |
| KNOB 2 TYPE | A filter, never a load and never an engine change: ALL, FAV, BASS, LEAD, PAD, PLUCK, KEYS, PERC, FX, OTHER, then **one entry per engine** (that engine's sounds only). It filters what the slots hold now (a saved sound sorts by its own category and engine). Kept with the settings. |
| KNOB 3 FAV | Marks the slot (the flag belongs to the slot: it stays through a save and a restore). A sound that is in no slot has no flag: the card is not shown. |
| KNOB 4 RESTORE | A turn right asks "RESTORE #NN TO <factory name>?" (the saved sound that goes is named under it; OCT+ yes, OCT- no) and takes the saved record out of the slot, so the **factory sound comes back**; the part that played the saved sound plays the factory sound. **Honest: it is only active when the slot holds a saved sound:** on a slot that holds its factory sound the card is faded with the reason **FACTORY** and a turn says ALREADY FACTORY. Stopped transport only (STOP TO SAVE). |
| SELECT | Turns the SAVE sub-pages only (PATCHES, PATTERNS, MULTIS, TOOLS), as on every page; it never moves in the list (label law). |
| SAVE (tap) | **Opens the page that fits (0.2.2, owner 2026-10-12):** PATTERNS when the current page is one of the SEQ button's pages (STEP, GRID, AUTOMATION: the sequencer); on any other page by the output MODE: LAYER opens MULTIS, DUEL and STEREO open PATCHES. TOOLS never opens by default. On a SAVE page again: the next SAVE page (PATCHES, PATTERNS, MULTIS, TOOLS, round); SELECT moves between the four too. |
| SAVE (hold) | **"OVERWRITE #NN <name>?"** (OCT+ yes), the one dialog of section 3.5: the slot the sound came from, K1 picks another, then OCT+ writes. The footer says HOLD SAVE = SAVE PATCH. **On PATTERNS (0.2.2): "SAVE OVER <name>?" for the pattern slot picked (OCT+ yes: saved straight into the slot under its name, "SAVED P03"; no slot is ever empty, a factory default counts and keeps its factory name); on MULTIS: "SAVE OVER <name>?" for the multi picked (OCT+ yes: "SAVED M05", the name kept), an empty multi slot goes through the NAME screen; the footer says HOLD SAVE = SAVE PATTERN / SAVE MULTI there.** |
| EDIT, HOME, FX, ENV, LFO, ARP, SEQ, SCL, GLO | **Leave the SAVE pages for that page, saving nothing, always** (2026-10-12: EDIT no longer renames). A dialog open (OVERWRITE, RESTORE, RESTORE ALL) is cancelled the same way as OCT- (nothing written, "NOT SAVED" shown once); so is the NAME screen. OCT+ stays YES and OCT- NO in dialogs. **The device has no rename** (the web editor renames; open question for the owner). |

**RESTORE ALL lives on SAVE > TOOLS** (the fourth SAVE page, next to CLEAR and INIT: K3, card RESTORE, the panel says "RESTORE ALL: every slot gets its factory sound"): K3 right picks it, OCT+ asks **"RESTORE ALL PATCHES?"** ("SAVED SOUNDS ARE LOST"; OCT+ yes), and every saved record goes, the 128 slots hold their factory sounds. It is a SAVE-family action about the patch list, so it sits with the other two destructive tools rather than in MENU > SYSTEM, where ERASE DATA (which also wipes settings, multis and patterns) stays. The card is faded with the reason FACTORY when no slot holds a saved sound. It writes the store chunk by chunk (each chunk one power-cut-safe write; a cut leaves some slots restored and the others as they were, never a damaged one: run it again). Stopped transport only.

**Storage.** The PATCH store lives in **sixteen chunks**, sixteen storage objects (`OBJ_PATCH0..5` and, since DUELL 0.2.15, `OBJ_PATCHX0..9`, ids 33..42; ids 30..32 are unused, 32 is the FM6 voice bank's), an A/B sector pair each, one chunk of records each (`upreset.c`, **3,584 bytes of payload a chunk**, which end at sector offset 0xF00 where the updater scans: a static assert in `upreset.c` and a test enforce it). Chunks 0..2 sit at 0xC8000..0xCDFFF (24 KiB, USR3's old place: owner's choice), chunks 3 and 4 at 0xDC000..0xDFFFF (Felucca's retired 32-slot preset banks, 16 KiB; nothing else uses those sectors: they lie in the user-data range below the OTA staging at 0xE0000), chunk 5 in the retired FM6 patch bank's pair, 0x9F000 / 0xFE000, and **chunks 6..15 (DUELL 0.2.15) at 0xA6000..0xB9FFF (ten pairs, 80 KiB)**. 0xA0000..0xA5FFF is the three user wavetables (0.2.14), **0xBA000..0xC7FFF (56 KiB, 14 sectors) is free** for later use. **The store holds only SAVED records, keyed by slot (0..127: the 7-bit slot id of the protocol and of MIDI program change), and every one of the 128 slots can always be written, whatever the other 127 hold.** The biggest record there can be is **434 bytes** (0.2.19; 435 before: the mixer level is no longer stored) (an FM6 sound with its 112-byte patch, a SEQ-mode arp with all sixteen values and a full 16-step pattern, End B and every one of the 127 values off its default); 8 of them fit a chunk with its 4-byte header, so 16 chunks always hold 16 x 8 = **128 records of any kind** (`PT_MAX` = `UP_SLOTS`, a static assert). There is no size rule and no message about size or room: a chunk takes a new record only under 8 records, a record that is rewritten stays in its chunk (it always fits). The only way a save can fail is a flash fault (SAVE ERROR). A store that already holds more than 8 records in a chunk (written under older rules) stays readable and takes new records in the other chunks. **RAM: no copy of the chunks.** The unit keeps a directory of 128 entries (20 bytes: chunk, offset, length, revision, name, engine, category; built at boot from the chunks, 2,560 bytes) and reads a record from flash when it is loaded or edited (a ring of four); a save loads the one chunk it changes into a 3,584-byte work buffer and writes it back. **The factory fallback is read through the factory sound reader** (`sounds.c`, the same as a PATCHES load of a factory sound: `sl_values` / `sl_load` in `upreset.c`): a slot with no record costs no flash. A record is **sparse** like the factory sound format: engine, category, name, End B's 7-byte block, then only the values that differ from the engine's INIT ((id, value) pairs, or from 17 values on a 16-byte bitmap and one byte each: whichever is shorter; the AK block's values likewise from 3 on), the 16-step pattern if it has one, an FM6 sound's patch (112 bytes) and the **AK block** (arp, keys, SEQ pattern; below) inside the record. **0.2.17: the shadow marker (flag 16, two bytes) is retired:** it is never written, and a record that carries one (made by the 0.2.15..0.2.16 betas) is read with the marker ignored, as a record of its slot. A save rewrites only the chunk that changed, with the standard commit-last write (erase the other sector, payload, commit record last, read back): a power cut leaves the old chunk (tests: a cut at every step of a SAVE, a RESTORE and a RESTORE ALL). A record that no longer fits its chunk (cannot happen below the guarantee; old stores) is written to another one first (its revision byte is higher) and cut from the old one after; if the cut falls between, the newer revision wins at the next boot. A typical sound takes 55 to 90 bytes (an FM6 sound about 170 to 190; an ordinary arp adds about 9, a SEQ-mode arp with a 12-step pattern about 120), so 128 typical records use about 10 KB of the 57 KB of payload room. Felucca's 32-slot banks and its FM6-patch object are no longer used as such. **Stored-format change in 0.2.17: none to the records** (the shadow marker only stops being written); the old "stored patch" of the 0.2.15 / 0.2.16 betas becomes the saved record of the slot number it has, whatever it was a copy of.

**Arp, keys and SEQ pattern travel with a patch** (owner, 2026-10-12). Saving a patch (hold SAVE: the sound over a slot) stores what the part holds now: the **arp** (when ON: mode incl. SEQ, rate, octaves, gate, swing, probability, hold, order), the **keys** (CHRD, VOIC when not default; never a scale: the scale is the instrument's) and, when the arp mode is **SEQ**, the **SEQ pattern** (the first 16 steps with note offset, accent, slide, ratchet, nudge, velocity, chance, and LEN at most 16, DIV, SWING, GATE, QUANTIZE). ARP TARGET is the sound's own and travels as it always did. A save of a factory sound stores the part's arp, not the factory sound's (a factory arp the player switched off stays off; an edited rate is what loads). Loading a slot (PATCHES K1, the PRESETS knob, MIDI program change; MULTI is separate and unchanged) applies what it carries under the factory sounds' **lend rule**: the part's own arp, keys and pattern go aside, the next sound loaded gives them back (unless they were edited since: the edit stays), and a patch that carries them replaces the lent ones. An older record that still holds a SCALE in its keys is read and the scale discarded. A patch **without** the block (every older patch, and one saved with the arp off, default keys and no SEQ arp) leaves the part's arp, keys and steps as they are. Steps 17..64 are never touched. Format: an additive block at the end of the record (flag 32; `upreset.c` "the AK block"), sparse against the defaults, steps in the packing of the MULTI and the pattern slots; a record without it loads as before. The editor's patch write (UP_PUT) carries no block (it drops one); RENAME and the FM6 patch write keep it. Firmware before this reads a record with the block as damaged (nothing released yet).
**Program change:** program n = slot n, #n + 1 (0..127), in **bank 0 and in bank 1 alike** (one list of slots now: the two banks were two lists before; bank select is read as it was, any other bank is ignored); a slot with no saved record loads its factory sound. Whatever TYPE the page shows, so a program number never moves with a browsing filter; a saved sound in a slot keeps its program number.
**Editor protocol (wire compatible inside this firmware generation, `web/EDITOR_PROTOCOL.md` "v2: the patch slots"):** `UP_LIST` / `UP_GET` report every slot as holding a sound (its record's, or the factory sound's) and add a SAVED byte per slot after the categories; `UP_STORE` and `UP_PUT` overwrite a slot; `UP_ERASE` of a slot is **RESTORE** (rc 0 and no write when it holds its factory sound); the capacity tag `58 01` still says 128; the list total byte saturates at 127.
