No command lists free places any more (an old editor that counted `used` reads 128 and never refuses a save).

---

### 3.7 PATTERNS: DUELL's own 16 factory patterns (built 2026-10-12; replaces Felucca's 13)

SAVE > PATTERNS is ONE list of 32 slots, P01..P32, for the whole device (no part chooser: LOAD goes to the selected part, SAVE saves it). All 32 are overwritable and none is empty: they hold the **32 default patterns** as ordinary slots on a new unit and after ERASE DATA, and ERASE of a slot gives its default back ("ALREADY DEFAULT" when it holds it). There is no second list: the arp's SEQ mode plays the part's own steps, which came from a slot or were played; a factory sound's PAT(n) (preset_t.pat) is the number of a slot. The set is our own: generic rhythmic and melodic idioms of dance, funk, dub and pop music, none copying a recognisable melody and none imitating a kick, snare or hat (this is not a drum machine); 8 to 32 steps (LEN, DIV per pattern). Defined in `engines.c` (`PATTERNS[]`, PATTERNS_PROPOSAL.md), names at most 12 characters. The slots are stored compactly (`patslot.c`, format PTS3, three flash objects of 11, 11 and 10 slots that share 3584 bytes each); a SAVE that does not fit its object says PATTERN LIST FULL.

**Offsets, not absolute notes.** Every note is **C4 (60) + an offset in semitones**, 0 = rest. That is exactly what the SEQUENCER arp mode reads (note - 60 per step), so a pattern loaded into a part also works as the SEQ arp pattern on the held notes, on any engine. In the step sequencer the patterns play from C4 (OCT- or SCL TRANS move them); **not while the part's arp MODE is SEQUENCER (0.2.12):** then the steps are the arp's, played on the held keys only, and PLAY does not also play them from C4 (`seq.c seq_play`; the transport, the automation and STEP PREVIEW go on as before). Flags used: accent, slide, tie (holds the previous note; the SEQ arp treats a tie as a rest). A slot sets the part's LEN, DIV, SWING, GATE and QUANTIZE with its steps (the defaults: SWING 0, GATE 64, DIV 1/16, a few on 1/8 or 1/8T).

| Slot | Name | Idiom |
|---|---|---|
| P01 | MORODER OCT | Disco / Italo bass: 16ths jumping root and octave, accent on each beat, the last beat walks up to the 5th and b7. |
| P02 | ACID LINE | 303-style acid line: 16ths with rests, octave jumps, accents and slides. |
| P03 | OFFBEAT | House bass: the root on the off-beat 8ths only, the last one an octave up. |
| P04 | TECHNO RUMBL | Techno rumble: three 16ths after every beat, the beat empty (the kick sits there). |
| P05 | TRANCE ROLL | Trance / psy rolling bass: unbroken 16ths, the octave on the off-beat 8ths. |
| P06 | 8TH PULSE | Straight 8th-note pulse (new wave / driving rock bass), the last 8th on the 5th. |
| P07 | ARP UPDOWN | Minor-triad arpeggio climbing two octaves and falling back, 16ths. |
| P08 | BERLIN RUN | Berlin-school sequencer run: the root as a pedal on every other 16th, a figure climbing and falling between. |
| P09 | DETROIT STAB | Detroit / house chord-stab rhythm: syncopated hits (gaps 3-3-4-3-3), root, minor 3rd, 5th. |
| P10 | FUNK 16TH | Funk bass groove: the one, ghost 16ths, octave pops, a walk-down fill. |
| P11 | DUB HALFTIME | Dub half-time: few long tied notes, the root, a 5th below after the space. |
| P12 | SKANK | Reggae skank: the off-beat of beats 2 and 4, a bubbling 5th in between. |
| P13 | GALLOP | Galloping bass: an 8th and two 16ths on every beat, the last beat up a minor 3rd. |
| P14 | PENTA RIFF | Minor-pentatonic riff: root, b3, 4, 5, b7 (and the b7 below), a slide into the 4th. |
| P15 | BREAKBEAT | Breakbeat syncopation: the hits of a broken kick / snare pattern, accents on the backbeats. |
| P16 | FOUR FLOOR | Four on the floor: one hit on every beat, a kick-style gate for any sound. |

**Random pattern (not built).** A one-percentage roll of a pattern (the morph AMOUNT) is cheap in code but has no honest control: the PATTERNS page uses all four knobs (SLOT, LOAD, ERASE, SAVE) and the label law forbids a second meaning. Open decision: where ROLL lives (see Appendix C).

## 4. MIDI (any MIDI device)

Goal: nothing to configure, everything automatable, no stuck or lost notes.

### 4.1 Channels
- Part A on channel 1, part B on channel 2 (default). **Each part's channel is settable 1..16 on GLO > MIDI (KNOB 1 P1 CH, KNOB 2 P2 CH; built 2026-10-12)** and is what the part receives (notes, bend, CCs, program change, bank select) and what the keys send; channels no part uses are ignored. Both parts on one channel: part 1 takes it. In LAYER part 1's channel plays both parts and part 2's is not heard. **MIDI IN** (GLO > MIDI, KNOB 3; the clock source AUTO / INT / USB / TRS is CLK on GLO > TEMPO KNOB 4 since 0.2.9; no longer in MENU; renamed 2026-10-12 from PARTS / SEL, which nobody could read): **OWN CH** (each part listens on its own channel, set with P1 CH / P2 CH) or **SELECTED** (every channel plays the part chosen with ALGORITHM, for a single keyboard; the card says SELECT, the line under it says what that means). Stored as before (0 / 1, the project's `G_ROUTE`); the former blocks CH5-8 .. CH13-16 are replaced by the settable channels. Kept in the settings ([15][0] part 1, [15][1] part 2: 0 = the default, else the channel 1..16; older settings load with 1 and 2), not in the MULTIs.
- **The sidechain's MIDI setup is the device's, not the sound's (0.2.22, owner).** The ducker's trigger SRC is PUMP, PART or MIDI (stored 0, 1, 2; it was PUMP, PART, CH1..CH16 = 2..17, with a NOTE card). A part whose SRC is MIDI ducks on a note-on of its **SIDECHAIN channel and note**, set on **GLO > SIDECHAIN**: K1 P1 CH (1..16, default 10), K2 P1 NOTE (ANY or a note, default 36 = C2, the GM kick), K3 P2 CH, K4 P2 NOTE. Those four are settings of the unit, like the parts' MIDI channels: kept in the settings record (`favorites.factory[15][14..17]`, 0 = the default, out of range = the default; fx.c `sc_midi[]` is the RAM copy, ui_input.c `sc_get` / `sc_put` / `sc_sync`), in no patch, multi or project, so a load never changes them; they are shown and editable whatever the SRC is (PUMP too), both parts at once (both badges). PART FX OFF hides them (the ducker is skipped). OCT- held + a knob: that card to its default. The rest of the ducker (SRC, RATE, THR, DEPTH, ATK, RLS, CURVE) stays a patch parameter on FX > DUCK 1/2 and 2/2; with SRC MIDI the second card of DUCK 1/2 is empty (RATE is PUMP's, THR is PART's). The id P_DKNOTE is retired (a blank, never read). Stored formats: the multi record version is 6 (MU_VER), the project revision 4 (PROJ_REV), the patch chunk version 2 (upreset.c PT_VER): a record of the earlier formats is refused, as at every stored-meaning change before the alpha; the factory multis were rewritten (tools/factory_multis_ducksrc.py). Editor: MENU settings 38..41 (P1 CH, P1 NOTE, P2 CH, P2 NOTE, tab SIDECHAIN), the SIDECHAIN card on the MIDI tab of the GLOBAL column (the unit lists SIDECHAIN with its MIDI pages); the DUCK card of the FX tab keeps the rest.
- MIDI LEARN is on GLO > MIDI LEARN (KNOB 1: right = ON, left = OFF; KNOB 2 right = CLEAR every learned CC, the card counts them; OCT+ done, OCT- clears the picked CC; MENU's LEARN CLEAR row is gone); it was GLO held + D4.
- Keys on the FM-1 send on the selected part's channel.

### 4.2 Messages in
- Notes with velocity; note-on velocity 0 = note-off; running status; correct all-notes-off (CC123), all-sound-off (CC120), reset-all-controllers (CC121), sustain (CC64).
- Pitch bend with RPN 0 bend range (default 2 semitones, max 24). Channel aftertouch and polyphonic key pressure (aftertouch as matrix source).
- Program change plus bank select (CC0/CC32): recall, without audio dropouts (see 4.2a).
- MIDI clock, start, continue, stop, song position pointer. Clock from USB or TRS MIDI; external clock locks tempo-synced LFOs, envelopes and the arp. Start/stop only restarts what the PLAY/STOP option (2.3) says. Clock jitter smoothing. **The arp on an external clock (2026-10-12, owner report with an Akai Force):** the arp's held-note list follows note-on / note-off at once, whatever the clock does (a note released with no clock running is gone; Start, Continue and Stop never create or keep notes); only the arp's STEPPING waits for pulses. It steps on the pulses of the selected source while the transport runs, and also on a clock that runs without a Start; after a Stop (0xFC, or this unit's STOP) it waits for the next Start / Continue even if pulses keep coming (HOLD keeps its latched chord). With no pulse arriving the arp is silent (a key pressed sounds its first note once, gated in real time, then waits) and the screen says so: the TEMPO page's K1 card and the MIDI page's CLK card show `NO CLK` under the value (the card is 57 px wide; the MIDI page's CLOCK line says `USB: NO CLOCK`) when no pulse arrived for 1 s, `USB` / `TRS` while pulses arrive; INT shows nothing. A multi's stored steps are the user's pattern: an external Start plays them like PLAY does (e.g. M01 / M02 part 2), with or without keys held. Code: `midi_clock.c clock_state`, `seq.c events_block`; tests: midi_control_test `arp_ext_clock_test`.
  **CLK AUTO (DUELL 0.2.14, owner; the default):** the internal clock runs until a MIDI clock arrives on USB or TRS, then that port's clock is followed; when its pulses stop for 1 s the internal clock takes over again. Lock: a port locks after **3 pulses 8..80 ms apart (24 ppqn at about 31..312 BPM) within 250 ms**, or at once on a **Start / Continue** from it (one stray pulse, or pulses far apart, never switch). While locked the other port is ignored completely (its pulses, Start, Stop, Continue). A Stop from a port that is not locked does nothing. **No stop on a switch:** INT to USB / TRS: the transport keeps its state and the steps keep their phase (the pulses go on from where the steps are, within an audio block); the tempo is the internal one until six pulses have measured the master's, then the master's (and TEMPO follows it, as for a fixed source). USB / TRS to INT (no pulse for 1 s): a pulse that is late does not stall the music (past the expected pulse the steps run on in real time at the measured tempo), so the internal clock takes over at the last external tempo (TEMPO, whole BPM; the step phases rescaled to it) and the current phase, with no gap; a playing transport plays on, a stopped one (after the master's Stop) stays stopped. A master that pauses without a Stop and comes back within 1 s: the steps went on, the returning pulses continue from there (no catch-up). Start / Stop / Continue follow the locked port exactly as for a fixed source (Start restarts from step 1, Stop stops and the arp waits for Start / Continue while pulses keep coming, Continue resumes); with INT active, PLAY works as on INT (the count-in included; a lock cancels a count-in in progress). The arp, the tempo-synced LFOs / envelopes / delay and PUMP follow whichever clock is active. The NO CLK state does not exist in AUTO. The screen: the CLK card shows `AUTO` with the source it plays on under it (`INT`, `USB`, `TRS`); the TEMPO card is EXT with `USB` / `TRS` under it while external (else editable; `AUTO USB` does not fit the card's line); the MIDI page's CLOCK line says `AUTO: INT (OWN TEMPO)` / `AUTO: USB (MIDI CLOCK)`; each switch shows `CLOCK USB` / `CLOCK TRS` / `CLOCK INT` in the header once. **The fixed INT / USB / TRS behave as before**; changing the CLK choice stops the transport only when the clock it plays on changes (INT -> AUTO with no clock arriving, AUTO on USB -> USB and the like play on). **Stored:** the project's `G_CLOCK` as before (0 INT, 1 USB, 2 TRS) plus **3 AUTO**; the parameter's default is AUTO, so a new unit, ERASE DATA and RESTORE LAST OFF start on AUTO; an autosave or a backup that holds 0 / 1 / 2 keeps it (an old INT cannot be told from the old default, so it stays INT: no migration; turn CLK to AUTO once). A multi never holds the clock. (0.2.13 reading a 3 clamps it to TRS.) Code: `midi_clock.c clock_auto / clock_lock / clock_unlock / clock_choice / clock_src`, `seq.c events_block`; tests: midi_control_test `auto_clock_test`.
- USB MIDI class-compliant, so any USB host (computer, hardware sequencer, controller with a USB host port) sees DUELL as a device with 2 channels. TRS MIDI IN kept (type per Felucca; **[verify]** type A/B).

### 4.2a Program change, bank select, mod wheel (built 2026-10-11)
- Channels: as for the notes. MIDI IN OWN CH: each part's channel (GLO > MIDI, default 1 and 2), other channels ignored; MIDI IN SELECTED: any channel, the selected part. LAYER: part 1's channel only (SELECTED: any).
- BANK SELECT: CC0 (MSB) and CC32 (LSB) are remembered per channel and applied at that channel's next PC: bank = MSB x 128 + LSB (power-on 0; CC121 does not reset it). They are never parameter CCs nor MIDI LEARN targets.
- PC in DUEL and STEREO: a SOUND for the channel's part only (the other part and this part's own settings, LEVEL, PAN, sends, stay; the arp, keys and SEQ pattern change only when the patch carries them (lend rule, section 3.6), otherwise they and the steps stay; the selection stays). Program n = patch slot n (0-based: slot #n + 1 of the PATCHES list; a slot holds its saved record or else its factory sound, section 3.6), in bank 0 and in bank 1 alike, whatever the TYPE filter (FAV, a category) shows, so a program number never moves with a browsing filter. Other banks and programs past 127: ignored.
- PC in LAYER: bank 0, program 0..15 = MULTI M01..M16, loaded exactly as the MULTI page's LOAD (both parts, MODE and globals the multi holds, each MORPH at fader A with its End B). An empty slot, programs 16..127 and any other bank: ignored.
- A recall is queued by the audio interrupt (8 waiting at most; more are dropped) and run by the main loop, as the MORPH's CC actions. It loads as the PRESETS knob / MULTI LOAD does: the part's notes off, an engine change fades, the MORPH starts again at A; a playing transport keeps running. No PROGRAM CHANGE is sent out.
- MOD WHEEL (CC1): always (no switch) also moves the MORPH fader of the channel's part, fader = CC value (0..127), through the same queue and TARGET rules as CC 20 (TARGET BOTH: both parts); LAYER: both parts' faders. It stays the matrix's MODW source unchanged.

### 4.3 Messages out
- The keys, and every knob move, send a CC on the part's channel, so any recorder or sequencer can record knob automation and play it back.
- No echo of received CC (no loops). MIDI THRU of notes off by default.

### 4.4 CC map (identical on both channels; fixed, no learn needed; MIDI LEARN kept for custom)
Standard: 0 / 32 bank select (4.2a), 1 mod wheel (MODW matrix source and the MORPH fader, 4.2a), 5 glide, 7 level (the MIXER LEVEL, 0.2.19: the player's volume for the part; the sound's TRIM is not a CC), 10 pan, 64 sustain, 71 resonance, 72 release, 73 attack, 74 brightness (cutoff; on PHASE LOFI VOICE WHEEL PHYS FM6 WAVE the multimode filter's CUT, 0..127 over its whole dial, 30 Hz .. the open end (0.2.9; before, 64 = centre = OFF), CC71 its RES, section 2.5; a MIDI LEARN map on CUT spans the new dial the same way), 75 decay, 91 reverb send, 93 chorus send, 94 delay send.
DUELL block (all 0-127, never 14-bit in the first build):

| CC | Function | CC | Function |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 20 | MORPH fader | 24 | KEEP (>=64 fires) |
| 21 | ROLL A (>=64 fires) | 25 | AMOUNT |
| 22 | ROLL B (>=64 fires) | 26-29 | EDIT page KNOB 1-4 |
| 23 | UNDO (1..63 = End A, 64..127 = End B, fires once; 0 re-arms) | 30-33 | ENV page KNOB 1-4 |
| 34-37 | LFO page KNOB 1-4 | 38-41 | FX page KNOB 1-4 |
| 102-109 | Matrix amounts slot 1-8 | 110 | Arp on/off |

As built (2026-10-11, firmware midi_control.c): 0 / 32 bank select; 1 MODW + MORPH fader; 20 MORPH fader; 21 ROLL A, 22 ROLL B (>=64 fires); 23 UNDO; 24 unassigned (KEEP removed); 25 AMOUNT; 111 TARGET (0-42 PART 1, 43-85 PART 2, 86-127 BOTH); 112 MODE (0-42 DUEL, 43-85 STEREO, 86-127 LAYER, any channel); 113 SMART, 114 ARP, 115 PITCH (>=64 on); 116 BALANCE, 117 XFADE, 118 DUEL note (any channel); 26-41 and 102-110 not built. Program change: 4.2a.

Page knobs give every parameter a CC through the page structure: a controller's "page" can follow a DUELL page. **[verify]** against Felucca's `midi_control.c` and the parameter table; numbers may shift if they clash with Felucca's learn defaults.

### 4.5 Acceptance tests (host tests, no hardware)
1. 2000 random notes across both channels: no hanging notes, correct polyphony and steal order.
2. Clock at 30-300 BPM, with jitter: tempo lock within 1 BPM.
3. Every CC round-trips: in produces the parameter value; the knob move produces the CC out.
4. PC + bank: preset loads in under one audio block of silence.
5. MPE is out of scope.

---

## 5. UI details

- HOME page 1: both parts side by side: name, engine, level; the active part highlighted. KNOB 1 LEVEL A, KNOB 2 LEVEL B, KNOB 3 PAN / WIDTH of the selected part (not shown in DUEL), KNOB 4 CUTOFF of the selected part. HOME page 2: MORPH (section 3.5).
- Chord keys and scale: the SCL pages (section 1): one global SCALE / ROOT / OFF-SCALE KEYS for the instrument (section 2.3); MIDI notes follow the same snap rule as keys (SNAP moves an out-of-scale note to the nearest scale note, a tie down; PLAY passes it), MIDI OUT sends the note that sounds; there is no CC for the scale.
- Header: part A / B indicator, tempo (always read-only except on GLO), morph position as a small bar.
- Every knob card shows its parameter name and value; a changed-by-morph value shows a small marker.
- No page changes meaning with hidden state. The tempo bug (SELECT changing tempo on some pages) is a spec violation of 1.1 and has an emulator test: turn SELECT on every page, assert the tempo is unchanged.

---

## 5a. Part colours and the part badge (owner's request, 2026-10-10)

**Naming:** on the screen and in the manual the parts are **1** and **2**. Where this spec says part A / part B it means part 1 / part 2. The morph's two ends keep the letters **A** and **B**, so the two never mix.

**Part colour.** Every palette gets a second theme colour. A palette today has five colours (BG, SURF, TEXT, THEME, ACCENT; `tools/gen_ui_palettes.py`). DUELL adds THEME_2, so part 1 uses THEME (values, gauges, curves, selection) and part 2 uses THEME_2. Swapping the part with ALGORITHM changes the whole screen's theme colour, so it is obvious which part you are on. The pairs are chosen to be clearly different from each other and to look good together, tuned to the unit's shell colours (the owner's unit is purple):

| Palette | Part 1 | Part 2 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| VIOLET | purple | pink |
| ICE | blue | teal / cyan |
| AMBER | amber | red-orange |
| GREEN | green | lime / yellow |
| ROSE | pink | purple |
| NIGHT | phosphor green | cyan |
| PAPER | dark green | terracotta |
| HI-CON | yellow | cyan |
| GREY | white | warm grey |
| MONO | solid white | white outline (no colour, so the badge is solid against outlined) |

The exact values are a design item: the build produces a preview sheet (PNG) of every palette in both part colours, on the HOME, EDIT, GLO and MORPH pages, for the owner to judge and adjust. Contrast checks of `gen_ui_palettes.py --report` apply to both colours.

**Part badge.** In the top-left corner of the screen, two numbers side by side: **1 2**. The selected part's number is bright, in that part's colour (a filled badge, as the single track badge today); the other is faded (the palette's DIM). Swapping the part swaps which is lit.
- With the morph TARGET set to BOTH (section 3.4a) both numbers are lit, so you can see that the morph moves both parts; with SELECTED, only the selected part's number is lit. 0.2.9 (owner 2026-10-14): both are lit only where both parts are the target: the MORPH page and PERFORM in BOTH (and the developer TEST screen); every other page shows the selected part's number alone (MORPH SET too), a GLOBAL page both as before.
- The badge never carries a second meaning; it only says which part(s) the screen is about.

---

## 5b. Stereo modes, two-part screens, and more panorama (owner's requests, 2026-10-10)

### 5b.1 Three output modes (replaces the two-mode switch of section 2.2)
One setting, **MODE**, with three choices. It is one of the most important settings, so it is on **HOME's second sub-page** (first knob), always one SELECT turn from HOME, and saved with the settings:
- **DUEL** (default, the dawless mode): part 1 is hard left, part 2 hard right, exactly. Everything stereo inside a part (chorus, insert, reverb sends, spread) is **folded to that part's side**: nothing of part 1 may leak to the right channel or the other way round. A test sends a loud part 1 and asserts the right channel is digital silence (and the reverse), with every effect on.
- **DUEL, effect by effect (measured 2026-10-12, `mode_test.c` t_leakmap: one part sounding, the other channel is digital silence, exactly 0, for every case):** each part is dual mono with its own chain and its own buses (A: part 1, B: part 2), the stereo return of a bus is summed to mono and put on the part's side only. CHORUS (one tap there: the STEREO CHORUS of section 5k works in STEREO and LAYER only), DELAY, ROOM / SPRING / HALL / SHIM / RESO / PLATE (0.2.14) sends: work per part, isolated. All 10 INSERT types (0.2.14: TAPE and VINYL too), DIST, EQ, DUCKER, BASS+ (per channel): mono per part, work. PERFORM's master effects (section 5e): each channel its own (part 1 left, part 2 right), work. BASS MONO (0.2.19, section 5h) does not run in DUEL (its MENU row is not shown). **Not heard in DUEL, shown faded with the reason DUEL (stored values kept for STEREO / LAYER):** PAN (and the matrix's / LFO's PAN), SPREAD (VOICE 3/3) and the delay's SPREAD (DELAY 2/2: nothing to spread). The delay MODE works in DUEL since 0.2.22, mono (section 5c.2): PING-PONG there is the plain delay, RANDOM and OCT UP run centred on each part's side. The delay's MODULATION (DELAY 2/2 DEPTH, RATE, section 5c.2) works in DUEL as a time wobble of each part's own delay (no stereo widening there: a part has one side); the two buses keep their own LFO state. The metronome click (audio.c) goes to both channels by design.
- **STEREO**: both parts are full stereo with free pan, width and the extra panorama controls below.
- **LAYER** (new): the two parts play together as one instrument: every note (keys and MIDI on channel 1) triggers both parts at once, their sounds are summed into a full stereo field, for a much more complex sound. Part 2 follows the same notes, velocity and bend; each part keeps its own engine, level, pan, envelopes, LFOs, matrix and insert. The MIDI channel of part 2 is ignored in LAYER (or follows the setting OMNI); **each part runs its own arp** (revised 2026-10-10): the notes you play (keys, MIDI channel 1) go to BOTH parts as held notes, and each part handles them by its own settings: a part with its arp on arpeggiates them (with its own arp mode, rate, octaves, SEQ pattern and ARP TARGET), a part with its arp off plays them directly. So one layer can be arpeggiated while the other holds the chord, or each layer can run a different arp. The morph TARGET can be BOTH. Voice budget: each note uses a voice in each part, so the polyphony halves; the screen shows the number.
Switching between modes is click-free (crossfade of the pan law, 50 ms).
- **MODE page, KNOB 2-4 (owner's request, 2026-10-11; saved in the settings ([15][5..7]) and in each MULTI, version 2):** BALANCE -100..+100 (0 = off; linear level balance at the part level stage, right fades part 1, left part 2; all modes; CC 116, 64 = 0), XFADE 0..100 % (0 = off, bit for bit; LAYER only: a note's velocity v gives part 1 cos and part 2 sin of pi/2 x v/127, blended to 1 by XFADE; a per-voice gain; keys count as velocity 100; CC 117), SPLIT OFF or a note C-1..G9 (LAYER only: below it part 1 plays alone, at or above it part 2; keys and MIDI channel 1; a held note ends as it started; CC 118, 0 = OFF; split first, then XFADE).

### 5b.2 More panorama in STEREO and LAYER mode (not in DUEL)
- Each part's PAN and WIDTH are modulation destinations (matrix: PAN already exists; add WIDTH), and LFO 1 and LFO 2 can drive them through the matrix.
- **Panorama inside the engines**: wherever an engine has a mixer of several sources, each source gets its own pan, so a part spreads across the soundscape. Survey (to do in the build, per engine, from the code): ANALOG (osc A, osc B), TRIO (osc 1-3), PHASE (main, sub), WHEEL (the partials, a pan law across the drawbars), FM6 (carriers: spread the carrier operators), PHYS, NOISE, VOICE, LOFI (voices), WAVE. Result: a table of "pan points" per engine and which are cheap. Each pan point is a continuous parameter and morph-randomisable in STEREO and LAYER mode only. Felucca already has SPREAD (voices of a POLY/UNISON part around PAN) and per-voice stereo in some engines: reuse.
- Goal: in STEREO and LAYER mode the sound should be "very stereo" without sounding phasey; every new pan point is checked for mono compatibility (mono sum must not cancel more than 3 dB).

### 5b.3 Redesign of the two-part screens
Felucca's mixer, global and song screens were drawn for four tracks. With two parts they leave empty space. Redesign: the **HOME mixer** with two wide, readable strips (name/engine, level meter, pan, mute, solo; 0.2.14: two cells side by side with GLO's meters, section 3.5), **GLO's main page** (step 6, below) and the SONG/track lanes as two large lanes, all at readable sizes; layout lint (0 findings) in every palette; the badge and part colours of section 5a.

**GLO main page (owner's feedback, 2026-10-12; reworked the same day, built; 0.2.21: removed from the page round, see section GLO above: what follows was the layout of QUICK GLO up to 0.2.23 (0.2.24: K1 LEVEL 1, K2 PAN 1, K3 LEVEL 2, K4 PAN 2, the mutes on OCT- / OCT+ with GLO held), and is the MIXER page's meters):** the former hold-GLO layer was the page a tap of GLO opened (`graph GR_GLO`), and **it is no longer a locked layer: the keys play notes**, as on every page; the key-based SOLO / MUTE / ALL / TAP are gone, and **SOLO was removed entirely (owner, 2026-10-12: MUTE is clearer; `perf_solo` deleted, it had no other user; nothing in MIDI or the editor protocol carried a solo id, so no protocol id changed).** Cards: **K1 MUTE 1, K2 MUTE 2, K3 LEVEL 1, K4 LEVEL 2.** K1 and K2 each own one part and use the ROLL gesture with ONE detent (0.2.3: a mute must be instant; one action per twist): **one click either way flips the mute (muted becomes sounding and the other way round).** The mute is the part's latched `P_MUTE` (the same the HOME mixer shows; stored in a MULTI, left alone by sound loads). The cards say `OFF` / `ON` (ON = muted, in the part's colour) (no building-up display: the first detent acts). K3 / K4 are the parts' LEVEL (recorded as on MIXER; MIDI LEARN picks them). Panel: per part one tall cell with the part's badge and colour: `LIVE`, or `MUTED` in the key colour with the badge dark; the muted part's level card and meter are greyed; **between the columns a dB level meter per part**: its own level before MASTER (what the part adds to the mix after LEVEL, DIST, INSERT, EQ and DUCK; in DUEL the part's bus with its delay / reverb return too), -48..0 dBFS, the bar follows the peak up at once and falls at 36 dB/s, a 2 px tick holds the highest peak 1.2 s, the part's colour up to -6 dBFS, the accent from -6, red from -1 dBFS (a colour like the part's own gives way to the text colour: HI-CON's red part 1), a lamp over the bar stays on 1.5 s after a peak past -1 dBFS. Flat colours only. Cost: nothing new in STEREO / LAYER (the per-part peak exists since the MIXER's meter); in DUEL two compares per sample while this page is shown. SELECT, the page buttons, OCT work as on every page; GLO held + PLAY = restart from the top. The header shows the parts only (the multi number is not shown on this page). **No 'unmute / unsolo all' control:** every printed label on the panel already means something else (OCT-/OCT+ are octave); the opposite twist unmutes. MIDI LEARN: K1/K2 pick `T1 MUTE` / `T2 MUTE`, K3/K4 `T1 LEVEL` / `T2 LEVEL`.

**TEMPO page (GLO, 2026-10-12; the old CLOCK page's place, own page again):** **K1 CLK** (0.2.18, first: the source decides whether the tempo is live), K2 TEMPO and **K3 TAP** (not shown with an external clock; each detent either way is one tap; the slot is `TEMPO_TAP_SLOT`; the GLO + key tap still says `TAP: CLK IS EXT`), K4 SWG; CLK is (the clock source AUTO / INT / USB / TRS, in that order, default AUTO since 0.2.14 (section 4.2, MIDI clock); since 0.2.9 here, it was the MIDI page's K4). The keys play notes.

### 5b.4 MULTI (owner's request, 2026-10-11; replaces SAVE > PROJECT)
Two-part playing is the heart of DUELL, so the multitimbral setup is saved whole in **16 slots, M01..M16** (SAVE > MULTIS, after PATTERNS; the four PROJECT slots A..D are gone). Each slot has a name (up to 12 characters, as projects had: the NAME screen opens on SAVE; EDIT leaves the page, it does not rename).
- **Held, per part (1 and 2):** the sound: engine, preset and every part parameter (filter, envelopes, LFO 1 / 2, FILTER ENV, the matrix, INSERT, EQ, DUCK, voice); the FM6 patch (an FM6 part only); the morph's AMOUNT and End B (as a user preset holds it: seed + AMOUNT + roll bits, 7 bytes, regenerated exactly on load; not the end's values); the arp; the SEQUENCER pattern (all 64 steps, LEN DIV SWING GATE QUANTIZE) and its automation / parameter locks; the mix (the MIXER LEVEL (0.2.19), PAN, MUTE, the sends) and the sound's TRIM (it is part of the sound).
- **Held, global:** TEMPO and SWING, CHORUS, DELAY 1/2 and 2/2 (incl. the delay MODE with PING-PONG, SPREAD, the delay modulation's MDEPTH and MRATE, and CHANCE in RANDOM and OCT UP), REVERB (incl. TYPE, SIZE, DAMP, LOCUT and, for SHIM, the SHIMMER page's PITCH and MOD); **a load sets all of these and every part's mixer settings (LEVEL, PAN, MUTE, the sends) from the multi: it never keeps the levels the unit had (0.2.21 states the rule; it was the behaviour since 0.2.19, tested)**; the output MODE (DUEL / STEREO / LAYER; saved in the record, but a load applies it **only when MENU > CONTROL > MODE RECALL is ON** (0.2.11, default OFF: loading a multi, by the page, a program change or the editor, keeps the unit in the mode it is in; until 0.2.22 the CPU TEST multis M13..M16 of developer builds always set theirs, since 0.2.23 every slot follows the setting); BALANCE, XFADE and SPLIT are saved too and follow the same option: a load applies them only when MODE RECALL is ON, else they stay as set); MORPH SET's SMART, ARP, PITCH; the selected part. (TARGET: written, not loaded since 0.2.9, section 3.4a.)
- **Not held (device settings):** colour theme, MIDI channels and MIDI IN routing, clock source, TUNE, display / LED / USB settings, and the **master effects: BASS MONO (0.2.21, owner, 2026-10-16; before, 0.2.19 and 0.2.20 saved and loaded it; 0.2.24: a UNIT setting proper, in the settings record's byte [15][21] with WIDTH, ON at 200 Hz on a new unit; the project's byte 65 is written 0 and not read), COMP (0.2.21), WIDTH (0.2.23; a master saturator existed in 0.2.23 and 0.2.24 and is removed in 0.2.25, section 5m).** A load leaves them as they are and a save does not store them. SPACE and KILL (PERFORM's live values) are never stored; PERF LEAVE decides what a load does to them.
- **Page:** KNOB 1 SLOT (M01..M16; the list shows the stored name, "--" when empty), KNOB 2 LOAD, KNOB 3 ERASE (asks ERASE Mnn?), KNOB 4 SAVE (over a used slot asks OVERWRITE Mnn?, then NAME); OCT+ does the picked action. LOAD asks nothing (as PROJECT's did) and replaces everything held; each part's MORPH goes to fader A with the multi's End B (the patch-load rule). Loading while playing keeps the transport running: all notes off, an engine change fades as usual. SAVE, ERASE and renames need the transport stopped (STOP TO SAVE). The header shows the number of the multi the selected part's pattern came from (with * once edited).
- **Storage (built):** a compact record (version, the values as bytes, the steps sparse: a 64-bit map plus 9 bytes per used step, the FM6 patch only for FM6, End B as its 7-byte block; at most 1916 bytes; the sound and steps always fit, automation beyond the room is cut and the save says AUTOMATION CUT). Two multis per storage object, each object an A/B sector pair with the commit record written last: a save rewrites the pair's other sector, so a power cut keeps both multis of the pair as they were. Flash: M01..M08 at 0x97000..0x9EFFF (the old project sectors), M09..M16 at 0xCE000..0xD5FFF (user sample slot USR3 shrinks from 56 to 24 KiB). Old PROJECT data is not migrated: it reads as empty slots.
- **Factory multis, DUELL 0.2.23 (owner's brief, 2026-10-16): all sixteen slots, in every build.** Made by one generator, `felucca-base/tools/factory_multis.py` (the list: sounds, settings, MODE, split, End B; `build` runs `tests/gen_factory_multis.c`, which loads each sound with the firmware's own code and saves with multi.c's packer; `check` compares the records with the list and runs the multi similarity check: no two multis with the same two sounds, or one sound in the same place with the same MODE and split, or both parts of the same engines and categories in the same MODE). Six records of before are kept byte for byte (the factory multis do not follow reworked sounds): M01 REESE SWAMP, M05 WHISPER TIDE, M07 FINAL REEL, M10 WIRED SNAIL, M11 EERIE SIGNAL, M12 DEAD BUFFER. **0.2.24 (owner, 2026-10-16: his own multis are never dropped or changed):** the owner's ACID ETHER, GARGLE ACID and COLD CHAPEL of 0.2.22 are back, byte for byte, in M09, M14 and M06 (`owner` in the generator: exempt from the similarity check, a pair with one is reported, not a failure; DEAD BUFFER, which holds his edits, is marked too). **0.2.25 (owner): the owner's original COLD CHAPEL is a factory multi again, in M13** (M06 is his edited TINE STRINGS; the six slots M01..M06 are his and stay); it took the slot of BASILDON, the least different from the rest by the similarity measure of the generator (BASILDON is spare data now: `tests/scenes/spare/M13_BASILDON.bin`). Three multis made for 0.2.23 left the sixteen for them, by the check's own measure of closeness to the kept ones: TINE STRINGS and SEVENOAKS (a PAD + KEYS layer, as EERIE SIGNAL) and the 0.2.23 drone that had taken the name COLD CHAPEL (it shared SINGING BOWL with it; BOWL & WIND now). They are spare data: `felucca-base/tests/scenes/spare/`, the generator's SPARE list. Order: 1-4 bass grooves and splits, 5-8 pads, layers and the dry pair, 9-12 the four of 0.2.1 as made, 13-15 performance (one tribute; owner: at most 2 or 3, no Moroder multi), 16 the stress test. Numbers are PATCHES numbers:

  | Slot | Name | Mode | Part 1 | Part 2 | Character |

  | M01 | REESE SWAMP | LAYER | #24 REESE BASS | #5 SQUELCH | Reese wobble at 1/4 under a 1/16 acid loop (kept; the first start's multi) |

  Spare (0.2.24, not in a slot): VELVET TINES (the 0.2.23 TINE STRINGS: #46 VELVET BOW + #83 TINE ROOM, XFADE 37; renamed in 0.2.25, the owner's M06 has the name), BOWL & WIND (#56 SINGING BOWL + #112 MOOR WIND, the 0.2.23 drone), SEVENOAKS (#48 LAMPLIGHT + #81 FROST BELLS, a tribute), BASILDON (#7 FM BASS 2 + #86 TIN TOLL, a tribute; M13 in 0.2.23 .. 0.2.25, out for the owner's COLD CHAPEL: the multi least different from the rest by the similarity measure), MONO DUEL (#15 BREADBIN + #27 SQUARE LEAD in DUEL; M08 in 0.2.24 .. 0.2.25 (M09 in 0.2.23), out for the owner's HAZY TENSOR: "Mono-dual is out"). MONO DUEL was the only factory multi in DUEL: since 0.2.25 none of the sixteen uses DUEL.

  The splits and XFADE (0.2.25: no factory multi is in DUEL any more) are heard with MULTI LOAD SETS MODE (MODE RECALL ON) or set by hand; with the default KEEPS MODE every multi also plays in LAYER. A split is compared with the key as played, before any TRN, so "split F4" is the key F4 whatever the parts' TRN are (the keyboard's lowest key is F3 at octave 0). In LAYER each part maps a played key with its own TRN (seq.c key_on; until 0.2.22 part 2 got part 1's note and the split compared it: "split F3" with part 1 at TRN -12 sat at F4, and FINAL REEL's strings TRN +12 never sounded an octave up from the keys). The CPU TEST multis M13..M16 of developer builds and the factory multis of 0.2.22 are test scenes (`felucca-base/tests/scenes/`, `tests/scenes.h`), so the tests written for them measure and hash what they did.
- **Factory multis (owner, 2026-10-11; history up to 0.2.22).** **The order since DUELL 0.2.13 (owner, 2026-10-14): M01 REESE SWAMP, M02 WHISPER TIDE, M03 EERIE SIGNAL, M04 WIRED SNAIL, M05 DEAD BUFFER, M06 ACID ETHER, M07 COLD CHAPEL, M08 GARGLE ACID, M09 NIGHT ORGAN, M10 FINAL REEL**; a new unit's first start loads M01 REESE SWAMP (in LAYER, with its BALANCE). The slot numbers below are the 0.2.13 ones. M03 **EERIE SIGNAL** (#2 VESPERS on VOICE + #3 JAZZ CLUB on WHEEL), M04 **WIRED SNAIL** (#13 SFORZANDO + #15 GLASS SHARDS, both ANALOG), M05 **DEAD BUFFER** (#17 FROZEN AIR on WAVE + #9 MODEM on NOISE; until 0.2.8 part 1 was #2 STRETCH on GRAIN, retired: rebuilt with `tests/swap_multi_sound.c`, which changed only part 1's engine, index and sound values and kept the owner's own edits of it, part 2, the effects, MODE, tempo and the End B blocks byte for byte), M06 **ACID ETHER** (#3 RUBBER BAND + #0 LADDER BASS, ANALOG) are built into the firmware (M01..M04 until 0.2.12). **DUELL 0.2.11 (owner request, 2026-10-14): six more (M05..M10 until 0.2.12; M01, M02, M07..M10 since 0.2.13), all in LAYER** (the mode the owner can try on the unit alone); numbers below are PATCHES-list numbers (docs/PATCH_NUMBERS.md), the recipes and settings in DUELL/SOUND_RECIPES.md chapter 7. **DUELL 0.2.12 (owner, 2026-10-14): they are played from the keys.** In LAYER a key or a chord makes BOTH parts play, with the transport stopped (the arps run on the internal clock from the key-down, section 2.3); there is no stored step pattern that plays fixed notes on PLAY, and a different key moves both parts. The grooves are arps on the held keys, the riffs SEQUENCER arps (offsets from the held key; a SEQUENCER part's steps are the arp's, PLAY does not play them from C4 as well: section 3.7): M07 **COLD CHAPEL** (#61 EVOLVING PAD + #56 SINGING BOWL, ambient, slow: the pad HOLD ALL, the bowl an UP arp at 1/2, GATE 115, HOLD ALL, MONO; End B opens up), M02 **WHISPER TIDE** (#60 DIGITAL PAD + #52 WHISPER PAD, ambient, slow: the pad HOLD ALL, the choir a RANDOM arp at 1/1 over 2 octaves, HOLD ALL; SHIM reverb), M08 **GARGLE ACID** (#21 GROWL BASS + #5 SQUELCH, 127 BPM: the bass plays the key two octaves down, its filter gargling at 1/8; the acid line a 16-step SEQUENCER arp at 1/16 on the key an octave above it, HOLD OFF), M01 **REESE SWAMP** (#24 REESE BASS + #5 SQUELCH, 124 BPM: the Reese on the key two octaves down, wobbling at 1/4; an 8-step SEQUENCER acid loop at 1/16 two octaves above; no key split stored since 0.2.12; 0.2.13: AMOUNT 17 % on both parts, End B clearly brighter, below), M09 **NIGHT ORGAN** (#91 DRAWBARS + #3 LADDER BASS, house, calm, 121 BPM: the organ's MIN7 chord keys under an 8-step SEQUENCER arp at 1/8 that stabs the chord on the off-beats of beats 1 and 3 (half-time), the bass an off-beat 8-step arp at 1/8, the last an octave up; the stored SWING 17 is the step sequencer's, the arps run straight), M10 **FINAL REEL** (#58 BRASS PAD + #54 STRING MACH an octave up, slow cinematic: both HOLD ALL, the strings with a slower attack, no arp; a large hall). They are built with the firmware's own code by `tests/gen_factory_multis.c` (the sound loaded as PATCHES loads it, then the multi's own settings, saved by multi.c's packer); the same tool prints what a factory multi holds (`dump`) and measures every factory multi's loudness (`loud`: K-weighted master output, the six within about 1.5 dB of WIRED SNAIL, DEAD BUFFER and ACID ETHER; each multi in its own MODE, a chord held: since 0.2.12 also for the six, which have no pattern that plays without a key). Each part keeps its sound's factory End B (seed and AMOUNT) except COLD CHAPEL's two parts, whose seeds were searched so End B stays within about 1 dB and is brighter (the `seeds` mode; `moves SLOT` lists what End B moves on each part), and (0.2.13, owner: "End B barely changes anything" at the sounds' 7 %) M01 REESE SWAMP's: both parts' AMOUNT 17 %, End B rolled at 17 % with the seeds 11710 / 41045, picked with `search` / `eval` so End B is clearly brighter (brightness +55 % with C4 E4 G4 held, +46 % with C4) and within 0.3 dB of End A. Source: the raw version-2 records `felucca-base/tools/factory_multis/M03.bin..M06.bin` (M01..M04 until 0.2.12, the same bytes; built in the emulator, extracted with `tools/flash_dump.py --extract-multis`, renamed in place; each holds its End B block, AMOUNT, steps and settings); `tools/gen_duell_multis.py` checks them (version, name, and that each part's engine + factory index is still the intended sound of `tools/duell_sounds.py`, so a changed sound list stops the build) and writes `build/gen/duell_multis.h` (4447 bytes of flash image since 0.2.12, 4654 in 0.2.11; none of the multi store). A slot whose stored record is empty shows and loads its factory multi (first boot, after a flash erase). SAVE over it, like over any slot, stores an ordinary record that wins from then on and is not touched by firmware updates (the ROM copy is only the default); a rename of an unchanged factory slot stores its record under the new name. ERASE of a saved overwrite deletes the record, so the factory multi is back (after a power cycle too); ERASE of an unchanged factory slot says ALREADY FACTORY and writes nothing (as PATTERNS' factory slots). M11 and M12 have no factory content (empty, "--"); M13..M16 are the four CPU TEST multis (section 6.1) in developer builds only (`DUELL_TEST_MODE=1`): in the release they are empty too, and `duell_multis.h` holds the ten factory multis (4447 bytes). A part stores its sound's values in full, so the multi sounds as built even when a sound is later edited in the list; the engine + index only name the sound.

## 5c. Effects additions (owner's requests, 2026-10-10)

1. **Reverb: lusher stereo space.** Improve the HALL and the other reverb models' stereo image (decorrelated left/right tank outputs, wider early reflections, slow modulation to avoid ringing, stereo width control and a mono-compatibility check). Judged by listening in the emulator and by measurements (inter-channel correlation, decay, no ringing, CPU within +30% of the current model). In DUEL mode the reverb return is folded to each part's side (5b.1).
2. **Delay: ping-pong option.** The delay's PING-PONG (the repeats alternate left and right; in DUEL mode each part's repeats stay on its own side, so a ping-pong has nothing to alternate between: since 0.2.22 PING-PONG in DUEL is the plain delay, see "Delay mode" below). Until 0.2.15 a PING on/off switch (`G_PINGPONG`); since 0.2.16 the delay MODE PING-PONG (see "Delay mode" below).
   **Delay modulation (owner, 2026-10-13, 0.2.5): "more modulation for the delay: enrich the sound and the stereo-space feel, without taxing the CPU".** The delay has its own small LFO (not LFO 1 / 2, not the matrix) that moves the delay time, a gentle tape / chorus-like wobble; two controls on DELAY 2/2 (0.2.18: the cards are MRATE and MDEPTH, modulation rate and depth; K2 and K3 since 0.2.16), global like the rest of the delay. **DEPTH** 0..100 % (0 = off, the default: the delay is bit for bit what it was and costs nothing extra): at 100 % the time swings +-10 ms, but never more than a quarter of the delay time (a short delay keeps its feel, a long one is hardly touched: 10 ms of 500 ms is 2 %). **RATE** 0.05 .. 3.4 Hz (the chorus's rate scale), default 0.51 Hz; a live card also at DEPTH 0 (it is heard once DEPTH is turned up; a zero is not a reason to fade). How: two taps read the delay line at the time +- the wobble with linear interpolation (no click), the left LFO and a right LFO half a turn away that runs 1/16 faster, so the two channels drift in and out of opposite phase; in STEREO and LAYER the return is their mid and their half-difference is added to the left and subtracted from the right, which widens the image; the line is fed back from the mid, so every repeat is chorused again. With PING-PONG on, the two returns (left return, the loop's end) are the two taps and the side stays PING-PONG's (SPREAD applies). In DUEL one tap per part's bus, bus B a quarter turn behind bus A, no state shared between the buses. The depth reached glides (at most half a sample a block, 0.6 s from 0 to the top) so turning DEPTH never clicks. No extra buffer. Ids: the two global ids nobody read, `G_SYNC` (13, was a placeholder, always 0) is DEPTH and `G_MIDI` (12, an USB / TRS choice nothing read) is RATE (`G_COUNT` 27 and the project / multi formats unchanged; a stored old value 0 or 1 in RATE is only a slow rate, heard once DEPTH is raised). Saved with the project and with a multi (MU_GLOBAL). Not a morph item (globals never roll), no MIDI CC (the CC map is per part), not a motion target. Dotted delay times exist already: TIME has 2D, 4D, 8D, 16D (dotted half, quarter, eighth, sixteenth) and the triplets 4T, 8T, 16T, 32T.
   **Delay mode (owner, 2026-10-15, 0.2.14; reorganised 2026-10-15, 0.2.16; `fx_bus.c` `dly_ev`, `fx.c` `dlyev_*` and `fx_buses`, `params.c` `N_DMODE`, `dly_mode_ui`, `dly_mode_why`).** Owner (0.2.14): "build the granular-style event delay as a delay mode, next to the ping-pong". Owner (0.2.16): "organise top-down: the most important setting first, the end of the chain last; the delay mode must be the very first delay setting and MIX the last; there must be no mode called DELAY (all modes are delays); ping-pong must not be a separate on/off switch: it becomes a mode." **Modes** (`G_DMODE`, on the id of the unused `G_INFO`, the old CPU reading that nothing wrote since 1.2; `G_COUNT` 27; a value past OCT UP plays as DIGITAL; in MU_GLOBAL, so a multi and a project carry it; no morph, no CC, no motion): **0 DIGITAL** (card DIGI: the plain delay, clean repeats with the low-passed feedback COLR and the optional modulation; the old delay with PING off, bit for bit), **1 PING-PONG** (card PING: the repeats alternate left and right, the modulation too; the old PING on, bit for bit; the render hashes of 02f849e are in `tests/dlyev_test.c` and hold for both), **2 RANDOM** (RAND) and **3 OCT UP** (OCT+). The names are not TAPE: nothing in DIGITAL is tape-like. The card is five characters at most, the header says the full name when MODE is turned (`dly_mode_full`). The id 26 (`G_PINGPONG` until 0.2.15) is unused (`G_FREE26`, stored 0; `G_COUNT` and the formats keep their size). Default: DIGITAL (the old default was PING off: the same sound). **Pages, top-down (two):** DELAY 1/2 = MODE, TIME, FDBK, COLR (the kind of delay, then its loop); DELAY 2/2 = SPREAD, MRATE, MDEPTH (CHANCE in RANDOM / OCT UP), MIX (the stereo width, the slow modulation, the level last). The separate DELAY MODE page is gone. **Which control works where (UI honesty rule: grey only what a selection makes impossible, the reason on the card):** SPREAD (`G_PINGSPREAD`) sets the side's width in PING-PONG, RANDOM and OCT UP and does nothing in DIGITAL (reason `DIGI`); MRATE (`G_DMRATE`, the card was RATE until 0.2.17) is the delay modulation's rate, which exists in DIGITAL and PING-PONG only (`dly_mod` is not in the event path): in RANDOM and OCT UP its card is not shown (the column stays empty); MDEPTH (`G_DMDEP`, was DEPTH) is the modulation's depth in DIGITAL and PING-PONG and **CHANCE** in RANDOM / OCT UP (`glob_desc`; the same stored value, 0..100 %). The matrix's DELAY MOD DEPTH destination (short name DMDEP; DELAY MOD RATE is DMRAT) reads CHNC / "DELAY EVENT CHANCE" and DELAY MOD RATE fades with the mode's name in the two event modes. **DUEL (SPLIT), 0.2.22 (owner, 2026-10-16: "all the delay modes should be working in DUEL, just make them mono"):** every mode works in DUEL, as a mono effect on each part's own bus (A: part 1, left; B: part 2, right), and the MODE card is shown. DIGI is the plain delay, bit for bit as before. PING is the plain delay too (a ping-pong needs two sides and a part has one: the card says PING, the sound is DIGI's, MRATE and MDEPTH keep their plain meaning). RAND and OCT UP run the event delay on each bus (`dly_ev`, called by `split_buses` with no side output; bus B has its own state `dlyev_b`, 76 bytes in the pool, and its own half of `dly_buf`): the same voices, the same clock (one edge per delay time, counted from PLAY), the same CHANCE, but the events are centred: no random pan in OCT UP, no Haas offset, one read of the line, no side, so each part's delay stays on its own channel and the other channel is exactly 0 (`mode_test.c` leak map, `dlyev_test.c`). MDEPTH is CHANCE in these two modes, MRATE is not shown, as in STEREO and LAYER. SPREAD is not shown in DUEL in any mode (nothing to spread). Each bus has half of the line (0.74 s), so an event that would read past it is not played: with a delay time over about 0.35 s a reverse, an octave down or a late repeat does not fit and that clock edge plays the plain delay instead (an octave up needs a time under 0.65 s); STEREO and LAYER, with the whole line, are unchanged. A change of mode crossfades in 12 ms on each bus; SENDS OFF, STOP ALL and a change of the output mode clear both buses' event states. The stored mode was "stored but ignored" in DUEL before 0.2.22; the factory multis store DIGI (0), so they render as before. MODULES SENDS OFF hides the delay's cards (`mods_of`). **Stored formats:** multi version 4 (`MU_VER`) and project revision 2 (`PROJ_REV`); records of earlier firmware are refused as before (no conversion before the alpha); the factory multis M01..M10, M13..M16 are rewritten by `tools/factory_multis_delaymode.py`. **Transitions:** every switch of MODE runs through the existing paths: DIGITAL <-> PING-PONG is the old PING switch (same code, same smoothness, tested); into and out of RANDOM / OCT UP the 12 ms crossfade, from the plain delay that ran last (`dly_plain_pp`: DIGITAL or PING-PONG) and back to the mode chosen. **CHANCE default (owner, 2026-10-15):** turning MODE (the card or its OCT- reset) from DIGITAL or PING-PONG into RANDOM / OCT UP with the stored `G_DMDEP` at 0 sets it to `DMD_CHANCE_DEF` (40) and says `RANDOM: CHANCE 40 %` in the header (`params.c` `dly_mode_changed`); back to a plain mode a value that the unit set itself and nobody has turned since goes back to 0 (`dmd_auto`, RAM only), so the delay is as it was; a DEPTH that was not 0, a loaded multi and a stored mode are never touched. The original (CHOMPI TEMPO `granularDelay.h`, `alt_control_`) starts the randomness at 0 and fires an event on 0.5 x that share of the clock edges (at most 50 %), so 40 % is DUELL's own number. The web editor mirrors all of it (`layout.js` `dmdAfterMode`, `DMD.chanceDef`, `DMD.why`, `DMD.full` from the firmware). **Stereo:** no alternation of the repeats between the sides in RANDOM / OCT UP: the left channel reads the head, the right 10 ms behind it, the pan scales both; the alternating echo is the PING-PONG mode.
   How: two voices read the delay line (`dly_buf` itself, no buffer of its own) at a read head each. The plain voice follows the write head one delay time back (smoothed: a TIME or tempo change slides). On a clock edge, with the probability CHANCE, one voice starts an event: RETRIG (1 1/8 of the delay time back), REVERSE (from one time back, backwards), UP (two samples a sample from two times back: an octave up, ending before the write head), DOWN (half a sample a sample); the two voices crossfade over 1024 samples with a pair of parabolas of about equal power; at the next edge without an event the plain voice starts again. RAND: one of the four at random, centred. OCT+: always UP, with a random pan (+-0.6, equal power). The left channel reads the head, the right 10 ms (441 samples) behind it (Haas); the feedback is the centre read, low-passed by COLR, times FDBK (so a repeat fed back an octave up each pass climbs, and COLR tames it; the line clamps at 16 bit as the plain delay's). The return goes out as PING-PONG's: the mid in `wet`, half the difference in `dly_sd` (`dly_side_mix`: SPREAD, 0 = mono), so the mix, the reverbs after it, LOCUT, SPACE and SENDS OFF need no change. Everything is integer; products round towards zero (the tails reach exact silence). **Clock:** one edge per delay time (`delay_split`, the TIME card's note value; doubled while it is under 2048 samples), counted in samples from PLAY (`dlyev_start`, as the DUCK's PUMP) and free-running; the period follows `beat_samples`, so the internal tempo and an external MIDI clock's measured tempo. The research assumed a fixed 1/8 note clock; with TIME 1/8 it is the same. **Generator:** a xorshift32 from a fixed seed at each start of the mode (after a clear, a mode change from DELAY): the same notes and settings render the same twice. **Mode changes:** `dlyev.x` crossfades the output (mid, side and the feedback written) between the delay as it ran (the plain path's return, or the ping-pong's, without the modulation) and the events in 512 samples (11.6 ms), either way: no click; with DELAY's modulation on the other end the crossfade starts from the unmodulated read (the wobble is at most 10 ms of the read point). **Clears:** `dlyev_clear` in `snd_lines_clear` (SENDS OFF and STOP ALL's flush) and `dm_lines_clear` (a change of the output mode, power-on). **Left out:** the lock (a loop of one delay time on a gesture: it needs a key or a hold, decision for the owner) and the grain variant (upstream does not ship it). Cost (host instructions per sample of a whole mix with one plucked part, `-O1`): DELAY 507, PING-PONG 538, DELAY + DEPTH 60 553; RAND 552 at CHANCE 0 (between events, the "solo" loop), 639 at 50, 673 at 100; OCT+ 641 at 50, 674 at 100. State 76 bytes of RAM (`dlyev_t`), about 3.3 KB of flash (3,066 B of code, 285 B of strings and names, measured with the JieLi clang on `felucca.c` alone, base 02f849e against this). Credit: the idea of the granular delay in the CHOMPI TEMPO firmware (MIT; CHOMPI Club), written again by us; NOTICE.md, LICENSING.md, LICENSES/MIT-CHOMPI.txt and ABOUT. Test: `tests/dlyev_test.c`.
3. **EQ module** (new FX): low shelf, parametric mid (frequency, gain, Q) and high shelf, each with gain ±12 dB, to clean up a mushy mix. Placement (decided): **one EQ per part only**, after the part's insert effect, no master EQ. Parameters per band are modulation destinations.
   **0.2.25: the filter in 32-bit multiplies, each band on its own.** The chip has a 32 x 32 -> 64 multiply-accumulate but no 64 x 64 multiply, so 0.2.24's loop (64-bit coefficients times 64-bit samples, a 64-bit clamp) cost about 85 device instructions a sample a band. Now (`fx.c eq_run`): the coefficients Q28 in 32 bit (every setting fits: the largest is 5.94; the five sum to under 16, so the 64-bit sums cannot overflow at full scale), the sample and the output in Q8, the states Q36, and the output's rounding remainder carried into the next sample (first-order error feedback): about 32 device instructions a sample a band, within 1 LSB of the ideal filter, and a quiet tail ends at exactly 0 (0.2.24 kept a DC offset of up to 426 LSB, about -38 dBFS, on a LOW band at 30 Hz with a cut, and up to 16 LSB in the factory multis). The response is the design's as before (within 0.05 dB of the exact RBJ curve). **Each band runs only while its gain is set**: a flat band costs nothing (one band set: that band only; host 0 / 34 / 61 / 88 instructions a sample for 0..3 bands, the device about 0 / 33 / 66 / 98); a band turned to 0 rings out flat for 23 ms and then rests; only a band whose values moved is designed again. **Fixed:** 0.2.24 tested the HIGH band's gain at MID's Q, so HIGH ran only with Q off its middle: at the default Q the HIGH knob did nothing (18 factory sounds store a HIGH gain: they now sound with it). Tests: `tests/eq32_test.c` (`./host_tests.sh eq32`), `eqon`.
4. **MIDI-triggered sidechain compressor.** A sidechain "ducker", **per part** (decided; each part has its own trigger settings), that is triggered by a MIDI note on a chosen channel, with no audio input and no drum machine needed: setting TRIG CH (off, 1-16) and TRIG NOTE (any note or one note), DEPTH (how far the level ducks), ATTACK, HOLD and RELEASE (or a tempo-synced release), SHAPE. A note-on starts the duck, the release is the pumping. The trigger notes never play a sound. Optionally also a built-in tempo-synced trigger (4 on the floor) for use without MIDI, off by default. The ducker is also a matrix source (DUCK) so it can move other parameters. Test: an exact gain curve on a rendered note after a trigger, no clicks, correct latency of less than one audio block.
   **Built (2026-10-11):** per part DUCK CH (OFF, 1-16), NOTE (ANY or one note), DEPTH, ATK (1-100 ms), HOLD (0-500 ms), RLS (20 ms-2 s, log) on pages DUCK and DUCK 2 (ids P_DKCH..P_DKRLS, P_COUNT 119, P_E0 111; not morph, not motion); matrix source DUCK; linear gain curve; applied before level, pan and sends (fx.c duck_*). A note matching a part's trigger is swallowed when NOTE is one note or the channel plays no part; with NOTE ANY on a channel that plays a part, the notes play and also trigger. Not built: tempo-synced release, SHAPE, the built-in 4-on-the-floor trigger. **Since built:** SRC PUMP (the built-in trigger: a duck on every RATE division of the tempo, default 1/4 = four on the floor, CLK INT free-running or the incoming MIDI clock), SRC PART (keyed by the other part, with THR) and the release CURVE (LIN, EXP); the DUCK page is DUCK 1/2 and 2/2 (ids P_DKSRC, P_DKRATE, P_DKTHR, P_DKCRV).
   **Pump tuning (owner, 2026-10-13, 0.2.5): "fine-tuned for 120 to 140 BPM house or techno: the pump should already work as soon as somebody twists DEPTH; HOLD is too much for a pump, normally it is just attack and release".** How the gain is applied (unchanged): the part's block times a gain 1 - r, the reduction r a linear amount of the signal (not dB), after INSERT / EQ and before LEVEL, PAN and the sends; the attack is a straight ramp of r from where it is to DEPTH in ATK ms; the release brings r back to 0 in RLS ms (20 ms .. 2.1 s on a log dial, in milliseconds, not tempo-synced). What changed: (1) **the release curve defaults to EXP** (CURVE stays a choice: LIN, the straight line, is still there): every sample r loses a share of what is left, so the gain returns fast at first and settles smoothly, as a compressor's release does; time constant = RLS / 3 (63 % back after RLS / 3, 95 % after RLS, then a short straight finish to 0). LIN, a straight ramp that ends in a corner, is the earlier curve. (2) **HOLD is gone from the pages** (DUCK 2/2 is now RLS and CURVE; DUCK 1/2 SRC, RATE / THR / NOTE, DEPTH, ATK). `P_DKHLD` keeps its id (the editor protocol, the stored records) and its stored value, but the DSP never reads it: the envelope is attack, then release; a stored non-zero HOLD is ignored (legacy). The morph still draws its random number (old End B seeds stay) but never moves the value. (3) **SRC PUMP counts its beats while DEPTH is 0** (nothing ducks): raising DEPTH later ducks on the next beat of the transport (PLAY / MIDI Start), not at the instant of the turn, so the pump is in time as soon as DEPTH is turned up. (4) Defaults of a new / INIT / factory sound: SRC PUMP, RATE 1/4, DEPTH 0 (off), ATK 5 ms, RLS 242 ms (step 36, about half a beat at 120 BPM), CURVE EXP, THR 30. **Old data:** projects, multis and autosaves store every value, so they keep LIN where they had LIN; the sparse user-patch store leaves out what equals the default, so it keeps LIN as its own default for CURVE (`upreset.c up_def`, as for the LFO TRIGs): an old patch without a CURVE value still loads LIN. The factory sounds that use the ducker (9, DEPTH 14..16, PUMP 1/4) take the new default EXP: their pump is smoother; M01..M04 do not use the ducker; the developer-only test multis M13..M16 (ducker 40 %) keep their stored LIN.

5. **SLICER insert removed (2026-10-11).** The per-part SLICER (GATE / STUT, page FX > SLICER) is gone: no page, no processing. FX family order: FX, INSERT 1, INSERT 2, EQ 1, EQ 2, DUCK 1, DUCK 2, CHORUS, DLY 1, DLY 2, REVERB (0.2.21: CHORUS, DELAY and REVERB are GLO's pages; FX ends with SEND FX). Its four ids (45..48) were **free**; since 0.2.2 45 and 46 are the filter's CUT and RES, and since 0.2.9 45..48 are the **multimode filter's CUT RES MODE SLOPE** (section 2.5; MODE and SLOPE never morphed, none kept on a sound load). P_COUNT 127 and every other id in place. A store of Felucca's layout that holds the SLICER's values there (1, 1, 127 ..) is put back to the defaults when it loads (`param_retired`: projects FUN1..FUN10, user presets of versions 1..5, a MIDI LEARN map without the DUELL mark), so the filter is never switched on by an old SLICER setting.

6. **The FX hold layer's performance effects are removed (owner, 2026-10-12, DUELL 0.2.2).** Gone: the hold-FX layer and its key map, REPEAT 1/8 1/16 1/32, REVERSE, TAPE STOP, FREEZE, OCT UP / OCT DN, FLANGER, PHASER (the master effects; the per-part INSERT's PHASR / FLANG are untouched), CRUSH, THROW, DEPTH, the FILTER macro as a master effect, the black-key track mutes, **MENU > CONTROL > FX LATCH** (settings bit 1 of `ui_prefs` is retired and stays clear; the web editor's MENU id 7 is retired and never reused, `MENU_DESC` lists 20 settings) and the layer's key map (`favorites.factory[15][14..23]` is no longer read or written, never reused). A short press of FX still opens the FX pages; **holding FX does nothing special** (it is a page button like ENV: the page opens when the button is let go, keys pressed meanwhile are notes). `perform.c` is deleted; what the per-part INSERT shared with it (the PHASER's stage table, the sweep's triangle, the wet mix) lives in fx.c. The 32 KB loop buffer `sl_buf` (slicer.c) only the layer used is gone (only the 2.9 ms gain ramp constants stay in slicer.c). **What stays:** the part mute of the GLO page (MUTE 1 / MUTE 2, `P_MUTE`, voice.c: it was never perform.c's), the metronome and the whole master chain; with no effect there the output is bit for bit as it was when idle (regress's goldens unchanged by this change). Freed on the device build: flash 9,360 B net (image 564,040 -> 554,680 B with the universal filter's code in), the POOL by exactly 32,768 B (351,808 -> 319,040 B), RAM `.data + .bss` 1,472 B net (53,808 -> 52,336 B; the layer's state and the FLANGER line gone, the filter's two integrators and one byte per voice added). **Since 0.2.5 the effects are back as PERFORM mode (section 5e)**: holding FX turns it on, so "holding FX does nothing special" and "keys pressed meanwhile are notes" above no longer hold; FX LATCH, CRUSH, THROW and the key map stay gone.

## 5d. Dual core (owner's request: use both cores of the device)
**Status (updated 2026-10-10): known to work in principle, device-only to prove.** The FM-1 chip has a second core (core 1). Two community firmwares already use it: **ChoralRoot** (github.com/Quixotic7/ChoralRootFM1, v0.15/0.16, doc `docs/DUALCORE.md`: it renders its bass part on core 1) and Melodee / fm1-x0x (the HAL it was ported from; GPL-3.0). What they teach:
- **Start-up:** core 1 is started from `firmware/hal/fm1_cpu1.{h,S}`: entry address written at the top of RAM (0x01C7FFF8), its own interrupt bank, a private 2816 B stack, its code placed in RAM (`.c1_text`) so flash being off never stops it. It must be started **before** `audio_init` and the timers, with the PC-limit guard opened for the start and re-armed after.
- **Work split per audio block:** core 0 does events, matrix and LFO ticks, voice allocation and renders part 0; core 1 renders part 1 into its own buffer; core 0 then waits (max 8 ms) and mixes everything (sends, FX buses, master) in one core's order, so the output is **bit-identical to a single-core render**. Hand-over by single-writer flags and a job function pointer with a checked sequence number, no locks; caches are shared (`csync`).
- **Stability lessons (important):** other firmwares crashed after playing notes. Cause found by Melodee: the chip runs above the vendor's tested frequency on the boot loader's rails, and with two cores running the supply margin is too small on some units (core 1 misreads its mailbox, core 0 data corrupts). Fixes: raise the core and DC rails (SYSVDD 1.35 V, VDC14 1.45 V) before starting core 1 (more on a misread), validate every job (sequence + check word, refuse a null function), 8 ms stall timeout with core 0 rendering the block itself and core 1 held, core 1 faults handled without a reset, and a **per-device Dual Core On/Off option** (effective at next power-on). The margin differs from chip to chip.
- **Gain:** for ChoralRoot the benefit is mainly the voice budget (its bass part is light). For DUELL the two parts are about equal in weight, so core 1 can take **part 2 and core 0 part 1**: close to half of the render time, which pays for the morph cross-fades, LAYER mode and the effects.
- **Risk:** ChoralRoot's author reports the code "built and verified on the host, not run on hardware, because the FM-1 unit is bricked". It must be tested on the real unit with the recovery path ready.
- **DUELL plan:** (1) device toolchain first (Docker, JieLi toolchain, 3 SDK files; section 6); (2) port the HAL (GPL-3.0, credit Quixotic7, Melodee and fm1-x0x) and the per-part job split in `mix_block` (our part 1 / part 2 split is the same shape as theirs), single-threaded and bit-identical on the host and in the emulator; (3) ship with **Dual Core default OFF** in the first builds, an option to turn it on, the rail boost and all fail-safes of ChoralRoot 0.16; (4) the owner tests on the unit and reports before it becomes the default. The emulator cannot prove stability, only logic.

**Other things learned from JIANT** (github.com/juanjiant-bit/JIANT, a Felucca fork): a visible **CPU load meter** on the screen; "keep divisions and LFO maths out of the audio interrupt's inner loop, compute once per block" (we already do it per block); a master **DUCK** and a slow leveler (about 1.5 s) in the master processing (check how DUCK is triggered before building our MIDI sidechain); five reverb models; static build-time memory only; a note that a big-buffer pool at 92% left only 25 KB (our DUEL bus doubling added 16.7 KB, so watch the pool on the device).

---

## 5e. PERFORM mode (owner's design, built 2026-10-13, DUELL 0.2.5)
The performance effects are back as a **mode with its own page**, PERFORM (Felucca's FX hold layer, removed in 0.2.2 (5c.6), ported and grown). Code: `perform.c` (the sound), `ui_perform.c` (the page, the knobs, the keys' input), `fx.c` (`perf_master`, SPACE: `sv`, `space_step`), `fx_bus.c` (`SV_*`), `seq.c` (`keyboard_block`); test: `tests/perform_test.c` (`host_tests.sh perform`).

**On and off.**
- **FX held 0.7 s** (the same hold timing as SAVE's) turns PERFORM on; held again in PERFORM, it turns it off. **A white key pressed while FX is held** turns PERFORM on at once, and that key is already PERFORM's (its effect starts). Either way FX's release then opens no page. So keys pressed with FX held are **never notes** any more.
- A **tap of FX** outside PERFORM opens the FX pages as before (again = next FX page). A tap of FX **in** PERFORM goes back to the page PERFORM was shown over.
- **HOME** leaves PERFORM for HOME's page; every other page button (EDIT, ENV, LFO, ARP, SEQ, SCL, GLO, SAVE) leaves it for its own page; the menu (HOME held) is shown over it and closing the menu goes to HOME's page. A dialog over PERFORM (SAVE held: SAVE OVER) keeps PERFORM under it.
- PERFORM is never saved: it is OFF at power-on.

**The page.** Header: the page name PERFORM in the top middle, the part badges (the part ALGORITHM picked; both lit with BOTH), as every page. Cards: **K1 MORPH, K2 SPACE, K3 KILL, K4 DEPTH**. The panel: the 16 white keys as a 4 x 4 map, a row per group of four keys (the dots on the keyboard), in the keys' order from F3: a held key's cell in the accent colour (a REPEAT / REVERSE waiting for its 1/16 in the theme colour), a key that cannot run now greyed (its key pressed says why in the header: `TEMPO UNDER 81 BPM`, `PUMP: NO CLOCK` ..). Footer, first row: `SELECT: BALANCE +20` at the left and `ALGO: PART 1 / BOTH / PART 2` at the right; second row as on every page: the engine's icon and name and the **patch name** of the selected part (PRESETS changes it here as everywhere; 0.2.7 .. 0.2.8 hid it while PRESETS was BALANCE; owner, 0.2.9), and no page number (PERFORM is on no family's round). FX's LED is lit.

**The keys.** While PERFORM is on, the **16 white keys** (F3 .. G5) play no note, send no MIDI and record nothing; each holds its effect **while it is held** (momentary: in and out with a 2.9 ms ramp). The **black keys stay notes** (they play the selected part as on every page). MIDI-in notes, the arps and the sequencer play on. A key held from before PERFORM ends as the note it was; a PERFORM key let go after PERFORM ended still ends its effect. With the menu, a dialog or NAME open, the keys are notes again and every held effect stops until its key is let go.

| Keys | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1-4 (F3 G3 A3 B3) | REPEAT 1/8 | REPEAT 1/16 | REPEAT 1/32 | REVERSE |
| 5-8 (C4 D4 E4 F4) | TAPE STOP | FREEZE | OCT UP | OCT DN |
| 9-12 (G4 A4 B4 C5) | FLANGER | PHASER | DRIVE | PUMP |
| 13-16 (D5 E5 F5 G5) | ROLL A | ROLL B | MUTE 1 | MUTE 2 |

**The LEDs.** The white keys show the map: a **held** key fully lit, every other key **dark**; the black keys lit while held or playing. **Row markers (0.2.11, owner):** the 1st, 5th, 9th and 13th of the 16 white keys (the first key of each row of four; the keyboard's printed dots do not fall on every fourth key) are lit steadily at the LEDS mode's in-scale level (no pulse; scale lights do not apply on this page), so the rows can be counted; before, every key that could run glowed at the mid level. The same picture in every LEDS mode (MAX does not invert it: its markers are at MAX's own in-scale level, 70 %, its held key full).

**The effects.** All but MUTE and ROLL are master stages on the final stereo mix, after the master level and before `master_out` (the limiter and the soft clip), each channel its own: in **DUEL** the left is part 1 and the right part 2 and every stage keeps the two apart (a silent side stays digital silence). Order: [REPEAT / REVERSE / TAPE STOP / FREEZE / OCT UP / OCT DN] -> FILTER -> FLANGER -> PHASER -> PUMP; DRIVE comes before the master level (so MASTER scales what it gives). Of the capture-buffer effects (the first six) the last pressed plays, letting it go returns to the one still held; effects of different kinds stack.
- **Capture buffer**: 32 KB in the pool (`pb_buf`): 8192 stereo frames, 16 bit, at 22.05 kHz: 371 ms.
- **REPEAT 1/8, 1/16, 1/32**: from the next 1/16 of the transport (at once while stopped) the buffer records that length and loops it while held; a shorter REPEAT of what is recorded switches with a cross-fade. **REVERSE**: a 1/8 recorded, then played backwards. A length that does not fit the buffer at the tempo cannot run (greyed): 1/8 and REVERSE below 81 BPM, 1/16 below 41, 1/32 below 21 (with an external clock, its tempo).
- **TAPE STOP**: the sound slows to a stop within a beat (at most 0.73 s), then silence while held. **FREEZE**: the next 186 ms caught and played as two overlapping grains, held. **OCT UP / OCT DN**: the mix an octave up / down beside itself (a rotating-delay shifter): 0.56 the live mix + 0.7 the shifted.
- **FLANGER**: 0.5 .. 8 ms swept, a bar a sweep in time with the transport, the right a quarter behind, feedback 0.5. **PHASER**: four all-pass stages, 150 Hz .. 4.8 kHz, half a bar a sweep, feedback 0.4.
- **DRIVE**: the INSERT's FOLD (the wave reflected at +-16384) into a hard clip, on the master mix (own state; no part's INSERT is touched): a 27 Hz low cut in front, the drive 1x .. 15.6x by K4 DEPTH (squared), the clip from 24000 (DEPTH 0) down to 9000 (100), out x 2/3 .. x 1/2 (DEPTH 0: unity below the fold; 100: about the dry mix's loudness, +1 dB on the test song), a DC blocker after. **Its model is chosen when the firmware is built (0.2.7, `PERF_DRIVE_MODEL` in perform.c; the owner picks by ear, only the chosen one is in the image; the default stays 0):** **0 FOLD** (the above); **1 TUBE**: tanh (dsp.c `softclip`) of the signal plus a bias of 0.3, less the bias's own value (the halves flatten unevenly: even harmonics), the drive 1x .. 11x, a one-pole high cut 9 kHz .. 4.5 kHz; **2 FUZZ**: the drive 1x .. 50x into a diode-style clip (the positive half hard at 14000, the negative a soft clip to 3/4 of full), a one-pole tone 5.5 kHz .. 2.8 kHz; **3 CRUSH**: the drive 1x .. 2x into tanh, then the bits (16 .. 4) and the rate (44.1 .. 1.8 kHz, a sample held, the same instants on both channels) reduced. All: the drive squared over DEPTH, the 27 Hz low cut in front, the DC blocker after, the 2.9 ms fade; models 1..3 their level from a 5-point table over DEPTH (0 25 50 75 100) measured to keep the loudness within about 2.5 dB of dry on M01 EERIE SIGNAL, #3 LADDER BASS and #63 PWM PAD (FOLD: -7.3 .. +2.3 dB), DEPTH 0 at the dry level. Cost over REPEAT (host): FOLD +83, TUBE +107, FUZZ +102, CRUSH +63 instructions a sample. `perform_test.c` passes with each (`-DPERF_DRIVE_MODEL=1..3` by hand; its DRIVE thresholds per model). Previews: `sound_previews/drive_models/` (README.txt).
- **PUMP**: on every beat of the tempo (internal or external clock: the beats the ARP LED counts, `seq.c events_block`; PLAY starts one) the master ducks in 2 ms down to K4 DEPTH (100 %: 0.95, -26 dB) and comes back with the part DUCK's EXP curve (three time constants in a beat), so the next beat finds it all but back. It is its own: no part's DUCK setting changes. With an external clock that does not run it cannot run (greyed).
- **ROLL A / ROLL B**: the MORPH page's ROLL of that end, once on the press (`morph_act MQ_ROLL`), on the part(s) ALGORITHM chose here; the header says ROLLED A / B.
- **MUTE 1 / MUTE 2**: part 1 / part 2 silent while held: its dry signal ramps out and back in 2.9 ms before its level and sends (after its EQ), so its delay and reverb tails ring on. Not `P_MUTE` (the GLO page's MUTE is untouched), never saved, never recorded.

**The knobs** (transient, never saved):
- **K1 MORPH**: the MORPH page's K1, the fader of the part(s) chosen by ALGORITHM (`morph_act MQ_FADER`).
- **K2 SPACE** (-100 .. +100, the middle 0): a macro on the shared chorus, delay and reverb, applied to the values the buses use (`fx.c sv`; song.g, the patch's and the multi's values, are never written). **0 = exactly the sound's own** (the buses read song.g as ever: bit for bit). SPACE glides (0.29 s over the range), each value from the stored one, s = SPACE / 100:

| What | left (-100): smaller | right (+100): immense |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the sends into chorus, delay and reverb (every part's; both buses in DUEL) | x (1 - 0.85 s): x 0.15 (a send at 0 stays 0) | x (1 + 0.6 s): x 1.6 (RESO's reverb send: x 1 at most), **and at least a floor** (0.2.9): s x chorus 24 %, delay 36 %, reverb 60 % of a send's range (sends 30 / 46 / 76 of 127; SHIM's reverb 30 %, RESO's 10 %), every part's send |
| the reverb's SIZE (its decay; ROOM, SPRING, HALL, SHIM, RESO, PLATE) | SIZE - 0.75 SIZE s: a quarter | SIZE + (150 - SIZE) s: 150 (ROOM's combs at most 0.986, SPRING's loop 0.97, HALL's lines 0.99, PLATE's loop 0.90); RESO: up to 100 (its combs 0.952), or its own SIZE if that is higher |
| the delay's FDBK | x (1 - 0.75 s): a quarter | up to 0.92 (below self-oscillation) |
| the chorus's DEPTH | x (1 - 0.8 s): a fifth | up to the knob's largest (DEPTH 127; 0.2.5 .. 0.2.8: 2x it, a +-0.8 semitone wobble on ACID ETHER's chorus, M04 then) |
| the STEREO WIDTH (0.2.9; STEREO and LAYER only, **not in DUEL**) | the side x (1 - s): at -100 **mono** (L = R = the mid) | the side x (1 + 0.9 s): x 1.9, the loudness kept (the guard) |

  **Not the delay's TIME** (0.2.9, the owner: 0.2.5 .. 0.2.8 glided it x 0.5 .. x 1.5, which bent the echoes' pitch while it moved, up to 1.6 semitones, twice that on PING-PONG's loop, and each echo in the feedback was bent again: it sounded weird and glitchy). SPACE changes the delay's level (its send) and FDBK only; the delay runs on its plain path as ever (its modulation path only for its own DEPTH). The reverb has no pre-delay to scale.
  **The send floor** (0.2.9, the owner: "the space should work even if the sends are on 0", about double the first proposal): to the right **every part's chorus, delay and reverb send is at least the floor**: effective send = softmax(send x factor, floor) (`fx.c` `space_tap`, per part: it adds the part's dry signal, at its LEVEL as its sends take it, x max(0, floor - send x factor) with a quadratic knee of 5 % of the range either side, no kink), so a sound with its sends at 0 gets space, and a send already above floor + 5 % gets exactly SPACE's x 1.6 and nothing more. The floor follows SPACE (0 in the middle); its values are the last block's (SPACE glides 0.25 % a block). Per bus: in DUEL part 1's floor only into bus A and part 2's only into bus B (part 1 alone with sends 0 at +100: the right side exactly 0); in STEREO / LAYER each part's into the shared buses. SHIM's reverb floor is half (with a part's own send its ROOM combs reached their int16 limit) and RESO's a sixth (its tuned combs). The limiter on a held chord at +100 (MASTER at its default; the stereo width included): sends at 0: M01 -2.7 .. -6.0 dB, M04 -0.5 .. -4.2 dB (the middle 0 dB); the multi's own sends: M01 -7.0 .. -11.2 dB (0.2.8's right side -6.1 .. -10.9), M04 -1.3 .. -4.4 dB (-0.1 .. -1.5: M04's sends, reverb 15 and 38, delay 0 and 26, chorus 0 and 66, are under the floors, so they rise to them). The cost: STEREO +1.2 .. 1.3 %, DUEL +1.1 .. 1.4 % of a half at +100 (every model; the width included in STEREO).
  **The stereo width** (0.2.9, the owner: "SPACE should decrease the stereo space to completely mono, and widen it on the plus side"; `fx.c` `space_width`): on the whole mix before the master (the dry parts and the effects' returns), mid M = (L + R) / 2, side S = (L - R) / 2, out L = g (M + gs S), R = g (M - gs S); gs from 1 - |s| (left, 0 at -100: L = R exactly once the master's DC blockers have settled) to 1 + 0.9 s (right). The guard g (at most 1, never a boost) keeps the mix's loudness: g = sqrt((Em + Es) / (Em + gs^2 Es)) from the mid's and side's energies (~23 ms), so a mix with much side (HALL's return) is not pushed into the limiter. A mono source has no side: it stays mono and as loud; the stereo content widens (the parts' PAN and SPREAD, HALL's return, PING-PONG, the delay modulation's sides). The gains glide a sample at a time (no seam); at SPACE 0 they end at exactly 1 and 0 and the stage stops (bit for bit). Measured (a held chord, SPACE +100, width on against off): side / mid +5 .. +7 dB, the limiter within 1.1 dB, the peak within 0.5 dB; the stage alone keeps the loudness within 0.1 dB. Cost: ~14 host instructions a sample (STEREO / LAYER while SPACE is off the middle). **DUEL: no width** (its left is part 1 and its right part 2, dual mono for external effects: a mono sum or a side boost would mix the parts into each other); the rest of SPACE works there as in the table, and turning SPACE in DUEL says `DUEL: NO WIDTH` in the header once while PERFORM is on.
