  **Smooth while it moves** (0.2.9): the chorus's DEPTH glides over each block from the last block's (fx_bus.c `cho_glide`; before, it stepped once a block and the read point jumped up to ~3 samples: a click a block, a 1.4 kHz buzz on the chorus while SPACE, the DEPTH knob or the matrix moved it); SPRING's loop length glides between samples (fx_bus.c `spring_glide`; before, a whole sample a block: a click a block for ~0.45 s after a big SIZE move). A value that holds is read as before (bit for bit). **RESO** to the right: SIZE 150 (0.9992) with its send x 1.6 drove its four tuned combs into their int16 limit (a hard clip inside the loop: 5 % of the comb cells at the limit on a held chord); now its SIZE goes to 100 at most and its send stays x 1 (0.3 %; in the middle a few cells a second, as before).
  Measured (`perform_test.c`): the HALL tail of the test song from 0.5 s after it stops -52 dB at -100, +17 dB at +100; every reverb at SIZE 127 with FDBK 127 and the sends full, SPACE +100, dies away (STEREO and DUEL; the output under full scale); the echo of a 1/8 at the same place at -100, 0 and +100; turning it round while the song plays makes no click; a tone into each bus, SPACE turned 0 -> +100: no seam at the blocks' starts (chorus and every reverb, STEREO and DUEL); in DUEL the other side stays silent. Its cost over the middle: +0.3 % of a half in STEREO, +0.5 .. 0.8 % in DUEL, every model (0.2.8: +0.7 .. 1.0 / +1.2 .. 1.8 %, the delay's interpolated taps).
- **K3 KILL** (-100 .. +100, the middle **OFF**: bit for bit, the stage does not run): to the left a low-pass from 20 kHz (just open; 18.7 kHz a detent off the middle) down to 30 Hz at -96, to the right a high-pass from 20 Hz (21 Hz a detent off) up to 15 kHz at +96, the same share of an octave a detent (DUELL 0.2.14: a **24 dB/oct stereo filter**, each channel two 12 dB stages of the trapezoidal state-variable filter of the multimode filter (2.5), a 4-pole Butterworth: flat, no resonance bump, coefficients Q30 with 64-bit products, the states 10 bits finer than the signal; 0.2.9 .. 0.2.13: one 12 dB stage, Q 1, a Q14 table and states at a quarter of the signal's resolution: below ~200 Hz its integrators stalled, a DC offset that moved in steps (the crackle deep in the low-pass, up to -27 dBFS) and noise from the first detent (-74 dBFS left, -31 dBFS right); 0.2.5 .. 0.2.8: 120 Hz and 3.8 kHz); beyond +-96 the output fades to silence at +-100 (the kill zone: **fully turned it is silent**; 4 steps, ramped 2.9 ms a step at most). The cutoff glides (a one-pole on the log cutoff, ~12 ms, a block at a time, the coefficients moving in straight lines every sample: no zipper, the detents become a smooth sweep). Turned from the middle the filter fades in over 23 ms at its open end; back in the middle, or across it, it sweeps open, fades out (23 ms) and rests: the low-pass and the high-pass trade places only at rest (turned across at once: ~0.1 s through open). Measured (`perform_test.c`): a detent off the middle 30 Hz .. 15 kHz within 0.3 dB; at -50 (677 Hz) and +50 (629 Hz) -3.0 dB at the corner and -24 dB/oct, the design's response within 0.04 dB; the noise floor at most -97.7 dBFS at every setting (0.2.13: -66 .. -89 dBFS); silence after a sound exactly 0; at -96 a 1 kHz sine more than 80 dB down (0.2.13: 26 dB), at +-100 silent; turned a detent every 20 ms the block-rate sidebands 80 dB under the tone, no spur above the ideally smoothed filter's. Cost (`cpu_scenarios_test.c` L, a steady 4 + 4 voice scene, % of the half): K3 off the middle +4.7 (low-pass) / +5.3 (high-pass), +5.5 / +6.1 while it turns (0.2.13: +4.6 .. +4.8); the stage's loops as the device compiler makes them (-Os, counted, not timed): ~104 instructions a stereo sample for the low-pass, ~124 for the high-pass, ~126 / ~144 while it turns (0.2.13: ~116).
- **K4 DEPTH** (0 .. 100 %, 100 at power-on): the strength of the held effects: the capture-buffer effects' level (OCT UP / DN: their **shimmer**, the shifted sound fed back, 0 .. 0.5), FLANGER's and PHASER's level, DRIVE's drive, PUMP's depth. It does not touch ROLL or MUTE.
- **PERF LEAVE (0.2.14, owner 2026-10-15): what happens to SPACE and KILL when PERFORM is left or a sound is loaded.** Why: SPACE is one global value (`perf_space`, also the MIXER's K3, never stored); up to 0.2.13 leaving PERFORM reset only KILL (instantly), and only a MULTI load reset SPACE, so a SPACE of +100 stayed on after leaving and after every patch load ("still in immense space"). Now **MENU > CONTROL > PERF LEAVE, FADE (default) / KEEP** (editor id 32; a device setting in the settings byte [15][10] bit 5, set = KEEP; a new unit, ERASE DATA and every older record read FADE; never in a patch or multi).
  - **FADE:** when PERFORM is left (FX, HOME, any page button), when a **patch / sound is loaded** (PRESETS, a saved patch, a MIDI program change, the editor) and when a **multi is loaded**, **KILL and SPACE glide to 0** in one smooth line (one detent per 4 ms: 0.4 s from the ends of the knob, less from nearer; KILL's and SPACE's own ramps in `perform.c` / `fx.c` follow it: no click). KILL 0 is the exact bypass and SPACE 0 the patch's own sends and width, so **after the fade the output is bit for bit the patch's own** (KILL's filter settles in at most about 1.5 s more with the output equal to the dry signal at the last bit; `perform_test`). SPACE is faded only when PERFORM moved it (turned while PERFORM was on; a multi load always): a SPACE set on the MIXER alone stays when PERFORM is just opened and left. Turning KILL or SPACE by hand, or opening PERFORM again, stops a running fade where it is.
  - **KEEP:** nothing is faded or reset: SPACE and KILL stay as they are after leaving PERFORM and after any load (a multi load too), and the cards keep showing the live value on PERFORM (and the MIXER). Nothing is hidden by either setting.
  - DEPTH (it sounds only with a key held) and K1 MORPH (the MORPH page's fader, the sound's own) are no live values of this kind and are left as they are in both settings; DEPTH keeps its value until power-off. None of it is saved: SPACE and KILL start at 0 and DEPTH at 100 % at power-on. OCT- steps the PERF LEAVE row back to FADE (a MENU row: no RESET gesture).
  - **Black keys in PERFORM (owner 2026-10-15):** they still play notes (a feature), and **while PERFORM is on they play in the scale**: a black key is moved to the nearest note of the global SCALE and ROOT (a tie goes down) **whatever MENU/GLO OFF-SCALE says** (SNAP or PLAY), as long as a scale is set (SCALE CHROMATIC: unchanged). It is the key-to-note mapping only (`seq.c kb_pitch`), only while PERFORM is on: notes already sounding when PERFORM opens or closes are left alone, other pages are unchanged, MIDI-in notes are not touched (they follow OFF-SCALE as ever). It costs nothing. QUICKSTART says it.

**The other controls in PERFORM.** **PRESETS = the sound**, exactly as on the other pages (the same list, the patch name in the footer and the header message): it changes the patch of the part(s) ALGORITHM chose; with BOTH each part moves the same step from its own current sound (turn +1: part 1 to its next sound and part 2 to its next sound; the two stay different; `ui_perform.c perf_presets`). **SELECT = BALANCE** (owner, 0.2.9; 0.2.5 .. 0.2.8 had it on PRESETS): PERFORM is one page, so SELECT has nothing to select; it is the MODE page's BALANCE, -100 .. +100, a step a detent, centre 0, CC 116, every mode (in DUEL it fades one side), the header says `BALANCE 2`. It is kept as ever (the global `mix_bal`: unchanged when PERFORM is left, not reset on entry, not saved into patches; saved in the settings and the multis with the MODE page), and said on the screen (`SELECT: BALANCE`). SELECT's presses are unchanged. **ALGORITHM = PART 1 / BOTH / PART 2** from left to right (0.2.10; was PART 1 / PART 2 / BOTH; a detent a step, stopping at the ends): the part(s) K1 MORPH and ROLL A / B act on. It is the MORPH TARGET, the same state as the MORPH page's ALGORITHM and CC 111 (section 3.4a), so leaving PERFORM keeps it. **MASTER** as ever.

**Idle.** PERFORM off, or on with no key held, every ramp and glide over and SPACE and KILL in the middle: every stage is skipped (`mix_block` tests a few flags and counts PERFORM's 1/16 clock) and the output is bit for bit the output without PERFORM (`perform_test.c` IDLE, regress's goldens). `perf_master` hands the block back to `mix_block`'s own master tail (its level at an exact identity), so `master_out` keeps its one call site and the idle path its code.

**MIDI.** No new CC; the CC map is unchanged. MIDI LEARN cannot pick SPACE, KILL or DEPTH (not built; turning the MIXER's SPACE card while learning says `SPACE: NO LEARN`).

**Cost** (host instructions a sample over the idle test song, STEREO, 1053, `perform_test.c`; the device estimate is the old FX layer's 1.7 % per 100): REPEAT 1/16 +110, TAPE STOP +109, FREEZE +134, OCT UP over REPEAT +81 (+116 with the shimmer), over REPEAT: FLANGER +139, PHASER +138, DRIVE +85, PUMP +18, KILL +92 (0.2.14's 24 dB: +83 low-pass, +101 high-pass); SPACE +100 +9 over idle (0.2.8: +57, the delay's moving time); everything at once (REPEAT, FLANGER, PHASER, DRIVE, PUMP, MUTE 2, KILL) +622 (~10.6 %). Idle: the same as the build without PERFORM (a host bench of the idle mix: 1183 against 1187 instructions a sample). Memory: the pool +32,768 B (the capture buffer: 319,040 -> 351,808 B of 368,640, more than 8 KB spare), RAM about +1.9 KB (the stages' state, FLANGER's 1.4 KB line). Not yet measured on the device build (flash, `tests/target_budget.py`).

---

## 5f. MODULES: four device switches (owner's decision, 2026-10-15, DUELL 0.2.14)
A page in the GLO group, after the global effects and the master pages (0.2.21; 0.2.24: **TEMPO, CHORUS, DELAY 1/2, DELAY 2/2, REVERB, SHIMMER, WIDTH, MASTER (BASS MONO), COMP, MODULES, SIDECHAIN, MIDI, MIDI LEARN**). It replaces the MENU proposal's two combined rows (ARP + SEQ, EFFECTS). Four separate switches, one a knob, each **ON** (the default) or **OFF**: **K1 ARP, K2 SEQ, K3 SENDS, K4 PART FX.** A turn right is ON, left is OFF (a detent switches, as MIDI IN). Code: `core.h` `MODS_*`, `mods_off`, `arp_mode`, `arp_hold`; `ui.c` `mods_*`; `ui_input.c` `mods_knob`; `seq.c` (`seq_play`, `seq_mods_off`); `fx.c` (`snd_*`, `pfx_*`); `ui_graph.c` `graph_mods`; test `tests/modules_test.c` (host_tests.sh `modules`).

**Device settings.** Kept in the settings record, byte `favorites.factory[15][10]` bits 1..4 (bit 0 is MENU's ANIM IDLE; bits 5..7 stay free): bit 1 ARP OFF, 2 SEQ OFF, 3 SENDS OFF, 4 PART FX OFF, so a clear bit is ON. A new unit, ERASE DATA and every older settings record read all four ON (0.2.14's one-time defaults change, `settings_persist.c settings_defaults`, clears bits 1..7 of an older record: nothing wrote them before). Never saved in a patch, a multi or the project; a multi load never changes them. A change on the page is saved about 2 s later (as the MORPH settings). The audio side reads its own copy, `mods_off`, refreshed every UI frame. **The stored values of the arp, the steps, the sends and the part effects are never touched by a switch:** turning a module ON again restores its behaviour.

**ARP OFF.** No part's arp plays, whatever its MODE says: the arp code reads the MODE through `arp_mode()`, which is 0 (OFF) while ARP is OFF, so keys and MIDI notes go straight to the voices (note on, note off), chord keys still build their chord, ARP TARGET moves nothing. The existing "arp switched off" transitions run as for a MODE turned to OFF: a chord latched by HOLD ARP ends, a HOLD ALL chord is handed to the voices (and back to the arp when ARP is ON again). **HOLD** (`arp_hold()`): ALL latches the keys as with the arp off; **ARP latches nothing** (it means "the arp keeps its chord": with no arp there is no chord to keep). **REC as HOLD** follows the arp as it plays now: with ARP OFF it toggles each affected part OFF <-> ALL (a stored HOLD ARP counts as off); the LAYER two-part rule is unchanged. The ARP button's beat flash stops.

**SEQ OFF.** No stored step plays: PLAY, an external MIDI Start / Continue, the count-in and a multi's patterns start the transport (the clock, the tempo-synced LFOs, the DUCK PUMP's beat, the click) and the arps, but `seq_play` plays no step: neither its notes nor its parameter locks nor the knob automation (what a step still sounds is let go, a TIE or SLIDE held over too; the automation's values go back to the sound's own; a RATCH's repeats stop). The arp's **SEQUENCER mode** is OFF too (it plays the same steps): such a part plays its keys directly, as under ARP OFF. Editing stays: the STEP and AUTOMATION pages edit as ever, **STEP PREVIEW still sounds a step**, REC arms and records (what is recorded plays once SEQ is ON). PLAY says `MODULES: SEQ OFF` in the header when it starts.

**SENDS OFF.** The chorus, the delay (PING-PONG and its modulation included) and the reverb, their buses and the SPACE macro (PERFORM K2, the MIXER's K3) are skipped. The switch fades the buses' whole contribution out against the dry mix over 993 samples (22.5 ms, the MODE dip's slope: `snd_fade`); from then on the parts send nothing, `fx_buses` / `split_buses` / the side mixes do not run, and the output is the dry mix **bit for bit** (SPACE glides back to its middle, where its width is exactly 1 and 0, so in STEREO / LAYER the output is the dry mix once it is there: at most ~0.3 s from SPACE at +-100, 64 / 25600 a 32-sample block; at 0 at once). The first blocks while OFF clear the lines (the delay line a 8192-sample chunk a block, then the chorus and reverb lines: 9 blocks, ~6.5 ms), so **ON** starts the buses from silence, no stale tail; their return is faded in over the same 22.5 ms. Not switched: LEVEL, PAN, MASTER, the DC blocker, the limiter, OUTPUT EQ, and **every PERFORM stage** (REPEAT, REVERSE, TAPE STOP, FREEZE, OCT, KILL, FLANGER, PHASER, DRIVE, PUMP, MUTE).

**PART FX OFF.** Each part's SAT, INSERT, EQ and DUCK (its key follower too) are skipped: the part's block passes as the voices made it (PERFORM's MUTE still applies), bit for bit after a 22.5 ms crossfade between the processed and the dry block (`pfx_mix`; a silent part takes the new state at once). ON again starts the four from rest (no stale filter, SAT or insert state; a DUCK not in the middle of a dip; a PUMP keeps its place in the beat) and crossfades back. A DUCK trigger note is still taken as a trigger and swallowed as before (the routing does not change), it ducks nothing. MIDI CCs, the morph and the matrix still change the stored values; they act again when ON. *Overlap:* the separate branch wip/perffilter has an "EQ exact bypass when flat" change; this switch is its own minimal bypass (it never calls the four), nothing shared.

**The page.** Four cards named exactly ARP, SEQ, SENDS, PART FX, values ON / OFF (OFF in the dim colour; icons: the arp's, the steps', the reverb's, SAT's). The panel (0.2.24, owner: far too much text before): one line a module in M, the name and what the state means now, no ON / OFF word (the cards say that), not dimmed: ARP "ARP: PLAYS AS SET" / "ARP: KEYS PLAY DIRECTLY"; SEQ "SEQ: PLAY RUNS THE STEPS" / "SEQ: NO STEPS PLAY"; SENDS "SENDS: CHORUS DELAY REVERB" / "SENDS: DRY MIX, SAVES CPU"; PART FX "PART FX: SAT INSERT EQ DUCK" / "PART FX: BYPASSED, SAVES CPU". A switch turned says it in the header: `ARP OFF: KEYS PLAY`, `SEQ OFF: NO STEPS PLAY`, `SENDS OFF: SAVES CPU`, `PART FX OFF: SAVES CPU`, `ARP ON` .. A global page (both badges lit). OCT- + a knob = that switch ON (`RESET ARP` ..). MIDI LEARN cannot pick them (turning one while learning switches it and says `ARP: NO LEARN` ..). The GUIDE is unchanged.
**Elsewhere, a module OFF hides the cards it makes do nothing** (`card_hidden_vp`, `mods_fade`): the card is not drawn, its column stays empty, the knob does nothing and says nothing; the value is kept and acts again when the module is ON. There is no reason on the card and no footer line (until 0.2.17 the card was faded with `ARP OFF` / `SND OFF` and the footer's first row said `MODULES: SENDS OFF`):
- ARP OFF: every card of ARP 1/3, 2/3, 3/3 and QUICK ARP, but HOLD while it is OFF or ALL (it latches the keys); HOLD ARP is not shown.
- SEQ OFF: on a part whose MODE is SEQUENCER, its ARP cards (`SEQ OFF`; MODE can still be turned to another mode, which plays); QUICK SEQ's DIV and SWING (they only time the playback); AUTOMATION's PLAY. The STEP page's cards stay (editing), as do LEN and GATE (STEP PREVIEW uses GATE).
- SENDS OFF: the FX page's CHO DLY REV, GLO's CHORUS, DELAY 1/2, DELAY 2/2, REVERB (and SHIMMER) pages, FX's SEND FX cards (the twist jumps nowhere then), the MIXER's SPACE, PERFORM's K2 SPACE.
- PART FX OFF: the FX page's SAT, INSERT 1/2 and 2/2, EQ 1/2 and 2/2 (the EQ switch too), DUCK 1/2 and 2/2, GLO > SIDECHAIN's four cards.
- **EQ (0.2.25 layout round, owner): the switch below is gone.** Id 106 is a free id again (a stored value reads 0); the EQ follows its gains (a band at 0 dB is not run). Pages: **EQ 1/2 K1 LOW FREQ, K2 LOW GAIN, K3 MID FREQ, K4 MID GAIN; EQ 2/2 K1 MID Q, K2 HIGH FREQ, K3 HIGH GAIN** (two-line labels), the panel draws the curve of the three bands from fx.c eq_design's coefficients (within 0.25 dB of the exact response, `tests/eqon_test.c`). The editor's EQUALIZER card has one BYPASS (the three gains to 0 dB, remembered).
- **EQ OFF (0.2.24 .. 0.2.25, retired):** EQ 1/2's first card was the EQ's own switch (`P_EQON`, the retired DUCK NOTE id 106; stored 0 = ON, the default and what every record of before holds, 1 = OFF). OFF: the seven band cards are hidden, no EQ code runs (the gains glide 1 dB a block to flat, the states ring out through the bands as identity filters for 23 ms, then rest: no click), bit for bit a flat EQ. Pages: EQ 1/2 EQ, LOW, MID, HIGH; EQ 2/2 LO HZ, MD HZ, Q, HI HZ. `tests/eqon_test.c`.
- **DUCK SRC OFF (0.2.24, owner):** SRC is OFF, PUMP, PART or MIDI (OFF stored 3, the default of a new / INIT sound; the sparse patch store keeps PUMP as its own default so a record that left SRC out still reads PUMP): with OFF nothing ducks and RATE / THR, DEPTH, ATK, RLS, CURVE are hidden; the knob shows OFF first. The SIDECHAIN page says each part's SRC (PART 1 PUMP, PART 2 OFF ...).

**Editor.** `MENU_DESC` / `MENU_SET` items, ids **28 ARP, 29 SEQ, 30 SENDS, 31 PART FX**, values 0 OFF / 1 ON, tab index 4 named `MODULES` (no MENU tab; the device shows them on GLO); `4E 01 24` (web/EDITOR_PROTOCOL.md).

**CPU saved** (the host cost model of `cpu_scenarios_test.c`, section O: 420 k host instructions = 100 % of an audio half; the device reads ~10-25 % lower; steady state, 4 + 4 notes, MORPH fader 63):

| scene | all ON | SENDS OFF | PART FX OFF | both OFF |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M01 REESE SWAMP (WAVE / ANALOG) | 39.4 % | 24.7 (-14.7) | 36.3 (-3.1) | 21.6 (-17.8) |
| M02 (WAVE / VOICE) | 82.2 % | 61.8 (-20.4) | 80.6 (-1.6) | 60.2 (-21.9) |
| M03 (VOICE / WHEEL) | 74.5 % | 58.1 (-16.3) | 71.8 (-2.7) | 55.3 (-19.2) |
| M04 (ANALOG / ANALOG) | 79.7 % | 63.1 (-16.6) | 72.5 (-7.2) | 55.9 (-23.8) |
| M14 TEST (PHASE / ANALOG) | 87.2 % | 78.5 (-8.7) | 75.5 (-11.7) | 66.6 (-20.6) |
| M15 TEST 3 HEAVY (FM6 / PHASE) | 107.5 % | 87.9 (-19.6) | 96.5 (-10.9) | 76.9 (-30.6) |
| bench: DUEL, FM6 #2 4n + WAVE #0 4n, sends (SHIM, PING-PONG + modulation), FLANGER insert, EQ, DUCK | 82.2 % | 61.7 (-20.5) | 71.1 (-11.2) | 50.6 (-31.6) |
| idle (no note, power-on FX) | 15.0 % | 6.7 (-8.3) | 14.9 (-0.1) | 6.6 (-8.4) |

The buses run every block whatever the sends (a reverb's lines decay on), so SENDS OFF saves ~8 % even idle and 9..21 % on the factory multis; PART FX OFF saves what the parts' effects cost (0..3 % on sounds that hardly use them, ~11 % on the TEST multis and the bench, where SAT / INSERT / EQ / DUCK all run). ARP OFF and SEQ OFF save nothing measurable. The switch itself (`loadcost_test.c`, M15): SENDS OFF +0.0 %, SENDS ON +3.5 %, PART FX OFF +0.0 %, PART FX ON +1.8 % over the steady state's busiest half.

## 5g. REVERB LOW CUT (owner's decision, 2026-10-15, DUELL 0.2.14; range and default changed 2026-10-16, DUELL 0.2.22)

"Every reverb ever should have a low cut, so it doesn't do muddy basses", PLATE included. One global parameter, **G_RLOCUT**, label **LOCUT**, on the REVERB page's free fourth card (K4: TYPE, SIZE, DAMP, LOCUT).

| | |
|---|---|
| id | 21 (the unused G_ENGGO's: G_COUNT stays 27, the project and multi formats and the editor's ids do not move) |
| value | 0 = OFF, 1..127 = the corner of a one-pole high-pass, 20 Hz at 1 to 2 kHz at 127, exponential: f = 20 x 100^((v - 1) / 126) Hz (a step of 3.7 %; **0.2.22**, owner: up to 2 kHz; up to 0.2.21 it ended at 450 Hz, 22.5^, 2.5 % a step); shown OFF, in Hz below 1000 Hz and in kHz from there ("1.5 kHz", as the filter's CUT; fmt F_LOCUT 25) |
| default | **75 = 299 Hz** (0.2.22, owner: around 300 Hz; the value whose frequency is the closest to 300 Hz). Up to 0.2.21 it was 61 = 88 Hz; the value 61 is 179 Hz now. The default lives in params.c (a new unit, ERASE DATA, the OCT- + knob reset) and in the factory multi records, which carry it (tools/factory_multis_locut.py), so a factory multi has the same cut as a new unit |
| where | fx_bus.c `rev_locut`, on the reverb bus's input, after SPACE's gain and send floors and before the model (so ROOM, SPRING, HALL, SHIM, RESO and PLATE all get it), once for each bus: A, and B in SPLIT, each with its own state |
| filter | lp += k (x - lp); out = x - lp, k pre-warped (tools/gen_tables.py LOCUT_K, Q15) so the -3 dB point is the printed frequency to within 0.5 %; 6 dB an octave below it; far above it close to unity (the leaky one-pole is 2 a / (1 + a) at Nyquist: -0.3 dB at 450 Hz, -1.3 dB at 2 kHz).  The 6 dB an octave slope stays (a steeper one is a decision of its own). It removes DC too (PLATE's tank passes DC) |
| smoothness | the coefficient moves at most 1/8 of itself (plus a little) a 32-sample block, as a linear ramp over the block; OFF: the low-pass's share fades out over 1024 samples, then the state is dropped and the block is the input itself (the model sees exactly what it saw before: bit for bit); ON from OFF: from rest, the share fades in |
| other places | SPACE reads song.g, never overrides it; MODULES SENDS OFF clears the state with the lines (ON starts from rest); a model change keeps the filter running; the mode change (dm_lines_clear) clears it. The matrix cannot reach it (not in MG_ID); morph and randomise exclude it (global, Appendix B). Multis carry it (multi.c MU_GLOBAL) |
| cost | about 9.6 host instructions a sample a bus (0.16 % of the device CPU at 1.7 % per 100), 0.9 with LOCUT OFF (the call) |
| old data | no backward compatibility is needed: a save, patch or multi from before holds 0 on that id, so LOCUT is OFF there; the factory multis M01..M10 (and the test multis) held 61 (88 Hz) up to 0.2.21 and hold 75 (299 Hz) since 0.2.22; a user's saved multi or project keeps its value, which then means another frequency (pre-alpha, no migration) |
| tests | tests/locut_test.c (host_tests.sh locut) |

## 5h. BASS MONO (owner's request, DUELL 0.2.19)

Wide effects (SPACE, the chorus, SPREAD, PING-PONG, the reverbs) put bass into the side of the stereo picture. In a club the bass must be mono (a PA and a subwoofer add the two sides, phase differences cancel), and on vinyl a wide bass makes the needle jump. **BASS MONO** makes the master output mono below a frequency you choose and leaves it stereo above it.

| | |
|---|---|
| where | **A sound setting, not a MENU one** (owner, 2026-10-15: "MENU is for settings not related to the sound design"). The page **BASS MONO** of the **GLO** family (owner, 2026-10-16), after the effects and before COMP: TEMPO, CHORUS, DELAY 1/2, DELAY 2/2, REVERB, SHIMMER, **BASS MONO**, COMP, MODULES, SIDECHAIN, MIDI, MIDI LEARN (0.2.21: the sound pages first, the MIDI pages after). It is the **master-stage page**: its title is BASS MONO while that is its only card, and it will be renamed **MASTER** when the master gets more cards (width, compressor; 0.2.25: the page MASTER keeps its name and its one card, BASS MONO, the saturator of 0.2.24 being removed; the title is one string, `MASTER_PAGE_TITLE` in params.c and `MASTER_TITLE` in the editor's layout.js): **K1 BMONO** (the card's label; BASS MONO in full in the header and the editor), K2..K4 free; the master compressor is the next page, COMP (section 5j). It is **global** (a GLO page holds global controls only, `SC_GLOBAL`) (not per part): the stage is one M/S stage on the master output, so a per-part value would have nothing to act on. MENU > AUDIO has USB LEVEL, CLICK, CLICK LEVEL, COUNT-IN, TUNE (OUTPUT EQ was removed in 0.2.19). Editor: the GLOBAL tab, the BASS MONO card (MENU setting id 33, tab MASTER) |
| value | OFF, then the corner: 40, 50, 63, 80, 100, 125, 160, 200, 250, 315, 400 Hz (a third of an octave a step; 100 to 150 Hz is the usual club corner). **Default OFF** (a new unit, ERASE DATA, an INIT of the power-on state). The card shows the value as the frequency (label BASS MONO, value "100 Hz") |
| DUEL | **Not run in DUEL** (part 1 hard left, part 2 hard right for an external mixer: the stage would mix the two parts). The **card is not drawn** in DUEL (its knob does nothing; the page stays, as the home of what comes next) and the stage is skipped (a render with it set is bit for bit the render without). The value is kept for STEREO and LAYER. The editor cannot see the mode, so it keeps showing the card (with a note) |
| what it does | the side (L - R) goes through a 2-pole Butterworth high-pass (12 dB an octave, -3 dB at the printed frequency, within 0.5 dB: tested at all eleven); the part the filter takes out is moved from the two channels (L - d/2, R + d/2), so L + R is **not touched** and below the corner L = R. At 100 Hz a 40 Hz out-of-phase tone is down 16 dB, at 160 Hz down 24 dB; a 2 kHz out-of-phase tone passes within 0.01 dB. The wet bus is mono, so it is no side and is not read |
| order of the stages | the stereo effects (sends, SPACE width, PERFORM's stages, the MODE's dip) -> **BASS MONO** -> the fixed reference level (MASTER_USB_REF; linear, so the order with it does not matter) -> DC blocker -> limiter -> soft clip -> USB; then MASTER's volume for the DAC (audio.c master_dac). The limiter sees the mono bass, as the speaker does. The USB audio and the DAC are one output (`uac_tap` reads it): **USB capture gets BASS MONO too**, and USB LEVEL FIXED does not change it. The metronome click is added after (DAC only) |
| smoothness | ON: the filter runs from rest while its share fades in over 1024 samples (23 ms); OFF: the share fades out, then the state is dropped; a change of corner moves the coefficients 1/16 of the way a block (11.6 ms) and in straight lines inside the block, so a turned knob is no zipper and no click (tested: every jump of the range, ON / OFF, with a bass side sounding) |
| OFF | the stage is not called: two compares a block in `mix_block` (`bm_sel`, `bm.a`), and the output is **bit for bit** what it was (tests: the sources-before hash; ON then OFF returns to the never-ON mix exactly after the fade) |
| other places | MASTER (the knob: the volume of the DAC feed, after the soft clip, 0.2.25) and PERFORM (its stages come before) work as ever; USB audio is fixed. MODULES SENDS OFF and STOP ALL do not clear it (it holds only a 23 ms memory of the side; the sends' lines are not part of it); a change of the MODE between DUEL and the others clears it (`dm_lines_clear`), so STEREO starts it from rest |
| stored | **With the music, not the settings**: `song.bm` (core.h `G_BM`, the pseudo id past the 27 stored globals, so `G_COUNT`, the multi and project sizes do not move), 0 OFF .. 11. **Not in the multis since 0.2.21** (owner, 2026-10-16: a master effect is the device's): byte 5 bits 1..4 of the record (0.2.19 and 0.2.20 stored it there) are written 0 and ignored when read, so a multi of 0.2.19 / 0.2.20 still loads, and a load leaves the unit's BASS MONO as it is (a stray bit 5..7 still makes the record no multi); the format version did not move. In the **project** (the autosave and the editor's backup: byte 65, the reserved byte, written 0 before; the hash covers it) and the autosave notices a change of it. Not in the settings record ([15][29], the retired STYLE byte, is ignored). `ui_input` copies it to the audio side every frame |
| cost | `bass_mono` is a `noinline` function in the flash (not in the hot RAM block: unchanged, 200 B free): 39.3 host instructions a sample steady, 60.3 while the corner or the share moves (about 0.7 % and 1.0 % of the device CPU at 1.7 % per 100); OFF: two compares per 32 samples |
| tests | tests/bassmono_test.c (host_tests.sh bassmono) |

## 5i. SHIM PITCH and MOD (owner's request, deferred to the next release, DUELL 0.2.19)

The SHIM reverb is ROOM plus a pitch-shifted copy of its own output fed back into its input (two crossfaded grains reading a 2048-sample line; section 5c's JIANT port). It had one fixed return, an octave up. Two global parameters change the return; they matter only while the reverb TYPE is SHIM.

| | |
|---|---|
| PITCH | **UP** (the octave up: the SHIM as it was), **DOWN** (an octave down: the same two grains at half the write speed, a warm bed under the sound), ~~BOTH~~ (0.2.19 .. 0.2.24: the two returns together; **removed in 0.2.25 by the owner**: it cost about 12 % of the half in DUEL and 5 to 6 % otherwise. A stored BOTH, value 2, in a multi, the project or the autosave plays UP: `project.c` reads it as UP, `fx_bus.c rev_shimx` plays anything but DOWN as UP. M16 STRESS TEST, the one factory multi that held BOTH, keeps its record and plays UP). Default UP |
| MOD | 0..100 %, default **0** = off. A slow drift of the return's pitch: two sines at 0.29 Hz and 0.47 Hz (they never line up) move the read speed, up to about plus or minus 25 cents at 100 % (measured plus or minus 23 over 40 s), so the shimmered part does not stand still and the partials it feeds back spread a little on each pass. Medium (40 to 50) is plus or minus 10 cents |
| ids | PITCH is **id 26** (G_FREE26, the old PING-PONG switch; stored 0 since 0.2.16), MOD is **id 17** (G_NAME, a card "NAME" that no page ever showed). Both read 0 in every older save, multi and project, and 0 is the old sound, so **G_COUNT stays 27, the multi version (4) and the project revision (2) do not change**: a 0.2.18 record loads and sounds the same, and a 0.2.19 record loads in 0.2.18 with the two values ignored. The editor protocol has the two ids as any global (`GET`, `SET`, `DESC`). Multis carry them (multi.c MU_GLOBAL), projects store them with the other globals; the factory multis hold 0 and 0 (not changed) |
| page | REVERB keeps TYPE, SIZE, DAMP, LOCUT. A second page, **SHIMMER**, follows it: K1 PITCH, K2 MOD (K3 and K4 empty). **SHIMMER is in the page cycle only while the TYPE is SHIM**: the GLO button and SELECT skip it for ROOM, SPRING, HALL, RESO and PLATE (ui.c page_visible, the same mechanism that skips VCF for an engine with no such filter), so there is no empty page. It is a page of the GLO family (0.2.21; the FX family up to 0.2.20), after REVERB and before the BASS MONO page. If the TYPE changes while SHIMMER is open (the editor, a multi, MIDI), the screen goes to REVERB. With MODULES SENDS OFF both cards are not drawn, as REVERB's. In the editor the SHIMMER card is shown only for TYPE SHIM |
| sound | the plain loop (PITCH UP, MOD 0) is the code of 0.2.18 untouched: bit for bit (the factory multis, every golden). Anything else runs `rev_shimx` (fx_bus.c, in the flash, not in the fast RAM block): the grains read in Q16 with linear interpolation. A change of PITCH fades the return out (10 ms), restarts the grains and fades it in; MOD on and off keeps the grains' phase (the plain loop's phase is taken over, and handed back, rounded to a sample, when PITCH is UP and MOD 0 again) |
| other places | SPACE reads SIZE as before; SENDS OFF and STOP ALL's flush clear the state with the line (rev_clear); LOCUT is unchanged and acts on the reverb input, the return is not filtered by it; bus B (DUEL / SPLIT) has its own state and reads the same two globals; the tails end in exact silence for every PITCH and MOD (loud noise, then nothing; SIZE 90 about 5 s, 127 about 9 s, 150 about 17 to 29 s) |
| cost | the SHIM alone, host instructions a sample: 150.9 before, 151.3 after (the default: one extra test per block), MOD 204.8 (+54), DOWN 200.8 (+50), BOTH 239.7 (+89); the device estimate is 1.7 % per 100: +0.9 %, +0.9 %, +1.5 % of the CPU while SHIM runs with them (ROOM alone 94) |
| size | the app 430,876 B (+1,800), the pool spare 29,172 B (-28: the new state of both buses), the fast RAM block 12,372 B of 12,512 (+60: only the call in rev_shim; rev_shimx is in the flash), elfcheck ok |
| tests | tests/shimmer_test.c (host_tests.sh shimmer): default bit for bit, the pitch of a sine through the return, MOD's range, no click, silence after the tails, buses, STOP ALL, the page cycle, cards, cost |

## 5j. COMP, the master compressor (owner's request, DUELL 0.2.21)

A compressor on the whole output, for gluing the mix and taming loud peaks. **One page, four knobs, an automatic makeup, and RATIO 1:1 (the default) is no code at all.** (The stereo widener was left out for now; a master saturator, added in 0.2.23, was removed again in 0.2.25: section 5m.) Design and measurements: `docs/MODULATION_AND_MASTER_STUDY.md` (private); this section is what was built.

| | |
|---|---|
| layout round | **0.2.25 (owner): the page is COMPRESSOR, after STEREO MASTER; K1 RATIO (its lowest value shows OFF: the stored 1:1, so a stored ratio keeps its meaning and the default is unchanged), K2 THRESH, K3 ATTACK, K4 RELEASE; the panel draws the transfer curve (cmp_gr, cmp_mk) and a live gain-reduction bar (0 .. 12 dB). The table below is the 0.2.21 state.** |
| where | **A sound setting, not a MENU one**: the page **COMP** of the **GLO** family, right after BASS MONO (GLO, TEMPO, SIDECHAIN, BASS MONO, **COMP**, MIDI, MODULES, MIDI LEARN). Global (`SC_GLOBAL`), four cards: **K1 THRESH, K2 RATIO, K3 ATTACK, K4 RELEASE** (top-down: what it catches, how hard, how fast). Editor: the GLOBAL tab, a COMP card beside BASS MONO (MENU settings 34..37, tab MASTER) |
| THRESH | -40 .. 0 dB in 1 dB steps, default **-17 dB**. The dB are those of the output at the default MASTER (0 dB = the full scale the output has with the MASTER knob at its default; the compressor works before MASTER, so the knob is a pure volume after it and never changes what is compressed) |
| RATIO | **1:1** (off), 1.5:1, 2:1, 3:1, 4:1, 6:1, 8:1, 12:1, INF. **Default 1:1**: nothing changes for anyone who does not use it |
| ATTACK | 0.1 .. 100 ms, 19 steps (0.1, 0.15, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10, 15, 20, 30, 50, 70, 100 ms; about 6 a decade), default 10 ms. The time constant of the level detector (the time to cover 63 % of a step up) |
| RELEASE | 20 ms .. 2 s, 13 steps (20, 30, 50, 70, 100, 150, 200, 300, 500, 700 ms, 1, 1.5, 2 s), default 200 ms. The time to cover 63 % of a fall, the detector's hold included (the hold is 11.6 .. 13 ms, so the 20 ms setting is that hold and a pole of 8 ms); measured within 1.5 % of the printed time at all 13 |
| knee | **Fixed soft knee, 6 dB** (a quadratic joins the line below the threshold to the ratio's line above it) |
| makeup | **Automatic and fixed**, computed from THRESH and RATIO: the gain reduction the same curve gives a level of **-8 dB** (on the THRESH scale), at most +12 dB. A level of -8 dB leaves the compressor as loud as it came in (unity there), quieter signals are raised by the makeup, louder ones are brought down by what the curve takes less the makeup. So turning it on does not make the music louder: a signal at the limiter's threshold (-5.2 dB) is never louder out than in, at any ratio or threshold (tested), and the loudness of the three demo pieces stays within 1.3 dB of the OFF render (RMS; their peaks within 0.4 dB). A fixed rule, not an average-following one: the makeup is exactly one number per setting (+6.75 dB at -17 dB / 4:1, so a quiet passage below the knee is lifted by that), and a transient that gets past a slow ATTACK is raised by it too (the limiter is after) |
| detector | **Stereo-linked**: max(|L|, |R|) is the level, both channels get one gain (an L-only loud signal lowers R equally: tested). A hold of the peak of the last 8 blocks of 64 samples, so a bass of 87 Hz or more does not ripple the gain; a smoothed level (Q12, a product of the step and an 8-bit coefficient shifted down: no stalling short of the target, as a Q16 coefficient does by about 2 dB). The gain is computed every 8th sample (0.18 ms) from the level by a leading-zero count, a 128-entry table and a quadratic knee, and runs as a straight line over the 8 samples |
| where in the chain | `mix_block`: the stereo effects (sends, SPACE's width, PERFORM's stages, the MODE's dip) -> BASS MONO -> **COMP** -> the fixed reference level -> DC blocker -> limiter -> soft clip -> USB; then MASTER's volume for the DAC (audio.c master_dac). The wet bus's mono return is folded into the mix when the stage runs. USB audio and the DAC get it (one output); USB LEVEL FIXED is after it |
| off | RATIO 1:1: the stage is **not called** (one load and a test a block, `(cmp_cfg & 0xF00) \|\| cmp.a`), and the output is **bit for bit** what it was (the sources-before hash; every factory multi's render, `mrender`). Changing THRESH, ATTACK or RELEASE at 1:1 changes nothing, and their cards are **not drawn** at 1:1 (they have nothing to act on) |
| DUEL | **Not run in DUEL** (its sides are the two parts: a linked gain would pump part 2 from part 1). The four cards are **not drawn** in DUEL (the page stays, as BASS MONO's) and the stage is skipped (a render with it set is bit for bit the render without). The values are kept for STEREO and LAYER; a change of the MODE clears the stage's state |
| smoothness | RATIO 1:1 <-> a ratio fades the effect in or out over 15 ms (the state runs from the first block's peak, so the first sound is not boosted); THRESH, the ratio's slope and the makeup glide to a new setting (a 40 dB threshold sweep in 38 ms); tested: 1:1 <-> 4:1 eight times and the settings walked over their whole range with a tone and a bass sounding, no step in the output faster than the signal's own slope (times 1.05, and 1.6 for the walk, whose net gain is up to +2.4 dB) |
| stored | **Device-wide, in the settings record, not in a multi, a project or a patch** (owner: master effects are probably not part of multis). In the retired `rsv` word of the record (`persist_t`: the old OUTPUT EQ field, written 0 until now): `0xC7 << 24 \| THRESH (0..40) \| RATIO (0..8) << 6 \| ATTACK (0..18) << 10 \| RELEASE (0..12) << 15`. A word without the tag 0xC7 (0, as every older record has, or the old OUTPUT EQ's 0..2) reads as the defaults; a field out of range reads as its own default (tested with every byte value in each of the four bytes and random words). The record's size and magic (PER4) are as ever, so **no version changes and nothing is refused**; a unit that never touches COMP writes exactly what it did. Saved ~2 s after the last turn (the settings timer). ERASE DATA resets it. `song.cp[0..3]` (core.h `G_CT .. G_CL`, pseudo ids past BASS MONO's `G_BM`; `ui.c cmp_sync`) hold the RAM values; `ui_input` copies them to the audio side (`cmp_cfg`) every frame |
| other places | **MORPH, MIDI LEARN and the matrix** reach part parameters only: COMP is not learnable and not morphed (as BASS MONO). **STOP ALL, SENDS OFF, MODULES** need no handling (a master stage; the state is a ~12 ms hold and a smoothed level). **PERFORM**: its stages come before, so DRIVE, PUMP and REPEAT are compressed. While a PERFORM effect is held its master stage has already applied MASTER's gain to the mix (`perf_master`), so the compressor corrects its level by log2(1024 / MASTER gain) (`cmp_poff`): THRESH is the same with PERFORM held or idle and at any MASTER knob (tested at five knob positions) |
| later | The gain computer (`cmp_gr`) is where an upward branch would go for an OTT-style mode (a second, lower threshold): not built |
| cost | `comp_stage` is a `noinline` function in the flash (not in the hot RAM block): **53.1 host instructions per sample** while it runs steady, 58.0 while the settings move (`comp_test`, the repo's counter, `-O1`; about 1.6 % of the device CPU at 33 instructions per 1 %, 1.2 % to 1.4 % on the device by the usual 0.75 to 0.9); 1:1: nothing. Device build: app 437,216 B (+3,228 B; the limit is 581,564), RAM .data+.bss 51,232 B (+128), pool unchanged, the start-up layout check (elfcheck) passes. `comp_stage` 1,148 B, the tables about 590 B |
| tests | tests/comp_test.c (host_tests.sh comp): 1:1 bit for bit, 480 steady tones within 0.1 dB of the ideal curve, ATTACK and RELEASE measured within 15 % (they come out within 1.5 %), the stereo link, silence, overshoot, no click, DUEL, the page, the stored word, cost; `comp_test DEMODIR` writes the listening set. tools/comp_tables.py prints the tables `fx.c` carries |

## 5k. STEREO CHORUS (owner's request, 2026-10-16, DUELL 0.2.21)

"Make the chorus stereo." Until 0.2.20 the global chorus bus was ONE modulated tap of a 2048-sample line (a delay of 400 samples plus a swing of 0 .. DEPTH x 6 samples, an LFO at RATE) summed into the bus's mid: it thickened the sound and added no width. Now, in **STEREO and LAYER**, it is a classic stereo chorus. No new knob: RATE and DEPTH (the CHORUS page) mean what they meant.

| | |
|---|---|
| taps | two reads of the same line (no new RAM): the **left** tap is the old tap, exactly (LFO phase 0); the **right** tap runs `CHO_OFF` = **a quarter turn (90 degrees)** behind it in the same LFO (`fx_bus.c cho_tap`, one more sine and one more interpolated read a sample) |
| why 90 degrees, not 180 | measured, for DEPTH 30 / 60 / 127 at 200 Hz .. 3 kHz (the swing of the mono sum's comb, dry + (left + right) / 2, over one LFO cycle): the old mono chorus 53 .. 66 dB peak to peak, **90 degrees 11 .. 50 dB (mostly 30 .. 50)**, 180 degrees only 4 .. 18 dB. At 180 degrees the two taps are mirror images around the centre delay, their average does not move, and the mono sum has almost no chorus left. At 90 degrees the sum still sweeps, and left and right are still far apart (the read points of the two channels differ by 0 .. 0.7 x the swing, the inter-channel correlation of a pulse train or noise 0.00, of a held chord 0.56) |
| where it goes | the mid ((left + right) / 2) to the wet bus as the mono chorus did; half the difference ((left - right) / 2) as a side to the left and from the right of the dry mix, the way PING-PONG's and HALL's sides go (`mix_l`, `mix_r`, inside the chorus loop: no extra buffer, and **not through SPREAD**, the width is fixed). Every chorus loop has it: the plain bus (`fx_buses_m`), the delay's modulation (`dly_mod`), RANDOM / OCT UP (`dly_ev`), with the delay's PING-PONG on too |
| mono | the left channel alone is the old chorus (within 1 LSB, rounding of the half-sum), so the built-in speaker (one channel) hears the chorus as before. The mono sum (L + R) / 2 has the chorus too (see above), the mid a little quieter than the old tap (the two taps average: the held SOFT PAD chord -0.7 dB) |
| DUEL | **unchanged**: no side there (each part is dual mono, its bus A or B sums to the part's own side), so the one tap, bit for bit (`mode_test.c` leak map: the other channel exactly 0, with the chorus on; `stereochorus_test.c`). SPLIT's buses A and B run the one-tap code |
| unchanged, bit for bit | DEPTH 0 (both taps are the same, the side is exactly 0 and the mid the old tap), a chorus send of 0, DUEL, the delay, the reverbs, PING-PONG, PERFORM, MODULES SENDS OFF, STOP ALL's flush (the chorus line is cleared with `snd_lines_clear`; the second tap reads the same line), the factory multis M01 .. M10 (their renders are the same hashes), the DEPTH glide (`cho_glide`, both taps use the same depth) and the click behaviour of RATE / DEPTH changes (the left channel is the old signal; the right moves like it) |
| SPACE | no double widening: SPACE's width (`space_width`) works on the finished left and right, the chorus side is just part of the side it sees. Measured with SPACE +100 and the chorus at DEPTH 127: peak under 19200 of 32767, left and right within 3 dB |
| sound | the goldens of every sound and multi that sends to the chorus in STEREO or LAYER changed on purpose (104 of 210 regress renders, M13 / M14 / M16 of the 42 multi renders); with the second tap on the first (`-DCHO_OFF=0u`) all of them are the old ones again |
| cost | the bus stage +41.6 host instructions a sample while the chorus loop runs (the idle mix 326 -> 372; the device estimate by the usual 1.7 % per 100 rule: +0.8 % of the CPU), DUEL none. **Left out while silent (fx_bus.c `cho_zrun`):** the plain bus (`fx_buses_m`) skips the chorus loop in a block whose chorus input is all 0 while the whole 2048-sample chorus line is 0 (a send of 0, or nothing playing; it starts 2048 samples after the last sound): then every tap would read 0, so the result is the same bit for bit; only the write index and the LFO phase move on, as the loop leaves them. The scan costs a few instructions a block when the bus sounds (it stops at the first non-zero sample). The idle mix is 316 again (0.2.20: 326). The delay's modulation (`dly_mod`, in the hot block), RANDOM / OCT UP (`dly_ev`) always run the chorus. Device build (`-Os`): the fast RAM block 12,348 -> 12,500 B of 12,512 (**12 B free**, was 164: `dly_mod` and `fx_buses_mod` grew with the second tap; `cho_zrun` and the skip are in the flash), elfcheck ok |
| tests | `tests/stereochorus_test.c` (`host_tests.sh stereochorus`; STEREO and LAYER, the four bus paths): correlation of left and right under 0.6 on a mono pulse train and on noise, the left is the old chorus, the mid is bounded and moves, DEPTH 0 and DUEL / SPLIT A and B and a send of 0 bit for bit (hashes of 0.2.20), DUEL isolation, no click, exact silence after the tails, SPACE +100, cost. `mode_test.c` MODE_STEREO_HASH, `reverb_test.c` ROOM reference and `cpu_baseline.txt` were re-based for it. `tests/chorusskip_test.c` (`host_tests.sh chorusskip`, built twice, with and without `-DCHO_NOSKIP`): the skip is invisible: every output sample and the chorus lines and states hash the same over silence, notes starting and stopping, DEPTH / RATE changes, the delay paths and a fuzz, in STEREO, LAYER and DUEL (`dlymod_test.c` and `dlyev_test.c` are built with `CHO_NOSKIP`: they compare the delay paths' costs) |

## 5l. WIDTH, the master stereo width (owner's request, DUELL 0.2.23)

**0.2.25 layout round (owner):** WIDTH and BASS MONO are one page, **STEREO MASTER** (K1 BASS MONO, two lines BASS / MONO; K2 WIDTH), where WIDTH and MASTER were; its panel pictures the mono region below BASS MONO and the width above it (wider only above 125 Hz). Nothing of the stages or their stored bytes changed.

| | |
|---|---|
| where | The page **WIDTH** of the **GLO** family, right before MASTER (the master pages follow the chain: WIDTH, MASTER (BASS MONO), COMP; the master saturator of 0.2.23 / 0.2.24 is removed). Global (`SC_GLOBAL`), one card: **K1 WIDTH**. Editor: the GLOBAL tab's MASTER card (MENU setting 42, tab MASTER) |
| WIDTH | 0 .. 200 %, 1 % a detent, default **100 %** (= off). Mid / side: the mid (L + R) is never touched; the side (L - R) changes by k = (WIDTH - 100) / 100 of itself (L + d, R - d, d = k x side / 2). 0 % = mono, 200 % = the side x 2 (+6 dB) |
| bass | **Wider (k > 0) only above a crossover of 125 Hz**: the side through a 2-pole Butterworth high-pass (BASS MONO's filter and its 125 Hz coefficients, `BM_C[5]`), so the bass is never made wider (+0.6 dB of side at 50 Hz at 200 %, +6.0 dB at 1 kHz; tested against the ideal within 0.15 dB). **Narrower (k < 0) on the whole side** (no filter): toward mono it narrows the bass too, which is always safe. BASS MONO comes after it, so below BASS MONO's corner the low end stays mono whatever WIDTH does (a 60 Hz side through 200 % and BASS MONO 160 Hz: -18.6 dB) |
| where in the chain | `mix_block`: the sends, SPACE's width, PERFORM's stages, the MODE's dip -> **WIDTH** -> BASS MONO -> COMP -> MASTER's level -> DC blocker -> limiter -> soft clip. (The request said after COMP; it sits before BASS MONO so BASS MONO keeps the last word on the low end, and COMP sees the mix as it will be heard.) The wet bus is mono (no side): it is not read |
| off | 100 %: the stage is **not called** (`wd_sel != 100 \|\| wdn.k`), the output **bit for bit** what it was (the sources-before hash). A mono source stays mono at every setting; the mono sum is exactly the same at every setting (tested) |
| smoothness | k glides 32 / 32768 a sample (0 -> +-100 % in 23 ms); back at 100 % k ends at 0 exactly, the crossover's states are dropped and the gate shuts. Tested: 100 -> 200 -> 0 -> 100 % under a tone, no step faster than the signal's own slope |
| DUEL | **Not run** (its sides are part 1 and part 2, dual mono). The card is **not drawn** in DUEL; the panel says NOT IN DUEL MODE, PART 1 LEFT, PART 2 RIGHT, NO STEREO MIX TO WIDEN (`ui_graph.c graph_mstg`). A change of the MODE clears its state |
| stored | **Device-wide**, like COMP: the settings record's bytes `favorites.factory[15][18..21]` (bytes of the FX layer's key map, retired in 0.2.2): [18] the tag 0xB7, [19] WIDTH, [20] the removed saturator's DRIVE (section 5m: never read, written 0), [21] BASS MONO + 1. Without the tag (every older record) the defaults; a value out of range reads as its default (every byte value tested). Not in a multi, a project or a patch; a load never changes it; ERASE DATA resets it. `song.ms[0]` (core.h `G_WD`, a pseudo id past COMP's), `ui.c ms_sync` copies it to the audio side (`wd_sel`) every frame. Not learnable, not morphed |
| cost | `width_stage`, `noinline`, in the flash: **38 host instructions per sample** steady (wider; narrower less), 47 while it glides (`master_test`); about 1.2 % at 33 a percent; 100 %: nothing |

## 5m. SATURATOR, the master saturator: removed (owner, DUELL 0.2.25)

The master saturator (0.2.23 as its own GLO page, 0.2.24 as K2 DRIVE of the page MASTER; a tanh curve at 2x oversampling after COMP) is **removed completely** (owner: users must never get used to it). What is left of it:

| | |
|---|---|
| firmware | `sat_stage`, `sat_chan`, `sat_tables.h` and `tools/master_tables.py` are gone, with the parameter `G_SD` (DRIVE), its format `F_DRIVE`, the state in `song.ms[]` (it holds WIDTH alone, `ms[1]`) and the audio side's `sat_sel` / `sat`. The page **MASTER** (the name stays) holds **one card, K1 BASS MONO** (K2 to K4 empty) |
| stored value | the settings record's byte `favorites.factory[15][20]` (it held DRIVE) is **never read** and written 0 when the settings are saved after a change here; a unit that had DRIVE set reads as no saturator, WIDTH and BASS MONO as they were. No record is refused |
| editor | MENU setting **id 43 (DRIVE) is gone and not reused**: `MENU_DESC` lists 34 items (`4E 01 34`); a `MENU_SET` of 43 answers rc 1 (unknown id) like any unassigned id. layout.json: `MSTG` holds the WIDTH descriptor alone, no `G_SD`; the editor's MASTER group has the cards COMPRESSOR, BASS MONO and STEREO WIDTH |
| sound | the saturator was OFF by default and nothing stored it in a patch or multi, so no factory sound or multi changes: every golden render is bit for bit what it was |
| cost | none left; the bench row `master/SATURATOR` (168 host instructions a sample at DRIVE 60) is removed from `bench_baseline.txt` |
| tests | `tests/master_test.c` keeps WIDTH, the pages, the stored bytes (including a 0.2.24 record with DRIVE 71) and the mix; its saturator tests are removed |

## 6. CPU and engineering plan

- Measure first: in the emulator (Felucca's instruction counts per sample per preset) and on the device when flashing is set up. The first deliverable is a table: cost per engine per voice, cost of the new LFO/envelope/matrix, cost of a waveform cross-fade.
- **Parts are tracks 1 and 2 of Felucca's 4-track structure.** Changing the track count to 2 breaks the saved-data layouts (static asserts in `core.h`) and about 120 test references, so tracks 3 and 4 are disabled instead: not shown, no voices, their MIDI channels ignored. Storage formats and the regression suite stay valid. (Tried and reverted: NPART 2.) **Superseded (memory trim 1, before the alpha): NPART is 2 now; tracks 3 and 4, and the song chain block of the project file, are gone from memory and from the stored formats (project revision 5; older projects are refused).**
- **Storage decision (2026-10-10, from the step-3 finding):** the project file has room for 4 more parameters per track, step 3 needs 23, and parameter ids are 7-bit in motion, user presets, MIDI LEARN and the editor protocol (limit 127). DUELL (a) stores only parts A and B in the project file (new format FUN11; older projects still load by count), and (b) reuses the 29 dead parameter ids (P_FM1_ATK..P_FM4_LEVEL, P_LN0..7, P_ED_FX) for the new parameters, so no format grows. This ends the FELUCCA_FM4 and FELUCCA_DRUM build options and Felucca-project compatibility, which DUELL does not need. About 22 ids stay free for the morph settings and the arp target.
- Build order: (1) rename and rebuild in the emulator, tests green; (2) two parts and Stereo/DUEL; (3) UI fixes (1.1-1.3) with the SELECT/tempo test; (4) second LFO, second envelope, 8-slot matrix (needs the storage decision below); (5) MORPH with undo; (6) MIDI map and the host tests; (7) polish, CPU report; (8) device build (needs Docker, the JieLi toolchain and 3 SDK files).
- Every step ends with the host regression suite green (`tests/run_tests.sh`), golden hashes updated only for intended sound changes.

### 6.1 CPU load meter, splash, ABOUT, version name (owner's requests, built 2026-10-12)

- **Build switch (owner, 2026-10-12): `DUELL_TEST_MODE`** (a compile-time define, `core.h`, default 0 = the release). Off, the official beta has **no MENU > SYSTEM > TEST** (no row, no hidden `TEST_PAGE`, no drawing or knob code; the enum values `SC_TEST` / `GR_TEST` and the editor-protocol menu id stay reserved, no other id moves) and **no CPU test multis M13..M16** (their slots read empty like M11, M12; `tools/gen_duell_multis.py` leaves them out of `duell_multis.h` and its checks; the `.bin` sources and `tests/gen_test_multis.c` stay). ABOUT's CPU line (since power-on) is kept. On: `DUELL_TEST_MODE=1` in the environment for `tools/build.py` / `build.sh` (device), `web/emu/build.sh` (emulator; regenerate with the same switch first, `multi.c` refuses a mismatch), `host_tests.sh` (its test binaries are built with it on; the `release` group, `tests/release_test.c`, builds with it off). The text on the TEST screen and M13..M16 below applies to developer builds only.
- **CPU load meter** (on the **MENU > SYSTEM > TEST** screen since 0.2.2, section below; it was GLO > TEST in 0.2.1, and before that MENU > SYSTEM > INFO; ABOUT keeps its CPU line, and now the USB line and the firmware version that INFO showed). The description that follows is the meter's, as it appears on the TEST screen. Placement: TEST is reached with SELECT (rows) and KNOB 1 right or OCT+ (open) like ABOUT (section 6.2); no new button. It shows **AVERAGE 1 S** (percent of the half buffer's time, with a bar and a mark at 80 %), **PEAK HOLD** (the longest half of the last second, held 4 s, then it falls to the latest), **MISSED SINCE POWER-ON** (halves that took longer than their time, or that the DMA overtook) and a word: OK, NEAR THE LIMIT (average 80 % and up, amber), AT THE LIMIT (95 % and up, red), BLOCKS MISSED JUST NOW (a miss in the last 5 s, red). Flat fills only (HI-CON included); GREY and MONO have no colours, the word carries it. The always-visible indicator is the CPU bar in the header (0.2.9, below); no footer dot.
- **What it measures.** Device: `fm1_ticks()` (TIMER4, 24 MHz) across one I2S half's render in the audio ISR (`audio.c fm1_alnk0_irq`), minus the TIMER5 ticks nested in it (the key scan): the audio's own time against the half's budget (`CPU_BUDGET_US` = 128 frames / 44.1 kHz). The ISR only adds to `cpu_sum` and `cpu_n`, keeps `cpu_pk` and `cpu_miss` (`core.h`): three adds, one compare, no division. The counters run all the time (the ISR code is the same three adds and a compare, nothing was added in 0.2.9). The UI reads them once a second while TEST or ABOUT is shown (`ui_menu.c cpu_meter_poll`) and, since 0.2.9, while the header is drawn (the CPU bar, below; `ui_draw.c cpu_bar_poll`, its own window, it never clears `cpu_pk`). The older 93 ms IIR (`song.cpu_q8`, console `cpu_pct`) is unchanged. Emulator (`FELUCCA_METER_EST`, `web/emu/felucca_web.c`): the browser has no clock in the audio worklet, so the meter shows an **estimate**: 16 % for the buses plus 10 % per sounding voice (8 voices read 96 %), labelled EMULATOR ESTIMATE on the screen; only the device measures. Host tests drive the counters directly (`tests/audio_test.c`, `tests/splash_test.c`).
- **CPU bar (DUELL 0.2.9, owner request: "a tiny CPU meter in the top right corner next to the battery on all pages").** A gauge in the header, **x 215..236, y 19..23** (22 x 5 px, directly under the battery's ink, which ends above row 19, down to the header's last row; it takes no room from the octave readout or the page name, whose limits x 208 / 212 are unchanged). It shows the audio load of the last second (`ui_draw.c cpu_bar_poll`: the sum of the halves' render times over the budget of the halves counted, from the counters above), in **8 steps** (any load above zero shows at least one; the header redraws only when the step or the colour changes, so its redraw cost is unchanged). Colour: the theme colour below 70 %, amber from 70 %, REC red from 85 % (the TEST card's colours, `cpu_col`: HI-CON's theme is red, so there the text colour stands for "fine"; GREY and MONO have one colour, the length tells); the empty part of the bar is the DIM colour. A half that missed its time in the last second turns the **whole bar** REC red for the next second. Shown wherever the header is drawn (all pages, layers, the MENU's TEST screen, NAME, dialogs that keep the header, the count-in); not on the splash, the ABOUT animation or screens without a header (the MENU's tab list). MENU > DISPLAY > CPU METER OFF hides it. After a gap of 4 s or more without a header (a menu) the window starts afresh, so an old miss is not shown. Emulator: the bar shows the same voice-count estimate as ABOUT (16 % + 10 % per sounding voice), not a measurement. Cost: no instruction was added to the audio ISR (the counters were always running); the UI does one poll a second (a few dozen instructions) plus the 110 px of the bar when the header redraws. Host tests: `tests/ui_test.c test_cpu_bar` (thresholds, flash, pixels, every palette, the setting's round trip), `tests/ui_render.c` (the lint with the bar in every scene and a header sweep over every palette and step), `tests/editor_test.c` (id 26).
- **Splash** (`ui_draw.c splash_frame`): at power-on and after 'Switch on' in the emulator, 1.2 s and then straight to the normal screen, any button, key or knob ends it (and is used up by that); the synth is already running (the first frame is drawn at `lcd_init`, the rest by the main loop, no delay), MIDI notes play through it, the keys after 0.1 s. The title block is a **graphic**, the art director's lockup `felucca-base/assets/splash_logo.svg` (source of truth; viewBox 480 = the screen at 2x: `DUELL` as an embedded PNG, `MultiMorphing` / `Bitimbral Synthesizer` and `by OSCILL/R` as vector outlines), used exactly as designed (at 1x: ink from x = 22, `DUELL` at y = 64, the tagline from y = 119, `by OSCILL/R` at y about 174..187), not drawn from a font. `tools/gen_splash_logo.py` (run by `build.py` and `gen_only.py`) rasterises it at build time to a 1x coverage mask (paths flattened and filled even-odd at 8x8 supersampling, the PNG's alpha resampled by 0.5, box-filtered), cropped to its ink box (166 x 124) and stored as `build/gen/splash_logo.h`: 4-bit coverage as a byte run list (about 4.3 KB, decoded in `splash_logo_draw` without RAM). It is alpha-blended in the theme's one text colour over the lines (HI-CON: its text colour), nothing behind it. The only background is 15 thin lines in the MORPH page's ribbon style (smooth on the left, overtones and ripples toward the right as the morph sweeps, no centre line; they run on unbroken behind the text, which is drawn on top: no box, gap or halo, the background is the theme's everywhere off the lines), coloured from the selected theme's part 1 colour (top line) to its part 2 colour (violet to pink in VIOLET). HI-CON: flat red lines above, flat blue below, no blend. MENU > ANIM OFF: the first frame stays still. While the first-start guide has not been seen (section 6.2, "First-start guide") the splash stays 0.5 s longer (`GUIDE_SPLASH_MS`, 1.7 s in all) and the guide opens as it ends (by its time or by the key that ends it).
- **Default theme:** VIOLET (`UI_DEFAULT_INDEX` 0, the first palette; a unit with no valid settings starts in it; saved settings keep their theme).
- **ABOUT** (MENU > SYSTEM > ABOUT): the **first view is the splash animation**, drawn by the splash's own code (`ui_draw.c splash_frame`): the thin morphing lines in the theme's part 1 to part 2 colours with the lockup on top in the text colour, animating continuously (a step every 66 ms, no timeout, nothing ends it; MENU > ANIM OFF freezes the first frame, HI-CON keeps its flat red and blue lines; it is redrawn only while the page is shown). The footer hints: `K1 SCROLL, SELECT SECTION, OCT- BACK` (0.2.9, the owner: "K1 should scroll down on the ABOUT page, not the SELECT. SELECT goes left and right"; before, SELECT scrolled and any knob turned left went back). **KNOB 1** scrolls (right down, left up, 18 px a detent, bounded; at the top it stays in ABOUT) on to the information over the plain themed background: the title block, the version, the build date, the CPU line, the USB line, `based on Felucca (GPL-3.0)`, the Felucca copyright and links, then CREDITS, then LICENSES + SOURCE; scrolling back to the top returns to the animation. **SELECT** jumps a section a detent: right the animation -> the information -> CREDITS -> LICENSES + SOURCE (then it stays), left back the same way (no wrap). OCT- (lit) goes back to the tabs, HOME closes the menu; KNOB 2..4, PRESETS and ALGORITHM do nothing here. The scroll bar at the right shows the place. The power-on splash itself is unchanged (1.2 s).
- **Version name:** `FELUCCA_VERSION` is the VERSION file as `DUELL <VERSION>` (`DUELL 1.0`; a 0.x.y test beta adds ` beta`) (splash-free places: ABOUT, INFO, console, editor info). `tools/build.py --release 0.1-beta` passes `DUELL 0.1 beta` and writes `duell-0.1-beta.fwsc` (identity still `FM-1_901`, the loader marker unchanged); the installer page loads `duell-0.1-beta.fwsc` and still accepts the old `felucca-0.1-beta.fwsc`.

- **MENU > SYSTEM > TEST (the CPU test screen, 0.2.2; it was GLO's last page, and before that the INFO item, whose editor-protocol menu id it keeps; the header names it TEST, top middle; keys play notes).** It is drawn by the page code with a hidden page (`params.c TEST_PAGE`, on no button and in no family's round) while `ui.menu` is 3. OCT+ or K1 turned right opens it from the item; **OCT- goes back to the tabs, HOME closes the menu** (K1 is MORPH there, both ways, so K1 left cannot be the way back), and any page button leaves the menu for its page (below). The panel shows the loaded **MULTI** (`Mnn NAME`, or NO MULTI when the music did not come from one), for **part 1 and part 2 the ENGINE name and the PATCH name** (the stored patch's name or the factory sound's), then the CPU meter exactly as described above, moved from INFO: AVERAGE 1 S with the bar, the 80 % mark and the peak tick, PEAK HOLD, MISSED (since the last RESET), the state word OK / NEAR THE LIMIT / AT THE LIMIT / BLOCKS MISSED JUST NOW, the same flat colours as before (HI-CON text colour at OK). Knobs, each doing what its label says: **K1 MORPH** and **K2 ROLL** are the MORPH page's (the same code: the morph fader of the part(s) named by MORPH TARGET; ROLL A<>B with the twist-past-the-detent gesture and its rest rule), **K3 LOAD** (card label LOAD; the multi's number below it) steps through the used multis M01..M16 (empty slots skipped, clamped at both ends, several detents in one turn load only the one it stops on) and **loads the one it stops on at once**, as the MULTIS page's LOAD, with the message `LOADED Mnn NAME` (no dialog; playing goes on), **K4 RESET** (any turn) clears the peak hold and the missed count of this page and restarts the average window (`RESET`); ABOUT keeps its since-power-on count. Host tests: `tests/multi_test.c` (group 3b), `tests/select_test.c` (page order).
- **The four CPU TEST multis M13..M16** (factory multis in ROM by the same mechanism as M01..M10: an empty slot shows and loads them, SAVE over one is an overwrite, ERASE restores; M11, M12 stay empty for users; ten factory multis since 0.2.11). Records in `felucca-base/tools/factory_multis/M13.bin..M16.bin`, built by `felucca-base/tests/gen_test_multis.c` with the firmware's own code (sound loader, parameters, multi.c's packer) and checked by `tools/gen_duell_multis.py` (names, the intended sounds, the output MODE). All four: both parts' arp UP at 1/16, CHRD MAJ7 (a 4-note chord from one key), VOICE POLY, REL about 500 ms (511 ms), tempo 138, chorus + delay + reverb sends, an INSERT on each part, EQ (LOW +3, MID +2, HIGH -2), the PUMP ducker (depth 40), TEMPO 138. **M13 TEST 1 BASE**: #87 JAZZ CLUB (WHEEL) + #45 SILK CHORUS (ANALOG), LAYER, HALL, PHASER / CHORUS inserts (the 58 % baseline). **M14 TEST 2 PHASE**: #104 CRYSTAL CAVE (PHASE) + #45 SILK CHORUS (ANALOG), the same (the 73 % / 27 missed blocks case). **M15 TEST 3 HEAVY**: #83 TINE ROOM (FM6) + #104 CRYSTAL CAVE (PHASE), DUEL, SHIM reverb, PING-PONG on, FLANGER inserts, sends 50. **M16 TEST 4 MIX**: #57 SEA ORGAN (NOISE) + #49 WOW FLUTTER (LOFI), LAYER, sends 60 (PHYS and GRAIN have only 3 voices per part, so they cannot fill the 8 voices and were not used). Numbers are PATCHES-list numbers. Verified in the host sim: a held key reaches 8 busy voices in each (TEST 3 in DUEL: key on part 1 plus MIDI channel 2 for part 2, since DUEL's keys play the selected part only). BASS+ is the device-wide SPEAKER EQ setting (MENU > AUDIO), not stored in a multi, so the multis cannot carry it: testers set it by hand.

### 6.2 MENU audit, MIDI in one place, first start, read-only screens (owner's feedback after the first flash, 2026-10-12)
- **MENU (DUELL 0.2.14, the owner's MENU rework, 2026-10-14).** Four tabs; the rows in this order (the device's names; the editor gets the same names, `MI_NAME`, at most 12 characters):
  - **DISPLAY:** COLOR, LEDS, SCALE LEDS (AUTO / OFF; moved here from CONTROL), ANIMATION (OFF / ON / IDLE; was ANIM), SCREEN OFF, CPU METER.
  - **CONTROL:** MULTI LOAD (KEEPS MODE (default) / SETS MODE; was MODE RECALL OFF / ON: whether a multi load also sets the MODE page saved in it, MODE, BALANCE, XFADE, SPLIT, see 5b.4), HOME OPENS (MORPH (default) / MODE / MIXER), KNOB ACCEL (OFF / ON, **default ON**), BUTTON HOLD (0.3 .. 0.6 s, default 0.4 s; was HOLD: how long a page button is held for its QUICK layer), STEP KEYS (WRITE (default) / ADD; was CHORD ENTRY HOLD / ADD: step entry on the SEQ page), STEP PREVIEW, **PERF LEAVE** (FADE (default) / KEEP; 0.2.14: whether leaving PERFORM and loading a sound fade PERFORM's SPACE and KILL back to neutral or leave them; section 5e).
  - **AUDIO** (BASS MONO is not here: it is a sound setting, the page GLO > BASS MONO, section 5h; **OUTPUT EQ** (FLAT / LOWCUT / BASS+, the low cut and the speaker bass) **was removed in 0.2.19**, editor id 9 retired; **USB LEVEL (MASTER / FIXED, editor id 10) was removed in 0.2.25**: USB audio is always fixed): CLICK (OFF / REC / ON, **default REC**: the click sounds while the transport runs and a part is armed for recording, which is only possible on the sequencer pages; ON always while running), CLICK LEVEL, COUNT-IN, TUNE (**MASTER TUNE, DUELL 0.2.22**, owner, 2026-10-16: the frequency of A4 in Hz as on a Roland keyboard, **415.3 to 466.2 Hz in 0.1 Hz steps (510), default 440.0 Hz**, shown "440.0 Hz"; up to 0.2.21 it was -50 to +50 cents. It is still the project's value (global G_TUNE, a 16-bit slot of the project, the autosave and a multi's record is not touched by a multi load) and a MENU row; the stored value is the frequency in tenths of a Hz (4153 to 4662, 4400 = 440.0), and a stored value outside that range, an older project's cents, reads as 440.0 (not the nearest end). The pitch offset 1200 log2(f / 440) is worked out once when the value changes (voice.c tune_get, integers only) in the form the voice already adds: at 440.0 it is exactly 0, the sound of TUNE 0 before. The offset is on the voice's 1/4096 grid, so it is within 0.21 cent of the exact one (a 0.1 Hz step is 0.39 cent at A4); every engine gets it through the voice's pitch and fine factor as before. KNOB ACCEL ON scales a fast turn of this row (range 510: x2 to x8, a full sweep in about 64 quick detents); OCT+ / OCT- one step of 0.1 Hz).
  - **SYSTEM:** RESTORE LAST (name kept, owner), BUTTON MAP (was CALIBRATION: Felucca's teach-in of the panel's buttons and encoders; OCT+ opens it, a knob does not; the OCT- + OCT+ combo at power-on opens the same screen), USB SERIAL (OFF / ON, **default OFF**; a two-line row, its hint DEVELOPER CONSOLE; **since memory trim 1 in developer builds only: the product has no serial console and no such row**), TEST (developer builds only), GUIDE, ABOUT, ERASE DATA.
  - **Removed:** SCOPE (OUT / MIX: the scope always draws OUT, after MASTER, as the default did; editor id 18 retired, a stored MIX bit ignored) and OVERVIEW (SCOPE / PARTS, with the OVERVIEW page of the HOME round; editor id 23 retired, its byte [15][26] kept as stored). HOME OPENS lost its value OVERVIEW: a stored 3 reads as MORPH. Ids are never reused.
  - **Layout:** a row is one unit (21 px, a 1 px rule) or two (a two-line label: the second line small, S in MID: USB SERIAL's hint); 7 units a page (`menu_items.c MPAGE_UNITS`, `menu_place`); a tab with more goes on to a page 2 and its pill reads `SYSTEM 1/2`. SELECT walks every row across the tabs and pages (moving onto a row of the other page draws that page); ALGORITHM and PRESETS do nothing, any knob or OCT+/- changes the value, as before. In the release every tab is one page (SYSTEM 7 units); a developer build's TEST row makes SYSTEM two pages (ERASE DATA on page 2). `tests/ui_test.c test_menu_tabs`: the rows in the owner's order, every page <= 7 units, every label (both lines) and its widest value fit the 208 px between the label's pen and the value's right edge with 8 px to spare.
  - **New defaults and their migration (settings_persist.c `settings_defaults`).** KNOB ACCEL ON, CLICK REC, USB SERIAL OFF. In each the old default is the bit value 0 ([15][30] bit 2 clear = ACCEL OFF, [15][28] bits 0-1 = 0 CLICK OFF, [15][30] bit 6 clear = USB SERIAL ON), so a value the user chose cannot be told from the untouched default. The change is made **once**, while the flag **[15][24] bit 7** (`ui.c PREF_DEFAULTS`) is clear: ACCEL set ON, CLICK OFF set to REC (REC and ON kept), USB SERIAL set OFF, [15][10] bits 1-7 cleared (room for the next settings), the flag set. `settings_import` does it on every record it accepts (an older record, an editor backup of one, a PER3 record); `project.c persist_boot` does it on a unit with no valid record (a new unit, ERASE DATA) and without a flash part, before `usb_serial_apply`, so a new unit enumerates without the console from the start. No write at boot: the changed record is the saved copy in RAM (`persist_saved`) and reaches the flash with the next settings save; until then each start does the same change again. After it the user's choice sticks. Downgrade to 0.2.13: the three bits read as they are, the flag is ignored. Tests: `persistence_test` (new unit, old record, the user's choice, PER3, no flash), `erase_test`, `editor_test` (the new defaults in MENU_DESC), `ui_test`.
  - Up to 0.2.13 (the audit of 2026-10-12): (4 tabs; the MIDI tab is gone): DISPLAY: COLOR, ANIM, LEDS, SCREEN OFF, SCOPE, **OVERVIEW** (was HOME: what HOME's last page shows under its cards, SCOPE or PARTS; Felucca's LEVELS, four tracks' levels, did not exist with two parts and is gone; a stored 2 reads as PARTS), **CPU METER** (0.2.9: ON (default) or OFF, the CPU bar under the header's battery; editor id 26; settings byte [15][24] bit 4, set = OFF, so every older setting reads ON). CONTROL: HOLD, KNOB ACCEL, SCALE LEDS, STEP PREVIEW, CHORD ENTRY, **HOME OPENS**, **MODE RECALL** (0.2.11: OFF (default) or ON; whether a multi load also sets the output MODE saved in it, see 5b.4; editor id 27; settings byte [15][24] bit 6, clear = OFF), (SCALE LINK, a row of 0.2.1 builds before the global scale, is removed: see Arp, chord keys and scale) (new: the page HOME opens from another page, at power-on and when the menu closes: MORPH (default), MODE, MIXER or OVERVIEW; HOME tapped on HOME's own pages still goes round MORPH, MORPH SET, MODE, MIXER, OVERVIEW; settings byte [15][2], 0 = MORPH). AUDIO: SPEAKER EQ, USB LEVEL, CLICK, CLICK LEVEL, COUNT-IN, TUNE. SYSTEM: USB SERIAL, RESTORE LAST (see Startup below), CALIBRATION, **TEST** (developer builds only, `DUELL_TEST_MODE`; was INFO; the CPU test screen, section 6.1; its editor menu id is the old INFO's and stays reserved), **GUIDE** (0.2.11: the first-start guide's pages again, see "First-start guide" below; a row with no value like ABOUT, no editor id), ABOUT (the version, the build date, the **USB link** (OFF WAIT BUS ENUM MIDI) and the CPU line: what INFO's FIRMWARE / USB / CPU rows showed), **ERASE DATA** (0.2.2, see "Erase data" below). Gone: MIDI IN and LEARN CLEAR (now GLO > MIDI K3 and GLO > MIDI LEARN K2). Kept on purpose: STEP PREVIEW and CHORD ENTRY (the STEP page and its recording are still Felucca's), CLICK / COUNT-IN (REC still arms and records), TUNE (the project's value; not in a MULTI), USB SERIAL (macOS audio needs it OFF).
- **MIDI in one place.** Every MIDI setting is on a GLO page: MIDI (K1 P1 CH, K2 P2 CH, K3 MIDI IN OWN CH / SELECTED; with where each part is heard, a line saying what MIDI IN does and the clock line), SIDECHAIN (0.2.22: K1 P1 CH, K2 P1 NOTE, K3 P2 CH, K4 P2 NOTE: the ducker's MIDI setup, a device setting), MIDI LEARN (K1 LEARN, K2 CLEAR); the tempo and the clock source are on the TEMPO page (K1 TEMPO, K2 TAP, K3 SWG, K4 CLK). Nothing else in the firmware is a MIDI setting (the keys always send on the selected part's channel; USB and TRS share the channel state; there is no THRU, CC-send or transport switch). Editor protocol: MENU item id 21 (MIDI IN) is retired (never reused), id 25 HOME OPENS is new, id 23 now names OVERVIEW with two values (SCOPE, PARTS); stored formats unchanged.
- **First start** (a unit with no valid settings: new, stock firmware's data or garbage in the settings sector, a flash erase; `project.c persist_boot` sees `settings_import` refuse the record): the theme is VIOLET (`UI_DEFAULT_INDEX` 0), MODE is LAYER and factory multi M01 is loaded exactly as SAVE > MULTIS' LOAD does (`ui_input.c first_boot_poll` -> `project_load(0)`, read from the factory multi path: tempo, globals and mode are what M01 holds; the owner's replacement of M01 changes the first start with no code change: since 0.2.13 M01 is REESE SWAMP; the first start applies its MODE LAYER and BALANCE whatever MODE RECALL says). Not done when an autosave was restored. The settings are saved at once, so the next start is ordinary (autosave / saved settings); any valid settings record, old or short, keeps its theme and mode untouched. Host test: `multi_test` group 4.
- **Startup (autosave)**: the unit starts **as you left it**. `MENU > SYSTEM > RESTORE LAST` (editor id 15; ON = default, OFF; settings bit 128, as in Felucca 1.2): ON keeps the music as it is in an autosave while the device is stopped and idle and puts it back at power-on, under the splash (`project.c autosave_boot`); OFF writes none and restores none. Factory multi **M01** (VIOLET, LAYER, M01's parts) is loaded **only on a fresh unit** (no valid settings: `first_boot_pending`, `ui_input.c first_boot_poll`), as before; after a crash or hang, or with no or a bad autosave on a unit with valid settings, the default sounds start, not M01. Host tests: `multi_test` group 4, `persistence_test`.
- **Flash writes wait for silence (DUELL 0.2.11; owner: the first long press of EDIT while playing a multi clicked and the CPU bar went high).** A flash erase stops the audio for ~40 ms. `project.c settings_poll` (the queued settings save, and with it the pattern slots' `ps_poll` which already used it) now waits on `autosave_quiet()` (transport stopped, no key or button down, no voice active, release tails included), as the autosave does; before it waited for the transport only. Cause: `ui_layer.c layer_opened` sets the one-time "layer seen" bit (`favorites.factory[15][31]`, all layers) and calls `settings_save`, which wrote at once whenever the transport was stopped, also with keys held or voices sounding. The bit is set in RAM at once and goes to flash with the next quiet poll. Host test: `persistence_test` (voice, key, button held: queued, no erase; silent: written, bit saved).
- **First-start guide (DUELL 0.2.11, owner-approved plan `docs/GUIDE_PLAN.md`).** A short guide of six full-screen pages, shown once after the power-on splash.
  - **When:** while the settings bit "guide seen" is clear: **bit 5 of `favorites.factory[15][24]`** (`ui.c PREF_GUIDE_SEEN`; the byte of MENU's 1.2 flags, `ui_prefs2`; bits 0-2 and 4 are MENU rows, bit 3 the retired HELP; bit 5 was free and never written by an earlier build). So it is clear on a new unit, after ERASE DATA or any settings record that is not valid, and in **every older settings record**: an update from 0.2.10 or earlier shows the guide once too. The settings format, its size and every other bit are unchanged (an old record reads as "not seen"); no editor or protocol id. On that start the splash stays 0.5 s longer, then the guide opens. **Leaving it** (HOME, a page button, or OCT+ on the last page) sets the bit and writes the settings once (`settings_save`; the write waits for silence, as every settings write since 0.2.11: see "Flash writes wait for silence"). A power-off with the guide still up leaves the bit clear: the next start shows it again. A fresh unit's start (M01, section "First start") happens under the splash as before; its "LOADED" is not shown over the guide.
  - **Screen:** the header as on every page (part badges, battery, CPU bar) with the title **GUIDE n/6** in the middle (a message such as HOLD ON or STOP ALL replaces it for its moment); under it the page: headings in L in the theme colour, lines in M in the text colour, at most 220 px wide (no line is cut or ellipsised), top-aligned; at the bottom the key hints `[SELECT] PAGE` and `[HOME] CLOSE` (`[HOME] DONE` on the last page). It is drawn over whatever page is under it (the MENU too) and only when the page changes. Code: `ui_guide.c` (`GUIDE_TXT`, `guide_input`, `guide_draw`), `ui_draw.c` (splash, header title), `ui_input.c` (`play_press`, the hook after the HOME / REC timers).
