  - **Keys:** SELECT right / OCT+ = the next page, SELECT left / OCT- = the previous one; SELECT stops at both ends (a fast turn cannot close it), OCT+ on the last page finishes. **HOME** leaves at once (used up: no HOME tap, no menu; from the MENU's GUIDE it closes the menu too, as HOME does there). **EDIT, FX, ENV, LFO, ARP, SCL, GLO, SEQ and SAVE** leave at once and then do their own job in the same press (a tap opens the page, a hold is its layer, PERFORM, or SAVE OVER), as they leave the MENU. While the guide is up the synth plays as on HOME: the keys and MIDI play, PLAY/STOP plays and stops (twice: STOP ALL), REC is the HOLD switch (the page under it is HOME's); KNOB 1-4, PRESETS and ALGORITHM do nothing (no parameter, sound, part or morph moves; the header says `[HOME] CLOSES GUIDE` for a moment, so the turn is not silently ignored), and OCT-/OCT+ never move the octave there.
  - **MENU > SYSTEM > GUIDE** (above ABOUT; SELECT the row, KNOB 1 right or OCT+ opens it) shows the same pages over the menu at any time; it never reads or writes the bit. HOME closes the guide and the menu; OCT+ on the last page goes back to the menu at the GUIDE row; a page button leaves both for its page.
  - **The pages** (owner's final text, 2026-10-14; as on the screen; a heading in L, then lines in M; a line marked * has its words up to the first ':' in the theme colour): 1 **MODES**: *LAYER: BOTH PARTS / COMBINED: BALANCE, / VELOCITY CROSSFADE, / KEY SPLIT; *DUEL: DUAL MONO, FOR / EXTERNAL EFFECTS; *STEREO: BOTH PARTS IN / ONE STEREO FIELD. 2 **MORPH A<>B** (the deck's morph slide): GLIDE BETWEEN TWO / VERSIONS OF THE SAME PATCH; A SMART RANDOMIZER / CREATES NEW VARIANTS / FROM YOUR STARTING POINT. 3 **LONG PRESS**: HOLD FX: PERFORM; HOLD FOR QUICK EDIT: / EDIT, LFO, ENV, ARP, GLO. 4 **REC = HOLD**: KEYS KEEP SOUNDING / AFTER YOU LET GO. / PRESS REC AGAIN / TO RELEASE.; NOT ON THE SEQUENCER / PAGES. 5 **RESET**: HOLD OCT- AND TURN / A KNOB TO RESET IT / TO ITS DEFAULT. 6 **STOP + SAVE**: *STOP ALL: PLAY/STOP 2X; *SAVE OVER PATCH: HOLD SAVE. No welcome page (the splash is the welcome). The L face (`tools/gen_aa_font.py L_CHARS`) gained `<`, `=` and `>` for the headings.
  - **Emulator:** the same code; the browser keeps its flash image, so the guide shows on the first visit and again only after the site data is cleared (or MENU > SYSTEM > ERASE DATA). `web/emu/emu_test.mjs` boots on an erased flash, checks the guide is up after the splash, plays a key and a MIDI note under it, turns a page and leaves it with HOME (a settings write); its deterministic heavy song keeps the guide up (PLAY plays through it), the same in the native build, so the bit-for-bit check is unchanged in kind. Export `web_guide()` (the page shown, 0 none).
  - **Tests:** `tests/guide_test.c` (`./host_tests.sh guide`, on simulated NOR: fresh = shown, the longer splash; seen = not shown; an older record = shown; leaving writes once; MENU's replay never touches the bit; every leave button on every page; playing under it; a random soak, no stuck state; every line's width and every page's height), `tests/ui_render.c` (the scenes `guide_1` .. `guide_6`, `guide_message`, `guide_octave`, `menu_guide_row` in every palette, the layout lint: no overlap, nothing cut), `tests/ui_test.c` (the L face holds the headings' glyphs), `tests/splash_test.c` (the plain splash with the bit set), `tests/release_test.c` (SYSTEM's six rows).
  - **Cost:** about 3 KB of flash (the six pages' text, about 560 bytes, and the L face's three new glyphs `<` `=` `>` and the code), seven bytes of RAM (`ui.guide`, `ui.guide_menu`, `splash_guide`, `guide_sig`); nothing in the audio ISR.
- **Erase data (DUELL 0.2.2; owner request: start from scratch).** A reinstall of DUELL, even via the official firmware, keeps the saved data (it lives in its own flash area), so the unit comes back as it was left. `MENU > SYSTEM > ERASE DATA` (last row of SYSTEM, label 10 characters; OCT+ opens the dialog, a knob does not) asks **ERASE ALL MY DATA?** and says what goes: settings, favourites, autosave, saved patches (every slot holds its factory sound again), multis and patterns; the FM6 voice bank is kept; factory sounds come back; it cannot be undone. OCT+ = YES, OCT- = NO, any page button or HOME cancels, nothing is written. YES is refused while the transport runs (STOP TO ERASE). What is erased (`src/erase_data.c erase_data_run`, one `st_wipe` per storage object, in this order): the autosave (OBJ_AUTOSAVE), the pattern slots (OBJ_PATSLOT0..2, factory patterns P01..P16 back), the multis (OBJ_MULTI0..7, M01..M10 and M13..M16 factory again), the patch store (OBJ_PATCH0..5: every stored patch and every factory sound's shadow copy), and the **settings last** (OBJ_SETTINGS: theme, scale, MIDI channels and learn map, favourites, preferences and the panel table). Not erased: the FM6 voice bank and the firmware (and the unused flash of the retired user sample slots, 0.2.9). Power cuts: each object is erased copy by copy, the copy that is not current first, so after a cut each object is whole as it was or empty (never an older copy); the settings go last, so a cut leaves a unit that still boots as it was, with less data (run ERASE DATA again), and only the finished erase gives the first-start path. After the erase the unit restarts after 0.7 s (the message ERASED, RESTARTING; `main.c` resets the chip, the browser emulator reloads the page and keeps its localStorage flash image, which now holds the erased state): VIOLET, LAYER and factory multi M01, as any fresh unit (see Startup). Blank sectors are not erased again. Editor op **80 ERASE_ALL** (`editor_erase.c`; `web/EDITOR_PROTOCOL.md` "ERASE ALL"): payload exactly the nine ASCII bytes `ERASE ALL`; reply rc 0 done (restart follows), 1 wrong token or length (nothing touched), 3 transport running, 4 flash failed. The installer's optional box "Erase my saved data after installing" (INSTALL card, off by default) sends it to the restarted unit; see docs/INSTALL.md. Host tests: `tests/erase_test.c` (`./host_tests.sh erase`), `web/test_installer_state.mjs`.
- **Mute at power-on**: MUTE (GLO MUTE 1 / MUTE 2, MIXER K4) stays a part setting kept in multis and the autosave, so editing and a multi LOAD keep it (the GLO page shows MUTED; a LOAD is the user's action). **Power-on never starts muted**: `project.c boot_unmute` clears every part's MUTE after a restored autosave (the factory M01 of a fresh unit holds none). Nothing silent or hidden at start. A muted part 1 in the autosave was one possible cause of "only the right side" in the owner's report (part 1 is the left channel in DUEL); it is cleared now.
- **Speaker and the outputs**: the firmware has ONE stereo I2S stream (`audio.c`, `fx.c master_out`) into the codec; headphone, line out and the built-in speaker are all fed from it by the hardware, USB audio takes the same master (`uac_tap`). Since 0.2.25 USB audio is always at a fixed level (MENU > AUDIO > USB LEVEL is removed; it was MASTER / FIXED since 0.2.5): `mix_block` feeds `master_out` (limiter, soft clip) at `MASTER_USB_REF` (2048, the default `master_q12`), so USB carries what the default knob gives, whatever MASTER says; `audio.c master_dac` then scales the DAC feed by `master_q12 / 2048` (the gain glides, a one-pole step every sample, 5.8 ms; the result keeps 7 more bits than Q15), saturating at the old ceiling (never wrapping) above the default knob. The codec has no volume register the firmware writes: this is the only volume. There is no separate speaker feed in the firmware and no jack detect, so the speaker cannot get its own mono sum without also folding the headphones. DUEL therefore stays hard-panned on every output (part 1 left only, part 2 right only), and a speaker that plays only the left channel is silent when only part 2 sounds. Not changed in 0.2.2: a fresh unit opens in LAYER (both parts in both channels); a unit restored as left keeps its saved mode. **To confirm on the unit** (owner): with no headphones in DUEL, part 1 alone then part 2 alone: which is heard.
- **Screens without a value (TEST, ABOUT).** SELECT picks the row; KNOB 1 turned right opens the screen (any knob does, as on every menu row; OCT+ still does); OCT- closes it (in ABOUT KNOB 1 scrolls, 0.2.9; on TEST K1 is MORPH), HOME closes the menu.
- **Removed in 0.2.2 (owner, 2026-10-12): STYLE, LARGE and HELP.** FLAT is the only look: the LINE style (black areas divided by 1 px rules) is gone together with its MENU > DISPLAY > STYLE item, its drawing branches and its render scenes; a stored STYLE byte (LINE, the old PIXEL, anything) is ignored. The LARGE view (tall knob cards, MENU > DISPLAY > LARGE) is gone with its layout code and scenes; a stored LARGE bit is ignored. HELP (MENU > SYSTEM > HELP, the per-page hints shown for ~2 s when a page opens) is gone for now and may come back if it proves useful; the small hints the pages draw themselves (HOLD SAVE = SAVE PATCH on MORPH, HOLD SAVE = SAVE PATTERN / SAVE MULTI, the key hints under cards) stay. The editor menu ids 1, 2 and 24 are retired and never reused (web/EDITOR_PROTOCOL.md); settings records stay compatible (the bits and the byte are simply not read).
- **MENU settings are saved when they change (0.2.2).** A setting changed in the MENU is written like the MORPH AMOUNT knob's: a two-value switch at once, a row with more values (COLOR, ANIM, LEDS, SCREEN OFF, HOLD, ...) once the knob has rested about 2 s (the write waits for silence: transport stopped, no key held, no voice sounding, as for every settings write since 0.2.11); leaving the menu in any way writes whatever is still pending. Leaving the menu shows no message. TUNE is the project's value and is kept by the autosave, not here.
- **Page buttons leave the menu (0.2.2).** While the MENU is open (any tab, ABOUT, TEST, ERASE DATA's dialog) a press of EDIT, FX, ENV, LFO, ARP, SEQ, SCL, GLO or SAVE closes the menu (a changed setting is saved as HOME does; the ERASE DATA dialog is cancelled, nothing erased, nothing else written) and the button goes on to its page like on any page, in one press (`ui_input.c`, the machinery of the dialogs' and NAME's page buttons). HOME closes it as before. OCT+ / OCT- / SELECT / knobs / keys keep their menu meanings. CALIBRATION runs its own loop and is left only by its own ways out. The hint row says it (SELECT ROW, K1 OPEN; ABOUT: SELECT SCROLL, K1 BACK). CALIBRATION is a setup, not read-only: OCT+ only.

### 6.3 CPU and audio safeguards (DUELL 0.2.11, owner's decisions on docs/CPU_ANALYSIS.md, 2026-10-14)

- **Flash writes: a soft duck, no waiting (owner: SAVE never waits for silence and never stops the notes).** A flash erase keeps the interrupts off for its whole time (~40 ms, ESTIMATE; the driver measures it), and the DMA loops the audio buffer meanwhile, so the buffer must hold silence. Before every erase (`storage_hw.c st_erase`) and every program burst of more than one page, and once over a whole record write (`storage.c st_save_to`: erase, payload, commit record), the main loop asks the audio ISR for a duck (`audio.c audio_duck`): the DAC output fades to 0 over one half (2.9 ms, linear), the ISR renders on in silence (voices, envelopes and effects run as ever) and counts two whole silent halves, so both halves of the buffer are 0; then the erase runs, and `audio_unduck` fades the output back in over the next half. What sounded keeps sounding after. The wait is ~3 halves (~9 ms) before the erase; at most 20 ms (the audio not running yet, e.g. at boot, goes on at once, as before). The USB audio feed is not ducked (it stalls with the interrupts as before). Single-page programs (256 bytes, ~0.5 ms each) are not ducked. Patch SAVE, multi SAVE, the editor's backup restore and FM6 bank writes keep their existing dialogs and their transport check (STOP TO SAVE, the editor stops the sequencer: the erase also stalls the sequencer's clock); nothing waits for the notes to end. The one-time settings writes (`settings_poll`) keep their own rule (wait for quiet: "Flash writes wait for silence", section 6.2). The driver's measured times go to `felucca_dbg` (`erase_max_us`, `erase_last_us`, `prog_max_us`, `erases`, since boot; `fm1t memr`), and the TEST screen (DUELL_TEST_MODE only) shows **ERASE nn MS**, the longest erase since boot (`--`: none yet), on its last row (the meter's rows moved up a few pixels for it). Host test: `tests/audio_test.c` flash duck (the fade half under a linear ramp, both buffer halves 0 before the erase, the notes still sounding, the level back within a half).
- **The LEDs go dark during a flash erase (0.2.14, from Felucca 1.6, #236).** The erase also stops the LED scan (the scan tick is an interrupt), so the column lit at that moment used to stay on at full duty for the whole erase while the others went dark: a bright flash of the keys and buttons at every save. Now `st_erase` switches all LED rows off (`fm1__led_lines(0)`) after the interrupts are off and before the erase; the scan tick lights them again at its next pass when the interrupts come back. The order: the audio ducks, the LEDs go off, the erase runs, the LEDs come back, the audio fades in. Program bursts (256-byte pieces, ~0.5 ms each) are unchanged. Host test: `tests/flash_led_test.c` (`./host_tests.sh flashled`).
- **MIDI-in drain: 16 events a block.** The audio ISR used to take the whole 64-entry MIDI-in ring in one block (a DAW chord dump or a flood: every note-on starts or steals a voice in that block, +25 % on the half, PHYS 121 %). `seq.c events_block` now takes at most **16 events per block** (`MIDI_DRAIN`, 0.73 ms), the rest in the next blocks, in their order. Two exceptions, so nothing that must be on time waits: **real-time bytes** (clock 0xF8, Start, Continue, Stop) behind the limit are taken in the same block, in their own order, and marked taken (the next block skips them; never twice); an **All Notes Off / All Sound Off** (CC 123 / 120) behind the limit pulls the drain up to it, so it ends exactly the notes before it in the same block (never ahead of an earlier note-on, never a block late). An overflowing ring still panics as before; USB input already waits for room (EP1). Host test: `tests/midi_control_test.c` drain_test, usb_burst_test. **Measured** (`tests/cpu_scenarios_test.c` F, 64 note events at once on 2 held notes, 24 dB HP): 16 a block spreads them over the 4 blocks of **one** half, so that half is as heavy as before (ANALOG 113 %, WHEEL 116 %, PHYS 138 %); the limit bounds a block, not the half. 8 a block (`-DMIDI_DRAIN=8u`) gives 84 / 87 / 107 %, 4 a block 63 / 65 / 90 % (a 64-event flood then takes 12 ms instead of 3). An owner decision (open).
- **Overload shedding: a missed half sheds at once.** The 85 % rule is unchanged (two halves in a row above 85 %: one voice a half, released voices first, then the youngest held note that is not a part's bass or lead). New: a **single half over 100 %** (it missed its deadline) sheds at once, without waiting for a second half: the **2 oldest released voices** (their tails) fade over the next block (`audio.c shed_released`, `SHED_FAST`). Held notes are only cut by the 85 % rule. Effects are not skipped (a skipped block of the buses would be a hard dropout, worse than the miss). Host test: `tests/audio_test.c` dma.
- **Overload guard (DUELL 0.2.25; automatic, always on, nothing stored).** When the audio is near its limit, by the 85 % rule above (two halves in a row over 85 % of their time), the delay's **RANDOM / OCT UP** runs as the plain delay it cross-fades from, through its own ~11.6 ms crossfade (the delay's modulation is not run in its place; the stored MODE is not touched). It lets go after the meter's load (the CPU bar's figure, `song.cpu_q8`) has stayed **under 70 % for about 3 s**, and fades back the same way; between 70 and 85 % it stays as it is (hysteresis). Below the threshold nothing changes, bit for bit. It runs alongside the voice shedding (which still cuts tails at the same moment). Shown on **ABOUT**: **LIGHT FX** at the right of the USB line while engaged; the console status prints `fx_light` and `fx_light_count`. No setting (MODULES has no switch for it). Saves, in the host model, about 5.5 points of M16 STRESS TEST in LAYER and 8.8 in DUEL (more on the unit: the event delay's reads cost about 1.5 x their host count there). Built also for SHIM's PITCH BOTH, which the owner then removed in the same release. Code: `fx.c ovl_step` (called from `audio.c shed_check`), `fx_buses`, `split_buses`; flash only, the fast RAM block unchanged. Host tests: `tests/ovl_test.c` (`./host_tests.sh ovlguard`: the measure forced through the real ISR, hysteresis, the fades, no click, built a second time with `-DOVL_NOGUARD` and equal render hashes), `tests/cpu_scenarios_test.c` V.
- **SMART morph wakes off stages with half the chance** (rule 3g, `morph_wake_half`). The biggest everyday load: End B used to switch the multimode filter on for every voice (+17 % at 4 + 4 voices for CUT 82 to 78 alone) and SAT, the sends, SPREAD and the LFO depths from 0. Measured (host model, % of the half's budget, 4 + 4 notes held, fader at 64, `tests/cpu_scenarios_test.c`): factory multis M01 79.8 -> 69.6, M02 91.6 -> 74.5, M03 76.7 -> 75.7, M04 36.0 -> 35.9, M14 93.9 -> 86.5, M15 114.4 -> 107.0 (8 notes on part 1: M01 49.8 -> 41.2, M02 94.9 -> 77.8, M03 80.9 -> 78.4, M04 27.0 -> 26.9, M14 96.7 -> 83.6, M15 89.3 -> 89.1). Over 8 engine pairs (ANALOG + PHASE, FM6 + WAVE, WHEEL + TRIO, PHYS + NOISE, sounds #0 and #1), fader 64: factory End B 78.6 -> 67.8 % on average, SMART AMOUNT 15 / 40 / 100 rolls 83.4 / 84.8 / 84.5 -> 78.9 / 74.3 / 77.6 %, WILD unchanged. The figures depend on each seed's coins: a multi whose coins fall on heads costs what it did (M03, M04).
- **VOICE CAP for heavy combinations** (owner, 2026-10-14; `voice.c vcap`, `vcap_poll`). The 8-voice pool stays; each part gets a cap (never below 4; an engine's own cap under 4, PHYS 3, stays) so that the **predicted** worst case of both parts stays under **85 %** of the half (the model scale of docs/CPU_ANALYSIS.md; the device reads ~10-25 % lower). A voice's cost (0.1 % of the half, `VCOST`): the engine's (its heaviest factory sound over the voices it holds, measured: ANALOG 5.1, FM6 6.1, PHASE 4.0, LOFI 2.4, VOICE 6.0, TRIO 4.4, WHEEL 5.9, PHYS 14.4, NOISE 4.7, WAVE 5.2 %), + the multimode filter (12 dB 1.8 %, 24 dB 3.6 %; bypassed 0), and with the part's MORPH on: + 0.5 %, the filter as the heaviest of the sound and both ends (12 / 24 dB that differ: 24), + 2 % when an engine choice is cross-faded. Idle mix 15 %. The worst case: the dearer part holds all the voices its cap allows, the other the rest of the 8. If that is over 85 %, the dearer part's cap goes down one at a time (the voices move to the cheaper part: the pool stays 8) until it fits; if even 4 cannot make the pair fit, each part instead gets the cap that fits it **alone** (85 % - 15 % over its voice, at least 4). LAYER (every note on both parts, 4 + 4): no cap (a cap of 4 or more changes nothing). A muted part costs 0. Computed in the main loop every frame (so a sound or multi load, an engine, filter, SLOPE or MODE change and a roll are seen at once), deterministic. It bites only on **new** notes: `voice_alloc` and UNISON take at most the cap (a note over it steals within the part, as at an engine's own cap); notes already sounding beyond it are never cut and end as usual. Shown **only on the TEST screen** (`VOICES p1 / p2`, the engine's own cap included; DUELL_TEST_MODE builds). Measured (`tests/cpu_scenarios_test.c` C, fader 64, the worst split held): #13 CARTRIDGE (LOFI, 24 dB HP) + #70 WIRE HARP (PHASE): caps 8 / 5, worst 104.8 -> 94.1 %; #60 DIGITAL PAD (WAVE, 24 dB HP) + #5 SQUELCH (STEREO): caps 7 / 7 (the pair cannot fit: each alone), DIGITAL PAD alone 87.7 -> 77.5 %; #117 GLASS SHARDS (ANALOG, 24 dB HP) + #53 CHIP CHAPEL: 7 / 8, alone 94.7 -> 86.1 %; #47 GLASS HALL (FM6) + #60 DIGITAL PAD, 24 dB both: 6 / 7 (the pair 103 % either way); #109 LESLIE STORM (WHEEL, 24 dB HP) + #37 PAN WHISTLE (PHYS): 5 / 3. Factory multis as loaded: no cap below the engines' own (M01 VOICE 4, M15 FM6 6). **Limits:** the model has no effects (sends, reverb type, inserts, EQ, ducker, PERFORM) and reads the heaviest sound of each engine, so it under-reads FX-heavy scenes (M14 / M15 4 + 4 at fader 63: model 51 / 60 %, measured 87 / 107 %) and over-reads light sounds of a heavy engine; and two dear parts together cannot be helped by caps of 4 or more.

---

## Appendix A: ARP TARGET pitch units per engine
Not used here: ALGORITHM selects the part. FM6's algorithm 1-32 stays an EDIT parameter and is excluded from the morph (it cannot be faded).

DRUM is removed from DUELL (its row is dropped); so are SAMPLE, GRAIN and SLICE (retired in 0.2.9: their rows are dropped). Read from `felucca-base/firmware/src` (2026-10-10). **Common path**: `voice.c:track_render` builds one pitch per voice (`v->pitch_cur` with glide, plus TUNE, bend, LFO/ENV pitch) into `vmod_t.pitch16` (1/16 semitone), `.inc` (`pitch_inc`) and `.fine`; `mod.c:mod_voice` then adds the matrix PITCH route and re-derives all three; then `engine->render(t, v, out, n, &m)`. ARP TARGET adds a second set (arp pitch16/inc/fine) built at that same point; each engine picks per unit which set it uses. "Root" = the held key's pitch.

| Engine | Units (mask bits) | Where each unit's pitch is derived from the voice pitch (file:function) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ANALOG | OSC A, OSC B (2 units; **no separate SUB**, see findings) | A: `m->inc` = `inc1` in `eng_analog.c:analog_osc` and `analog_render_x`. B: `cents_inc(m->pitch16, DTN, 0)` = `inc2` in `analog_osc`; in WAVE SUB it is `cents_inc(...) >> 1` (octave below A, `analog_render_x`); in WAVE SYNC it is `pitch_inc(m->pitch16 + DTN*3 + shape)` (`analog_render_x`). |
| FM6 | OP1..OP6 | One value per block: `eng_fm6.c:fm6_render` computes `lf = fm6_note_logfreq(m->pitch16 + (TRNSP-24)*16, m->fine)` and passes it down; `fm6_core.c:fm6_note_compute` then sets per operator `op[k].freq = fm6_freq(logfreq + base[k] + pitch_mod + dt[k])`. Splitting = pass a second `logfreq` and pick it per k. Patch index 0 is OP6 (OP1 = index 5). `base[k]` is fixed at note-on from `v->note` (`fm6_note_init`, `fm6_osc_base`). |
| PHASE | LINE 1, LINE 2, SUB | `eng_phase.c:phase_render`: line 1 `inc = m->inc`; line 2 `inc2 = pitch_inc(m->pitch16 + DTN)` (runs only if DTN != 0 or LINE = RING); SUB is `ph2 += inc >> 1`, so it follows line 1 and needs its own `inc` to be a unit. WAVE and WAVE2 alternate per cycle on each line; they are not units. |
| LOFI | ALL only | `eng_lofi.c:lofi_render`: one oscillator, `pitch = m->pitch16 - sweep + ARPS[E6][step]*16`, then `pitch_inc` (the engine has its own chord arp, ARP E6). |
| VOICE | ALL only (F0 only) | `eng_formant.c:formant_render`: glottal pulse `inc = m->inc`. The formant filters do not follow the pitch except a fixed key tracking (FOLOW 30/127) and an output gain, both read from `v->pitch_cur` directly (not from `m`). |
| TRIO | OSC1, OSC2, OSC3 | `eng_trio.c:trio_render`: `inc1 = m->inc`; `inc2 = trio_inc(m->pitch16, INT2, +DTN, fine)`; `inc3 = trio_inc(m->pitch16, INT3, -DTN, fine)`; `trio_inc` -> `cents_inc`. `fine` is recovered from `m->inc` vs `pitch_inc(m->pitch16)`. Sets with RING (osc1 flipped by osc3's phase) or SYNC (osc2 restarts with osc1) couple units; some sets mute an oscillator (NOIS, RING, SYNC, SYNCP, P+N). |
| WHEEL | SUB (16', 5 1/3'), BODY (8', 4'), TOP (2 2/3'..1'): the SUB/BODY/TOP knob groups of `drw_level`; or 9 drawbars | `eng_wheel.c:wheel_render`: per partial k, `inc = drw_ratio(k, x)` with `x = m->inc` (folded down an octave above ~6 kHz, `DRW_FOLD_LIM`). Each partial has its own phase (`drw_vc_t.ph[k]`), so any split works. The percussion partial rides partial 3 or 4 (`wheel_block`, `T->pk`); the key click is unpitched. |
| PHYS | BODY (main resonator/string), SYMP strings (MODEL SYMP only) | `eng_phys.c:phys_render`: `f0 = m->inc` into `px_modal_block` / `px_memb_block` / `px_string_block`. `phys_symp_pitches`: `m->pitch16 + PHYS_CHORDS[CHRD]` cents -> `pitch_inc` per sympathetic string; CHRD = ROOT uses the track ROOT (does not follow pitch at all). MODAL and MEMB are partial banks of one body (STRC stretches them): ALL only there. |
| NOISE | CLOCK (LFSR and META modes), FILTER (key tracking) | `eng_noise.c:noise_render`: LFSR `inc = noise_inc(m->pitch16 + CLK*16 + drift)`; META the same, or `m->inc` when CLK and drift are 0; filter `cut += (m->pitch16 - 60*16) * TRK`. ANLG and DUST have no pitch except the filter tracking: ALL only there. |
| WAVE (0.2.5) | ALL only | `eng_wave.c:wave_render`: one read at `m->inc` (DTN's second read `cents_inc(m->pitch16, DTN, m->fine)`, SUB `inc >> 1`, SYNC a ratio of it): all follow the one pitch. |

Findings that change the plan in 2.3:
- **ANALOG has two oscillators, not three.** SUB exists only as WAVE SUB, where it *is* OSC B (an octave below A). Mask = A, B. In WAVE SYNC, B on the arp while A holds the root gives a sync sweep.
- **ANALOG OSC B ignores `m->fine`** (`cents_inc(..., 0)`): unison detune, TUNE and bend reach only OSC A today. A per-unit pitch must carry its own `fine`.
- (0.2.9: SLICE is retired.) **SLICE could not take a split pitch**: it cancels the key pitch (the note chooses the slice). If `arp_pitch16` replaced `pitch_cur` inside `m->pitch16`, SLICE's subtraction of `v->pitch_cur` would have to use the same value.
- **VOICE reads `v->pitch_cur` directly** (formant tracking, output gain) and **ANALOG KTR reads `v->pitch16`** (`analog_osc`, the `cut` term), bypassing `m`: they follow the root, never the arp, unless changed on purpose.
- **FM6**: a fixed-frequency operator (`FP_MODE` = 1) ignores pitch, so a mask bit on it does nothing. Keyboard level/rate scaling and `base[k]` come from `v->note` at note-on only. Per-operator pitch is a small change in `fm6_note_compute` (the sum is already per k).
- **Set at note-on** (until 0.2.9): SAMPLE, GRAIN and SLICE picked zone/slice from `v->note` when the voice started (all three retired since).

## Appendix B: randomisable and excluded parameters
Read from `core.h` (enum P_*, G_*), `params.c` (TP[], descriptors, pages) and each engine's `edit[]` and `desc` hook. Tags: **continuous** = random offset, blends; **choice-fadable** = a choice inside an engine / LFO wave / filter type / matrix src+dst that can be cross-faded (3.3); **choice-hard** = cannot be faded, never rolled; **excluded** = never rolled (rule 3). Owner decisions of 2026-10-10 are applied; DRUM is removed from DUELL (its engine table and lane levels P_LN0..7 are gone from these tables) and SLICE stays. A **?** now appears only on an item that is also in the Review list below. A family row (x4, x8, x20) is one row for identical parameters. New DUELL parameters (FILTER ENV, LFO 2, matrix slots 5-8) take the tag of their Felucca twin.

### Review list (open questions) — SETTLED 2026-10-10: all 13 as recommended (see REVIEW_LIST.md)
Each item is a parameter whose class is not settled. Items are numbered; the table rows carry a **?** only for these.

1. **Arp rhythm: P_AGATE, P_ASWING, P_APROB (GATE, SWG, PROB).** Gate = how long each arp note is held; swing = how late every second note is pushed (a shuffle feel); probability = the chance each step actually plays. They change the feel of the pattern, not the tone. Options: excluded, or continuous. Recommendation: excluded, so a morph never changes the groove you set (a morph is about sound; the groove is performance).
2. **ANALOG WAVE (E0).** The oscillator shape: saw, square, triangle, sine, pulse-width, hard-sync, sub-octave. The first five share one code path and can be cross-faded; SYNC and SUB use a different one. Options: fade among the first five only, or fade among all seven, or exclude. Recommendation: fade among the first five, never roll to or from SYNC or SUB.
3. **FM6 macros FB and MEG (E1, E4).** FB is the feedback amount (more = brighter, harsher, noisier tone); it moves in whole steps -7..7. MEG scales all the envelope speeds at once (it changes how fast sounds attack and fade, heard from the next note). The earlier safety analysis of these two is not in this brief and was not available to me. Options: continuous, or excluded in the first build. Recommendation: FB continuous with a limit of 2 steps from the original, MEG continuous but small, both only after you hear them in the emulator; until then excluded.
4. **FM6 own patch, later step (FP_*, needs the `fm6_set_patch` path).** Per-operator envelope rates and levels (how each of the six sine-wave partners swells and fades), per-operator fine frequency (FF) and detune (DET) (tiny pitch offsets between operators), keyboard and rate scaling (how the sound changes up the keyboard), and the voice LFO speed, delay and depth items. Options: continuous with caps, or exclude. Recommendation: later step only; envelope rates/levels and LFO items continuous, FF and DET excluded (they are pitch-like), scaling items excluded (they are switch-like).
5. **PHASE DTN (E4) from zero.** Detune of the second oscillator line. At 0 line 2 is off; any value above 0 turns it on and adds thickness. Options: continuous (can switch line 2 on), or continuous only if the original is not 0. Recommendation: only if the original is not 0 (same spirit as "zero stays zero"), so the morph never adds a second line.
6. **PHASE LINE (E5).** Chooses MIX (two lines added) or RING (two lines multiplied: metallic, bell-like). RING with DTN at 0 squares the signal and sounds wrong. Options: choice-fadable, or excluded. Recommendation: excluded.
7. **LOFI WAVE (E1).** The chip waveform: pulse, triangle, saw, noise, wave-RAM. Noise and wave-RAM carry their own state, so a cross-fade can click or glitch. Options: fade among pulse, triangle, saw only, or exclude. Recommendation: fade among pulse, triangle, saw; never to or from noise or wave-RAM.
8. **LOFI DUTY (E2).** Pulse width of the chip pulse wave (only four true settings), or the wave-table number in WRAM mode. Options: excluded, or continuous in pulse mode only. Recommendation: excluded (four fixed widths do not blend).
9. **LOFI CRSH (E3).** How coarsely the chip sound is stepped (bit-crush feel); in STEP chip mode it is a decay time instead. Options: continuous, or excluded. Recommendation: continuous except in STEP mode, where it is excluded.
10. **VOICE SHIFT (E3).** Moves the vowel colour up or down in semitones without changing the sung pitch (a smaller or bigger mouth). Not a pitch parameter, but it is in semitones. Options: continuous, or excluded. Recommendation: continuous, capped at 3 semitones from the original.
11. **WHEEL ROTR (E7).** Rotary speaker: OFF, SLOW, FAST. The speeds ease by themselves, so a change is smooth already. Options: choice-hard (never rolled), or choice with a normal AMOUNT chance. Recommendation: choice-hard (never rolled), because a speaker that suddenly switches to fast is a gesture, not a variation.
12. **PHYS DAMP and BOW/BEND/BUZZ (E3, E6).** DAMP is how fast the body stops ringing (at the top it rings forever). BOW/BEND/BUZZ is how hard the string is bowed, bent or buzzed, which keeps the sound sustained. Options: continuous with a ceiling, or excluded. Recommendation: continuous, with a ceiling so a roll cannot give a note that never ends.
13. **Global effects G_DTIME .. G_CDEPTH (delay, reverb, chorus settings).** Shared by both parts, so morphing one part would change the other. Options: excluded, or morph them only when both parts share the same morph. Recommendation: excluded.

| P_E0..P_E7 | engine labels | see B.2 | engine-specific |

### B.2 Engine-specific parameters (P_E0..P_E7)
Type: enum(n) = F_ENUM with n values; pct, bipct, time, cutoff, semi, int = the F_* format. Same tag words as above. The engine itself (G_ENGSEL) is never rolled.

**ANALOG** (`eng_analog.c`)
| E | Label | Type | Tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | WAVE | enum(7) SAW SQR TRI SIN PWM SYNC SUB | choice-fadable ? (Review 2; fade only among 0-4: same loop; SYNC and SUB run `analog_render_x`, never roll to or from them) |
| 1 | DTN (shown SYNC in WAVE SYNC) | int, ct 0-127 | continuous (fine detune, cap 10 ct; in WAVE SYNC it is the sync interval, free) |
| 2 | MIX (shown SUB in WAVE SUB) | pct | continuous |
| 3 | NOIS | pct | continuous |
| 4 | CUT | cutoff | continuous |
| 5 | RES | pct | continuous (resonance/drive coupling, rule 4) |
| 6 | DRV | pct | continuous |
| 7 | KTR | pct | continuous |

**FM6** (`eng_fm6.c`: macros on top of the track's patch)
| E | Label | Type | Tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | ALG | int 0-32 (PAT, 1-32) | choice-hard (cannot be faded) |
| 1 | FB | int -7..7 | continuous ? (Review 3; macro, first build only if cleared) |
| 2 | MLVL | bipct | continuous (macro, first build; live each block) |
| 3 | MRAT | int -16..16 | excluded (integer ratio steps are semitone-like coarse tuning) |
| 4 | MEG | bipct | continuous ? (Review 3; macro, envelope rates, heard from the next note) |
| 5 | VMOD | int -7..7 | excluded (first build morphs only the macros MLVL, DTUN, FB, MEG; taken at note-on) |
| 6 | DTUN | pct | continuous (macro, first build; capped at about 10 ct between the carriers) |
| 7 | SLOT | enum F1-F8, OWN, B1-B32 | excluded (loads a whole patch, `fm6_poll`) |

FM6's own patch (`fm6_core.c` FP_*, 155 bytes in `fm6_patch[]`, **not** in `t->p[]`: not automatable, not in the matrix, not in motion). The morph only reaches it through a new patch-blend path (`fm6_set_patch`, which makes SLOT = OWN and takes one undo). Until that exists: **leave all of it alone**; the first build morphs only the macros above. Playing safe (owner decision): per-operator output levels and the rest below are a **later** step. Tags for that step:
| FP_* (per operator x6, OP6 first) | Tag |
| --- | --- |
| R1-R4, L1-L4 (envelope rates, levels) | later: continuous ? (Review 4) |
| OL (output level) | later: continuous (keep carriers audible; needs `fm6_set_patch`) |
| FF (fine freq), DET (detune -7..+7) | later: ? (Review 4; recommended excluded) |
| FC (coarse), MODE (ratio/fixed) | excluded (coarse tuning; ratio/fixed cannot be faded) |
| BP, LD, RD, LC, RC (level scaling), RS (rate scaling), AMS, KVS | later: ? (Review 4; recommended excluded) |

| FP_* (voice) | Tag |
| --- | --- |
| PR1-4, PL1-4 (pitch envelope) | later: continuous, only if original != 0 (zero stays zero) |
| FB, LFS (speed), LFD (delay), LAMD (amp depth) | later: continuous ? (Review 4) |
| LPMD (pitch depth) | later: continuous, only if original != 0 (zero stays zero) |
| ALG | choice-hard (as E0) |
| OKS (osc key sync), LKS (LFO key sync), LFW (LFO wave, 6), LPMS (pitch mod sens) | choice-hard (never rolled) |
| TRNSP | excluded (transpose) |
| NAME | excluded |

### B.3 Global parameters (G_* in core.h, for completeness)
All **excluded**: they are shared by both parts or are system, tempo or file items. G_BPM, G_SWING, G_CLOCK, G_TUNE (tempo, clock, tuning); G_ROUTE (MIDI); G_MIDI, G_SYNC (since 0.2.5 the delay's modulation MRATE and MDEPTH, section 5c.2), G_INFO (since 0.2.14 the DELAY MODE, section 5c.2), G_DTIME, G_DFDBK, G_DCOLOR, G_DMIX, G_RSIZE, G_RDAMP, G_RLOCUT, G_RTYPE, G_CRATE, G_CDEPTH (global delay, reverb, chorus: a morph of one part would change the other; excluded, see Review 13); G_SLOT, G_NAME (since 0.2.19 SHIM's MOD, section 5h), G_LOAD, G_SAVE, G_CLRSEQ, G_INITSND (slots, names, actions); G_ENGSEL (engine selection; the old G_ENGGO's id 21 is G_RLOCUT since 0.2.14, section 5g, excluded: global); G_INFO, G_DRLVL, G_DRREV (readings and inert; G_DRREV's id 26 is SHIM's PITCH since 0.2.19).

### B.4 Not classified, or classified on weak evidence
- Every parameter has a tag. The doubtful ones are exactly the 13 items of the Review list at the top of this appendix.
- Mode-dependent parameters (LOFI E2/E3, PHYS E1/E4/E6, NOISE E5/E7, ANALOG E1/E2) need the tag looked up with the current mode; a roll must not change the mode that decides the tag.
- DUELL's new parameters do not exist in Felucca (FILTER ENV, LFO 2, matrix slots 5-8): tagged by analogy only.

## Appendix C: open decisions
1. (Settled: DRUM is removed. SLICE stayed until DUELL 0.2.9 retired it with SAMPLE and GRAIN: section 2.1.)
2. Section 3.5: the ROLL/UNDO/KEEP twist gesture (knob past a detent) is chosen; judge it in the emulator, four white keys are the fallback.
3. Appendix B Review list: 13 open questions awaiting the owner's answers.
4. Arp scale: check Felucca's scale list and its arp code (`seq.c`, `chord.c`) for reuse.
5. FM6 per-operator output levels and the rest of the FM6 patch: a later step, needs the `fm6_set_patch` path.
6. Section 3.7: a random-pattern ROLL (one percentage, the morph AMOUNT) needs a control on the PATTERNS page (all four knobs are used). Owner to choose; also whether the bass patterns should sit an octave lower in the step sequencer (they are C4-based so the SEQ arp reads them as offsets).
